D6-P025-0001 §1 [body] This standard defines the DDR6 specifications, including features, functionalities, AC and DC characteristics, packages, and ball/signal assignments.
D6-P025-0002 §1 [body] The purpose of this standard is to define the minimum set of requirements for JEDEC-compliant DDR6 devices.
D6-P025-0003 §1 [body] This document was created based on the LPDDR6 (JESD209-6) and DDR5 (JESD79-5) standards, and some aspects of the following standards: DDR (JESD79), DDR2 (JESD79-2), DDR3 (JESD79-3), DDR4 (JESD79-4), and LPDDR5 (JESD209-5).
D6-P025-0004 §1 [body] Each aspect of the standard was considered and approved by committee ballot(s).
D6-P025-0005 §1 [body] The accumulation of these ballots was then incorporated to prepare the DDR6 standard.
D6-P026-0001 §2.1 [body] DDR6 is a high-speed synchronous DRAM device internally configured with pseudo-split die (PSDs), each containing 3 or 6 double-data-rate (DDR) I/Os (DQs) and a shared differential data strobe (DQS) for both PSDs.
D6-P026-0002 §2.1 [body] The DQs from each PSD contribute to forming pseudo-channels.
D6-P026-0003 §2.1 [body] The bank architecture is two (2) banks in 16 bank groups per PSD.
D6-P026-0004 §2.1 [body] Further description of PSD organization and pseudo-channel configurations is provided in subsequent sections.
D6-P026-0005 §2.1 [body] The PSD can be accessed pseudo-independently in the 2p3 and 2p6 configurations or combined in the 1p6 and 1p12 configurations.
D6-P026-0006 §2.1 [body] The PSD configurations support operational modes such as 1p6, 1p12, 2p3, and 2p6, where "1p" and "2p" denote the number of pseudo-channels and the second number denotes the number of DQs per PSD.
D6-P026-0007 §2.1 [body] Configuration details are defined in the Addressing Tables section.
D6-P026-0008 §2.1 [body] The devices use a shared clock (CK) between both PSDs for command/address inputs that operates at the same rate as the shared DQS that is used for data.
D6-P026-0009 §2.1 [body] There are six single-data-rate (SDR) command/address (CA) pins that the memory controller uses to transmit command, address, bank, configuration, and training information to the device.
D6-P026-0010 §2.1 [body] CA signals are latched on the rising CK edges, with the commands starting on an even rising CK edge when indicated by a HIGH signal on the SDR CS pin that is 2N in duration and centered on the even rising CK edge.
D6-P026-0011 §2.1 [body] Commands are 2nCK, 4nCK and 6nCK in duration.
D6-P026-0012 §2.1 [body] See the Command Truth Table for details.
D6-P026-0013 §2.1 [body] The DDR I/O signaling is designed to operate with on-die termination on both the memory controller and the device.
D6-P026-0014 §2.1 [body] Data access size is configuration dependent, with 128 data bit prefetch supported.
D6-P026-0015 §2.1 [body] A single BL24 read or write access for a 3 DQ PSD consists of 72-bits (64-bits of data and 8-bits of non-data).
D6-P026-0016 §2.1 [body] A single BL24 read or write access for a 6 DQ PSD consists of 144-bits (128-bits of data and 16-bits of non-data).
D6-P026-0017 §2.1 [body] Non-data refers to bits that may be stored in the array and accessed using address carve-out, or bits that are not stored in the array, such as unstored CRC.
D6-P026-0018 §2.1 [body] Read and write accesses to the devices are burst oriented.
D6-P026-0019 §2.1 [body] Accesses begin with the registration of an ACTIVATE command, followed by a READ or WRITE command.
D6-P026-0020 §2.1 [body] The address, bank and bank group bits registered coincident with the ACTIVATE command are used to select the row and the bank to be accessed.
D6-P026-0021 §2.1 [body] The address, bank and bank group bits registered coincident with the READ or WRITE command are used to select the bank and the column location for the burst access.
D6-P026-0022 §2.1 [body] Prior to normal operation, the device must be initialized.
D6-P026-0023 §2.1 [body] The Power-Up and Initialization section provides detailed information covering device initialization.
D6-P027-0001 §2.1.1 [table_cell] RESET: Reset is active when RESET_n is LOW, and inactive when RESET_n is HIGH.
D6-P027-0002 §2.1.1 [table_cell] RESET_n must be HIGH during normal operation.
D6-P027-0003 §2.1.1 [table_cell] RESET_n is an asynchronous signal.
D6-P027-0004 §2.1.1 [table_cell] Clock: CK_t and CK_c are differential clock inputs.
D6-P027-0005 §2.1.1 [table_cell] The first crossing point is the rising (falling) edge of CK_t (CK_c) and second crossing point is falling (rising) edge of CK_t (CK_c).
D6-P027-0006 §2.1.1 [table_cell] Command/Address and CS inputs are sampled on the crossing point that is the rising (falling) edge of CK_t (CK_c).
D6-P027-0007 §2.1.1 [table_cell] One CK_t and CK_c pair are shared for both pseudo-split die (PSD0, PSD1).
D6-P027-0008 §2.1.1 [table_cell] Chip Select: CS is part of the command code, the pulse width is 2CK and is sampled on the even rising (falling) edge of CK_t (CK_c) unless the device is in power-down mode where it becomes an asynchronous signal.
D6-P027-0009 §2.1.1 [table_cell] All commands are masked when CS is registered LOW.
D6-P027-0010 §2.1.1 [table_cell] Command/Address Inputs: CA signals provide the command and address inputs according to the Command Truth Table.
D6-P027-0011 §2.1.1 [table_cell] Note: Since some commands are multi-cycle, the pins may not be interchanged between devices on the same bus.
D6-P027-0013 §2.1.1 [table_cell] Data Strobe: DQS_t and DQS_c are the differential output clock signals used to strobe data during a READ and differential input clock signals used to latch WRITE data.
D6-P027-0014 §2.1.1 [table_cell] The differential crossing point is edge-aligned with READ data and centered for WRITE data.
D6-P027-0015 §2.1.1 [table_cell] One DQS_t and DQS_c pair are shared for both pseudo-split die (PSD0, PSD1).
D6-P027-0016 §2.1.1 [table_cell] ALERT: If an error is detected and reported to the fault register, the ALERT encodes the type of error on the pin.
D6-P027-0017 §2.1.1 [table_cell] See the Alert section for details.
D6-P027-0018 §2.1.1 [table_cell] ZQ: ZQ is used to calibrate the output drive strength and the termination resistance as calibration reference.
D6-P027-0019 §2.1.1 [table_cell] There is one ZQ pad per die.
D6-P027-0020 §2.1.1 [table_cell] Power supplies: Isolated on the die for improved noise immunity.
D6-P027-0021 §2.1.1 [table_cell] Includes the main power, DQ power and row activation power.
D6-P027-0022 §2.1.1 [table_cell] Ground Reference: Power supply ground reference.
D6-P027-0023 §2.1.1 [table_cell] Reserved for future use .
D6-P028-0001 §2.1.1 [table_cell] No Connect: No internal electrical connection is present.
D6-P028-0002 §2.1.1 [table_cell] NOTE 1: This pin is referenced to VSS.
D6-P028-0003 §2.1.1 [table_cell] NOTE 2: In a multi-die package stack, one die will arbitrate for all dice.
D6-P028-0004 §2.1.1 [table_cell] NOTE 3: Pseudo-Split Die 0 (PSD0) and Pseudo-Split Die 1 (PSD1) have independent pads.
D6-P028-0005 §2.1.1 [table_cell] NOTE 4: CA5 (6th CA) is an optional pin on DDR6 that provides Chip ID (CID) address inputs.
D6-P030-0002 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] RESET: Reset is active when RESET_n is LOW, and inactive when RESET_n is HIGH.
D6-P030-0003 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] RESET_n must be HIGH during normal operation.
D6-P030-0004 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] RESET_n is an asynchronous signal.
D6-P030-0005 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] Clock: CK_t and CK_c are differential clock inputs.
D6-P030-0006 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] The first crossing point is the rising (falling) edge of CK_t (CK_c) and second crossing point is falling (rising) edge of CK_t (CK_c).
D6-P030-0007 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] Command/Address and CS inputs are sampled on the crossing point that is the rising (falling) edge of CK_t (CK_c).
D6-P030-0008 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] One CK_t and CK_c pair are shared for both pseudo-split die (PSD0, PSD1).
D6-P030-0009 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] Chip Select: CS is part of the command dcode, is two UI the pulse width is 2CK and is sampled on the even rising (falling) edge of CK_t (CK_c) unless the device is in power-down mode where it becomes an asynchronous signal.
D6-P030-0010 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] All commands are masked when CS is registered LOW.
D6-P030-0011 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] Chip Select number signifies the Rank with a min of 1 and a max of 4.
D6-P030-0012 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] Unused/unavailable ranks are NC.
D6-P031-0001 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] Command/Address Inputs: CA signals provide the command and address inputs according to the Command Truth Table.
D6-P031-0002 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] Note: Since some commands are multi-cycle, the pins may not be interchanged between devices on the same bus.
D6-P031-0003 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] CA5 (6th CA) is an optional pin which provides Chip ID (CID) address inputs.
D6-P031-0004 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] If unused, this is either CS3 or NC depending on package configuration.
D6-P031-0006 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] Data Strobe: DQS_t and DQS_c are the differential output clock signals used to strobe data during a READ and differential input clock signals used to latch WRITE data.
D6-P031-0007 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] The differential crossing point is edge-aligned with READ data and centered for WRITE data.
D6-P031-0008 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] One DQS_t and DQS_c pair are shared for both pseudo-split die (PSD0, PSD1).
D6-P031-0009 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] DQS1 is NC for 2p6 and 1p12 package configurations.
D6-P031-0010 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] ALERT: If an error is detected and reported to the fault register, the ALERT encodes the type of error on the pin.
D6-P031-0011 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] See the Alert section for details.
D6-P031-0012 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] Each PSD has an Alert.
D6-P031-0013 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] Alert A1 and B1 will be NC for 1p12 configurations.
D6-P031-0014 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] ZQ: ZQ is used to calibrate the output drive strength and the termination resistance as calibration reference.
D6-P031-0015 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] There is one ZQ pad per die.
D6-P031-0016 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] Power supplies: Isolated on the die for improved noise immunity.
D6-P031-0017 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] Includes the main power, DQ power and row activation power.
D6-P031-0018 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] Ground Reference: Power supply ground reference.
D6-P031-0019 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] Reserved for future use.
D6-P031-0020 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] No Connect: No internal electrical connection is present.
D6-P031-0021 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] Not Active: Internal package connection, not used/needed.
D6-P031-0022 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] Only applicable to block diagram die connection.
D6-P032-0001 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] NOTE 1: This pin is referenced to VSS.
D6-P032-0002 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] NOTE 2: In a multi-die package stack, one die will arbitrate for all dice.
D6-P032-0003 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] NOTE 3: Pseudo-Split Die 0 (PSD0) and Pseudo-Split Die 1 (PSD1) have independent pads.
D6-P032-0004 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] NOTE 4: Suffix A [1:0] and B [1:0] signify DRAM package IO Signal Group, X24 has two IO groups, X12 only uses Group A.
D6-P032-0005 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] Each group is split into two sub-groups [1:0].
D6-P032-0006 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] NOTE 5: System designers must connect both RESET_N_[A,B] to a common net on PCB hardware for both X12 and X24 packages.
D6-P032-0007 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] NOTE 6: A1 and B1 nets are NC for 1p12 configuration.
D6-P032-0008 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] See package block diagram for details.
D6-P032-0009 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] NOTE 7: xy suffix illustrates package BGA to die mapping.
D6-P032-0010 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] x=die/dice and y = PSD within the die/dice.
D6-P032-0011 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] Suffix is not shown in combination BGA map.
D6-P032-0012 §2.1.2.1 [table_cell] See block diagram(s) for further details.
D6-P032-0013 §2.1.2.2 [body] In order to keep package connectivity optimized for signal integrity, there may be cases where die to package connectivity may need to change.
D6-P032-0014 §2.1.2.2 [body] The following are acceptable signal swaps:
D6-P032-0015 §2.1.2.2 [body] 1) DQ[5:3] within the same PSD.
D6-P032-0016 §2.1.2.2 [body] 2) DQ[2:0] within the same PSD.
D6-P033-0001 §2.1.2.2 [body] 3) Full PSD[1:0] which includes all associated signals (i.e. DQ, CA, CS, Alert).
D6-P038-0001 §2.1.3.1 [body] This section defines the features and functional operations of Base Die Buffering (BDB) DRAM package.
D6-P038-0002 §2.1.3.1 [body] DDR6 can support BDB DRAM which is desinged for high-density multi-die stacked package, facilitating reduced load on CK, CS, CA, and DQ/DQS signals for improved singal integrity as well as lower power relative to unbuffered DRAM package.
D6-P038-0003 §2.1.3.1 [body] In multi-rank package, unbuffered DRAM has multiple indipendent DRAM dies wire bonded together, increasing electrical loading on the system channels.
D6-P038-0004 §2.1.3.1 [body] The BDB DRAM technology mitigates this with the “Base DRAM Die” (hereinafter “Base Die”) being a single electrical load, exclusively interfacing the memory controller for the stacked DRAM package no matter if the stack height is 2, 4, or 8.
D6-P038-0005 §2.1.3.1 [body] One or more vertically stacked “Core DRAM Dies” (hereinafter “Core Dies”) are directly connected to the Base Die through die-to-die interconnects within the package.
D6-P038-0006 §2.1.3.1 [body] This topology isolates external IO between the host and the base die and internal IO between the base die and core dies, improving memory subsystem SI and saving power from Core Dies by turning off redundant IO circuit blocks mainly built for external communications.
D6-P038-0007 §2.1.3.1 [body] MRxx[y]=1 indicates that the DRAM is BDB.
D6-P038-0009 §2.1.3.1 [body] Package implementation details are not in scope of this standard.
D6-P038-0011 §2.1.3.2 [body] The BDB DRAM supports an extra CA pin, CA5, for CID address inputs which are used for determining the targeted logical rank that is desired to perform the given commands.
D6-P038-0012 §2.1.3.2 [body] Commands that have CID address bits are ACT, RD, WR, PRE, REF and RFM.
D6-P038-0013 §2.1.3.2 [body] For other commands that do not have CID address bits, the base die is the targeted logical rank unless otherwise specified.
D6-P038-0014 §2.1.3.2 [body] Targeted logical rank is selected by CID[2:0] and DDR6 can support up to 8 logical ranks.
D6-P038-0015 §2.1.3.2 [body] The base die is associated with logical rank 0.
D6-P038-0016 §2.1.3.2 [body] The number of available logical ranks in package is provided by MRxx[y:z].
D6-P039-0001 §2.1.3.2 [body] The functional behavior of logical rank(s) shall not deviate from that of physical rank(s) of unbuffered DRAM packages, except when noted in this standard.
D6-P039-0003 §2.1.3.2 [body] The Base Die serves as the logical host interface for the entire stack.
D6-P039-0004 §2.1.3.2 [body] CA inputs are buffered and decoded centrally on the base die, which then redriven to the target core die(s) corresponding to the decoded CID.
D6-P039-0006 §2.1.3.2 [body] Because of buffering operation in the base die and in package die-to-die interconnects between the base die and core dies, additional asynchronous delays are needed.
D6-P039-0007 §2.1.3.2 [body] TBDns is added to tAA.
D6-P039-0008 §2.1.3.2 [body] Different Logical Rank (DLR) timing parameters need to be defined in future ballots.
D6-P040-0001 §2.1.4 [body] The core architecture will be a Pseudo Split Die (PSD) implementation with separate left and right DRAM arrays (each array is half of the total DRAM density) with a common IO block containing a shared CK and DQS.
D6-P040-0002 §2.1.4 [body] For 2p3 and 2p6 configuration, each half array will have an independent Chip Select (CS) and Command/Address (CA) bus.
D6-P040-0003 §2.1.4 [body] Both half arrays will share a CK and DQS clocking port.
D6-P040-0004 §2.1.4 [body] The two PSDs cannot transfer data in opposite directions simultaneously as they share strobes; however, one PSD may begin to transfer data before the other.
D6-P040-0005 §2.1.4 [body] It is the Host controller’s responsibility to provide proper turnaround times based on the last PSD transferring in one direction to the first PSD transferring in the other.
D6-P040-0006 §2.1.4 [body] One PSD may transfer data without the other transferring any data at all.
D6-P040-0007 §2.1.4 [body] For the 1p6 configuration, each die will have a single CS, CA and CK/DQS clocking port per device.
D6-P040-0008 §2.1.4 [body] For the 1p12 configuration, each die will have a single CS, CA and CK/DQS clocking port per device.
D6-P040-0009 §2.1.4 [body] BL24 is the burst length for all configurations.
D6-P040-0010 §2.1.4 [body] Example die architectures for each configuration is shown in the sections below.
D6-P040-0011 §2.1.4 [body] NOTE: Within the following die diagrams, the placement of the DRAM array BGs/BA and pads are for illustration only.
D6-P040-0012 §2.1.4 [body] The meaning of the abbreviations are as follows:
D6-P040-0014 §2.1.4 [body] Portion of a DRAM die with independent chip select(CS), command/address (CA) bus and DQ bus, but a CK port (CK_t/c, DQS_t/c) which is common to both of PSDs
D6-P045-0021 §2.1.4 [body] NOTE: In Figure 1p12, CA[5] is shown grayed out because CA5 is not functionally required for 1p12 devices.
D6-P045-0022 §2.1.4 [body] The connection is included solely for consistency with combo devices.
D6-P046-0001 §2.1.5 [table_cell] NOTE: For non-binary memory densities, only one quarter of the row address space is invalid.
D6-P046-0002 §2.1.5 [table_cell] When the MSB address bit is HIGH, then the MSB-1 address bit must be LOW.
D6-P047-0001 §2.1.5 [table_cell] NOTE: For non-binary memory densities, only one quarter of the row address space is invalid.
D6-P047-0002 §2.1.5 [table_cell] When the MSB address bit is HIGH, then the MSB-1 address bit must be LOW.
D6-P047-0003 §2.1.5 [table_cell] NOTE 1: For the 1p6 Configuration, the CA "PSD" bit becomes a Bank Group and the full device is treated as a single channel.
D6-P047-0004 §2.1.5 [table_cell] NOTE 2: For non-binary memory densities, only one quarter of the row address space is invalid.
D6-P047-0005 §2.1.5 [table_cell] When the MSB address bit is HIGH, then the MSB-1 address bit must be LOW.
D6-P047-0006 §2.1.5 [table_cell] NOTE 3: BGs with different PSD addresses may have separate command timing constraints between PSDs.
D6-P047-0007 §2.1.5 [table_cell] See the Command Timing Constraints section for more details.
D6-P048-0001 §2.2.1 [body] DDR6 SDRAM devices support multiple speed grades defined by their maximum data rate in MT/s (Mega Transfers per second).
D6-P048-0002 §2.2.1 [body] Speed grades represent the maximum data rate capability of the device.
D6-P048-0003 §2.2.1 [body] Higher speed grade devices can be operated at lower data rates with appropriate timing adjustments.
D6-P048-0004 §2.2.1 [table_cell] NOTE 1: 6400, 8000, 9200, and 9600 MT/s are supported as performance down-bins
D6-P048-0005 §2.2.1 [table_cell] NOTE 2: tCK(max) shall be less than or equal to 416.7ps
D6-P048-0006 §2.2.1 [table_cell] NOTE 3: Higher speed grade devices may be operated at any lower supported data rate with timing parameters adjusted according to Mode Register settings for the target frequency
D6-P049-0001 §2.3 [body] The data packet format is based on burst length 24.
D6-P049-0002 §2.3 [body] The entire data packet is divided into 8-bit sub-packets.
D6-P049-0003 §2.3 [body] In the following figures, numbered bit locations are data bits.
D6-P049-0004 §2.3 [body] Shaded bit locations depend on device configuration and Mode Register settings.
D6-P049-0005 §2.3 [body] In the figures, the green and blue bit locations may be Metadata bits or CRC bits, which is selected by Mode Registers upon initialization.
D6-P049-0006 §2.3 [body] (Reference to Metadata section and CRC section.)
D6-P049-0007 §2.3.1 [body] DDR6 supports a Metadata feature for Write and Read operations.
D6-P049-0008 §2.3.1 [body] In this mode, the data and Metadata bits are written/read in parallel to/from the array.
D6-P049-0009 §2.3.1 [body] The Metadata feature supports the DRAM storing 0, 8 or 16 bits of Metadata as MD_OFF, MD_8b and MD_16b, respectively, where one of these modes is selected by MRxx:OP[y] at initialization.
D6-P049-0010 §2.3.1 [body] For 2p6 and 1p6 DRAM configurations, Metadata sub-packets are 8 bits.
D6-P049-0011 §2.3.1 [body] When MD_OFF is selected, both sub-packets are non-Metadata (Non MD).
D6-P049-0012 §2.3.1 [body] Non MD bits are not stored in the DRAM array, but are available for other functions like CRC.
D6-P049-0013 §2.3.1 [body] MD_8b mode enables blue bit locations for Metadata and green bit locations for Non MD, as shown in the tables below.
D6-P049-0014 §2.3.1 [body] MD_16b mode enables both blue and green bit locations for Metadata, also as shown in the tables below.
D6-P050-0001 §2.3.1 [body] For the 2p3 DRAM configuration, the sub-packets are half that of 2p6 and 1p6.
D6-P050-0002 §2.3.1 [body] The tables below show the Metadata sub-packets as 4 bits each, with their respective assignments for MD_OFF, MD_8b and MD_16b.
D6-P052-0001 §3 [body] DDR6 SDRAM's command and address interface operates from a differential Clock pair (CK_t/CK_c).
D6-P052-0002 §3 [body] Commands and Addresses are registered single data rate (SDR) at every rising edge of CK_t and falling edge of CK_c.
D6-P052-0003 §3 [body] CS is sampled at every other rising edge of CK_t and the falling edge of CK_c.
D6-P052-0004 §3 [body] DDR6 uses a double data rate (DDR) data interface.
D6-P052-0005 §3 [body] The data interface uses two different types of clock pairs for Read (CK_t/CK_c) and Write (DQS_t/DQS_c).
D6-P052-0006 §3 [body] CK_t and CK_c are used to drive DQ Read, and they are source synchronous to the DQS during Read operation.
D6-P052-0008 §3 [body] The DQ Write data is registered by DQS at every rising edge of DQS_t and rising edge of DQS_c for write operation.
D6-P052-0009 §3 [body] DDR6 SDRAM uses an un-matched DQS-DQ path for Write to enable high speed performance and save power in the SDRAM.
D6-P052-0010 §3 [body] Therefore, a per-pin training by a host is required to adjust a timing of each DQ signal relative to DQS.
D6-P052-0011 §3 [body] This will allow a data eye to arrive at a SDRAM DQ receiver latch being centered on the DQS transition.
D6-P052-0012 §3 [body] CA is matched with CK, and CAs skew training shall be used to compensate the increased CA and CK path delay.
D6-P054-0001 §4.1 [body] For power-up and reset initialization, in order to prevent DRAM from functioning improperly, default values for the following MR settings need to be defined.
D6-P054-0002 §4.1 [table_cell] FSP-OP/WR[0] is enabled
D6-P054-0003 §4.1 [table_cell] CA-ODT is based on CAOTP value1
D6-P054-0004 §4.1 [table_cell] CS-ODT is based on CSOTP value1
D6-P054-0005 §4.1 [table_cell] CK-ODT is based on CKOTP value1
D6-P054-0007 §4.1 [table_cell] CK-ODT is based on CKOTP value1
D6-P054-0016 §4.1 [table_cell] NOTE 1: CK/CS/CA OTP(One-Time Programmable) registers are set to 0 or 1, by vendor implementation at RESET sequence.
D6-P054-0017 §4.1.1 [body] DDR6 SDRAM supports CKOTP, CSOTP and CAOTP for default operation.
D6-P054-0018 §4.1.1 [body] Since there are various types of packages and modules, DDR6 internally have OTP registers for CA, CS and CK for initial state.
D6-P054-0019 §4.1.1 [body] Default RTT value of CA/CS signals, as seen from the package balls, is set to 240Ω by OTP and MR definitions, regardless of the number
D6-P055-0001 §4.1.1 [body] of dies in the package, by enabling CA/CS ODT in a single die.
D6-P055-0002 §4.1.1 [body] When all dies enable CK ODT in the initial state, the effective RTT becomes the default RTT divided by the number of dies.
D6-P055-0003 §4.1.1 [body] Since appropriate VREF level and ODT values are affecting each other, VREF and ODT values need to be changed simultaneously with single command to avoid abnormal operation.
D6-P055-0004 §4.1.1 [body] DDR6 SDRAM supports Apply function for VREF, and ODT MR for CK, CS, and CA.
D6-P055-0005 §4.1.1 [body] When MRUPDATE is issued to change VREF or ODT MR, it's stored in shadow-registers.
D6-P055-0006 §4.1.1 [body] When Apply MRUPDATE is issued, values stored in corresponding shadow-registers are transferred to corresponding normal-registers, and impact on function and MRR operation.
D6-P055-0007 §4.1.1 [body] Every shadow register's RESET default value is same as normal-registers RESET default value.
D6-P055-0008 §4.1.1 [body] Changing CK/CS/CAODT or Apply operations toward these registers are only allowed in MRUPD mode.
D6-P055-0009 §4.1.1 [body] Other than MRUPD mode, using MRW command, changing VREFCS/CA is allowed, but it directly impact on normal-registers without changing shadow-registers.
D6-P055-0010 §4.1.1 [body] In 2PSD configuration, VREFCA, VREFCS, CA ODT, CS ODT normal/shadow registers are PSD Mode Registers, while CK ODT normal/shadow registers are Shared Mode Registers.
D6-P055-0011 §4.1.1 [body] Apply operation is Functional Mode Registers which is in Shared Registers.
D6-P055-0012 §4.1.1 [body] So, in 2p3 or 2p6 configurations, VREFCA, VREFCS, CA ODT and CS ODT registers shall be controlled by each of PSD, while CK ODT register can be controlled by either of PSD.
D6-P055-0013 §4.1.1 [body] Apply operation is shared by MRUPDATE command of either of PSD.
D6-P055-0014 §4.1.1 [body] Shadow-registers are activated bypassing normal-registers in train state.
D6-P055-0015 §4.1.1 [body] When train-state in CSTM is entered, CS VREF and CS ODT MR's shadow-registers are activated without applying operation.
D6-P055-0016 §4.1.1 [body] When train-state in CATM is entered, CS VREF, CA VREF, CS ODT, CA ODT MR's shadow-registers are activated without applying operation.
D6-P055-0017 §4.1.1 [body] Bypassing operation is disabled when exit from train-state.
D6-P055-0018 §4.1.1 [body] Any changes on VREFCS and VREFCA in CSTM and CATM directly stored in shadow-registers and will be applied to normal-registers when Apply MRUPDATE is issued.
D6-P055-0019 §4.1.1 [body] DDR6 SDRAM also supports CA-parity recovery mode, defined on MR[y] OP[a].
D6-P055-0020 §4.1.1 [body] When this mode and CA parity check modes enabled, VREFCA shadow-register value is automatically applied to VREFCA normal-register when CA parity error occurs.
D6-P055-0021 §4.1.1 [body] In physical multi-rank configuration, MRUPDATE command for Apply-VREF/ODT command needs to be issued simultaneously to all ranks.
D6-P055-0022 §4.1.1 [body] To accomplish successful transition of VREF and ODT, Apply operation is recommended to be issued in MRUPD mode, because broadcasting CS is only allowed in MRUPD mode.
D6-P055-0023 §4.1.2 [body] The following sequence shall be used to power up the DDR6 SDRAM.
D6-P055-0024 §4.1.2 [body] Unless specified otherwise, these steps are mandatory.
D6-P055-0025 §4.1.2 [body] 1) While applying power (after Ta), RESET_n is recommended to be LOW (equal or less than 0.2 x VDD) and all other inputs shall be between VSS and VDD.
D6-P055-0026 §4.1.2 [body] The SDRAM outputs remain at High-Z while RESET_n is held
D6-P056-0002 §4.1.2 [body] Power supply voltage ramp requirements are provided in Table 2.
D6-P056-0003 §4.1.2 [body] VPP must ramp at the same time or earlier than VDD.
D6-P056-0004 §4.1.2 [body] VDD must ramp at the same time or earlier than VDDQ.
D6-P056-0005 §4.1.2 [table_cell] VPP must be greater than VDD
D6-P056-0006 §4.1.2 [table_cell] Ta is reached
D6-P056-0007 §4.1.2 [table_cell] VDD must be greater than VDDQ
D6-P056-0008 §4.1.2 [table_cell] NOTE 1: Ta is the point when any power supply first reaches 300mV.
D6-P056-0009 §4.1.2 [table_cell] NOTE 2: Voltage ramp conditions in Table above apply between (Ta) and controlled Power-off.
D6-P056-0010 §4.1.2 [table_cell] NOTE 3: Tb is the point at which all supply voltages are within their defined ranges.
D6-P056-0011 §4.1.2 [table_cell] NOTE 4: Power ramp duration tINIT0(Tb-Ta) must not exceed 20ms.
D6-P056-0012 §4.1.2 [body] 2) Following the completion of the voltage ramp (Tb), RESET_n shall be maintained LOW for a minimum of tINIT1, after which RESET_n can be de-asserted to HIGH(Tc).
D6-P056-0013 §4.1.2 [body] DQ, DQS_t, DQS_c, voltage levels shall be between VSS and VDDQ to avoid latch-up.
D6-P056-0014 §4.1.2 [body] 3) At least tINIT2 before RESET_n de-assertion, CS level is required to be VSS to prevent malfunction.
D6-P056-0015 §4.1.2 [body] 4) After RESET_n is de-asserted (Tc), CK/CS/CA/DQ and DQS ODT state should be valid following the default states.
D6-P056-0034 §4.1.2 [body] NOTE: 1: Training is optional and may be done at the system architect's direction.
D6-P056-0035 §4.1.2 [body] The training sequence after ZQ Latch in this figure (Th) is simplified recommendation and actual training sequence may vary depending on systems.
D6-P056-0036 §4.1.2 [body] NOTE: 2: Initial ZQ Calibration is started automatically by DRAM when RESET_n goes high after tINIT1 and is completed before Td
D6-P056-0037 §4.1.2 [body] NOTE: 3: DDR6 SDRAM initialize on IDLE state after CS Pulse.
D6-P056-0038 §4.1.2 [body] IDLE state shall be maintained for tINIT5 before entering into MRUPD mode
D6-P056-0039 §4.1.2 [body] NOTE: 4: In 2PSD configuration, Initialization pulse and MCME shall be issued for both PSDs.
D6-P056-0040 §4.1.2 [body] 5) CK_t and CK_c are required to be toggled or valid to be complementary level at least tINIT4 before CS receives one toggling (Td).
D6-P057-0001 §4.1.2 [body] 7) Between Tg to Th, the following initial configuration modes shall be completed prior to other training modes: a) MRUPDATE for setting Frequency MR (MR9) shall be issued.
D6-P057-0002 §4.1.2 [body] Input clock shall be sustained for tDLYCAL (Tg) b) MRUPDATE to execute ZQ Latch shall be issued before entering CSTM.
D6-P057-0004 §4.1.2 [body] 8) Host can also change CK/CA/CS ODT and CA/CS VREF values to change SI conditions in MRUPD mode.
D6-P057-0005 §4.1.2 [body] It is recommended to configure VREF and ODT registers to appropriate level and issue Apply MRUPDATE.
D6-P057-0006 §4.1.2 [body] 9) After CS/CA training is done, host shall change Mode Registers for RL(WL), nWTP(nRTP) to appropriate values with input clock frequency, before host start the Read and Write related training sequences.
D6-P057-0007 §4.1.2 [body] 10) Only in MRUPD mode can the host change the clock frequency without Full-PD with Frequency change.
D6-P057-0008 §4.1.2 [body] Frequency change sequence shall be done accordance with FSP and switching specification.
D6-P057-0009 §4.1.2 [body] a) Host sets FSP-WR to 1.
D6-P057-0010 §4.1.2 [body] b) Host sets Frequency MR that matches the 2nd clock frequency.
D6-P057-0011 §4.1.2 [body] c) If needed, host sets CK/CA/CS ODT and CA/CS VREF values for 2nd clock frequency on shadow registers, and issues CK/CA/CS Apply MRUPDATE to set 2nd FSP registers.
D6-P057-0012 §4.1.2 [body] d) Host issues MRUPDATE to change FSP-OP to 1 and turn off the clock after tCKFC_E.
D6-P057-0013 §4.1.2 [body] From the rising edge of CK_t (at cycle Tb3) until the rising edge of CK_t at the end of tCKFC_X timing (Tg0), only DES command is allowed and CS should keep low.
D6-P057-0014 §4.1.2 [body] e) If FSP_OP transition includes changes on VREFCA or VREFCS value, following command (Tg0) after the clock frequency change need to be delayed satisfying VREF transition specification.
D6-P057-0015 §4.1.2 [body] f) The period after turning off and before turning on the clock, CK_t and CK_c shall be driven differentially.
D6-P057-0016 §4.1.2 [body] g) The clock with new frequency shall start tCKFC_X before the next MRUPDATE.
D6-P057-0017 §4.1.2 [body] h) The host shall synchronize with DRAM with their internal clock phase by either CPS of CSTM or CK Sync Off functional mode followed by CK sync.
D6-P057-0028 §4.1.2 [body] NOTE: 1: In multi-rank configuration, the MRUPDATE command to change FSP-OP shall be issued to all-Ranks simultaneously.
D6-P057-0029 §4.1.2 [body] NOTE: 2: In 2p3 or 2p6 configurations, the MRUPDATE command to change FSP-OP shall be issued to either of PSDs.
D6-P058-0001 §4.1.2 [table_cell] Valid Clock Requirement after the first Frequency MR set
D6-P058-0002 §4.1.3 [body] The following sequence is required for RESET at no power interruption initialization.
D6-P058-0003 §4.1.3 [body] 1) Assert RESET_n below 0.2 x VDD anytime when reset is needed.
D6-P058-0004 §4.1.3 [body] RESET_n needs to be maintained for minimum tPW_RESET.
D6-P058-0005 §4.1.3 [body] CS must be pulled LOW at least tINIT2 before deasserting RESET_n.
D6-P059-0001 §4.1.4 [body] The Frequency Set Points (FSP) allow the DDR6 SDRAM CK/CS/CA Bus to be switched between 2 different operating frequencies, with changes in voltage swings and termination values, without ever being in an untrained state which could result in a loss of communication to the SDRAM.
D6-P059-0002 §4.1.4 [body] This is accomplished by duplicating all CA Bus mode register parameters, as well as other mode register parameters commonly changed with operating frequency.
D6-P059-0003 §4.1.4 [body] These doubled registers form 2 sets that use the same mode register addresses, with read/write access controlled by MR bit FSP-WR (Frequency Set-Point Write/Read) and the DRAM operating point controlled by another MR bit FSP-OP (Frequency Set-Point Operation).
D6-P059-0004 §4.1.4 [body] Changing the FSP-WR bit allows MR parameters to be changed for a selected Frequency Set-Point without affecting the DDR6- SDRAM’'s current operation.
D6-P059-0005 §4.1.4 [body] Once all necessary parameters have been written to the selected Set-Point, changing the FSP-OP bit will switch operation to use all of the new parameters simultaneously with Full PDE with frequency change, eliminating the possibility of a loss of communication that could be caused by a partial configuration change.
D6-P059-0006 §4.1.4 [body] 2 mode registers for each of the parameters above can be modified by setting the appropriate FSP-WR value, and how device operation can be switched between operating points by setting the appropriate FSP-OP value.
D6-P059-0007 §4.1.4 [body] The FSP-WR and FSP-OP functions operate completely independently.
D6-P059-0008 §4.1.4 [body] Unlike normal-registers with FSP, shadow-registers are shared within FSPs.
D6-P059-0009 §4.1.4 [body] When MRUPDATE is issued to change VREF or ODT, it is buffered on shadow-register.
D6-P059-0010 §4.1.4 [body] When Apply MRUPDATE is issued, FSP-WR value specifies to which FSP the shadow value is applied.
D6-P059-0011 §4.1.4 [table_cell] 0B: Data write to Mode Register N for FSP-OP[0] by MRW or MRUPDATE command.
D6-P059-0012 §4.1.4 [table_cell] Data read from Mode Register N for FSP-OP[0] by MRR command.
D6-P059-0013 §4.1.4 [table_cell] 1B: Data write to Mode Register N for FSP-OP[1] by MRW or MRUPDATE command.
D6-P059-0014 §4.1.4 [table_cell] Data read from Mode Register N for FSP-OP[1] by MRR command.
D6-P059-0015 §4.1.4 [table_cell] 0B:SDRAM operates with Mode Register N for FSP-OP[0] setting.
D6-P059-0016 §4.1.4 [table_cell] 1B: SDRAM operates with Mode Register N for FSP-OP[1] setting.
D6-P059-0017 §4.1.4 [table_cell] NOTE 1: FSP-WR stands for Frequency Set Point Writ/Read.
D6-P059-0018 §4.1.4 [table_cell] NOTE 2: FSP-OP stands for Frequency Set Point Operating Point.
D6-P060-0001 §4.2.1 [body] The Multi-Cycle CS Mode Enable (MCME) command is a single-function command to enable MRUPDATE Mode with multi-cycle CS (MCCS).
D6-P060-0002 §4.2.1 [body] Prior to successfully completing the CS, VREFCS, CA and VREFCA trainings, or any time the status of the CS, CA and CK alignment is unknown, the CAs are driven with additional setup and hold beyond the CS assertion.
D6-P060-0003 §4.2.1 [body] For the device to latch the MCME in cases where the alignment between CS, CA and CK may be unknown, the CA inputs must reach the proper command state and provide tMC_Setup of setup prior to CS transitioning from LOW to HIGH, CS must remain HIGH for tMC_CSPW, and the CA inputs must remain in the proper command state and provide tMC_Hold of hold after CS transitions from HIGH to LOW.
D6-P060-0004 §4.2.1 [body] While the DRAM may only decode CA[2:0] of the MCME command, all CA inputs are high (H) to ensure proper parity.
D6-P060-0005 §4.2.1 [body] Following the issuing of the MCME command, tMCME_Delay needs to be met before the first cycle of an MRUP- DATE begins.
D6-P060-0006 §4.2.1 [body] No commands are allowed during tMCME_Delay.
D6-P060-0007 §4.2.1 [body] The MCME command timing diagram and timing parameters are shown in the figure and table below.
D6-P062-0001 §4.2.2 [body] Per DRAM Addressability (PDA) enables the programming of a selected device on a rank.
D6-P062-0002 §4.2.2 [body] The PDA applies to MRW commands and MRUPDATE Mode, except when entering PDA Enumeration Mode or disabling Mode Register Functional Modes (MR05), and when writing to the PDA mode registers.
D6-P062-0003 §4.2.2 [body] The Direct Addressable PDA Mode Register (MR02) contains the Enumeration ID which is common to both PSDs, while the Page Space PDA Mode Register MR82 contains the Select ID, which can be unique per PSD.
D6-P062-0004 §4.2.2 [body] PDA is a CA interface-only method where a unique Enumeration ID can be programmed in MR02[3:0] and a per PSD unique Select ID is programmed in MR82[3:0].
D6-P062-0005 §4.2.2 [body] Programming the unique Enumeration ID requires the use of the DQ signals and the PDA Enumeration Mode.
D6-P062-0006 §4.2.2 [body] The Enumeration ID is a 4-bit field, and the Select ID is also a 4-bit field.
D6-P062-0007 §4.2.2 [body] When the Select ID, written by an MRW command or MRUPDATE sequence, matches the Enumeration ID, or when the Select ID is set to the “All DRAM” code of 1111b, an MRW command or the MUPDATE sequence will be executed.
D6-P062-0008 §4.2.2 [body] During the RESET procedure, the Enumeration ID and Select ID values are reset to the default 1111b.
D6-P062-0009 §4.2.2 [table_cell] MR02[3:0] Enumeration ID Read Only, programmed through PDA Enumeration Mode.
D6-P062-0010 §4.2.2 [table_cell] xxxxb encoding is the Enumeration ID.
D6-P062-0011 §4.2.2 [table_cell] MR82[3:0] Select ID The Select ID is set by MRW or MRUPD sequence.
D6-P062-0012 §4.2.2 [table_cell] Default setting is all DRAMs execute MRW/MRUPD.
D6-P062-0013 §4.2.2 [table_cell] For all other encodings, the DRAM will execute MRW/MRUPD when Select ID = Enumeration ID, except to this Mode Register which ignores the PDA match and will always be executed.
D6-P062-0014 §4.2.2.1 [body] 1) PDA Enumeration Mode is a Functional Mode within MR05 that can only be entered by the MRUPDATE sequence with MR05=06h after entering MRUPDATE Mode.
D6-P062-0015 §4.2.2.1 [body] PDA Enumeration Mode requires DQS to be low when entering and exiting the mode.
D6-P062-0016 §4.2.2.1 [body] 2) Upon entering the PDA Enumeration Mode, the DRAM will internally reset the DQS rising pulse count to 0 to prepare for counting 4 rising pulse edges (DQSR1 - DQSR4)
D6-P062-0017 §4.2.2.1 [body] 3) As shown in the timing diagram below, the second DQS rising edge (DQSR2) and the fourth DQS rising edge (DQSR4) are used to sample the data on DQ0 and DQ1
D6-P063-0001 §4.2.2.1 [body] 4) The sampled data is then combined to form the Enumeration ID of the device and is stored in the PDA Mode Register, MR02[3:0]
D6-P063-0002 §4.2.2.1 [body] 5) PDA Enumeration Mode is exited by latching "All Functions Disabled" in MR05 (MR05=00h).
D6-P063-0027 §4.2.2.2 [body] Once the PDA Enumeration IDs have been programmed in all devices, the execution of future MRW commands and MRUPDATE sequences depend on the value of the Select ID.
D6-P063-0028 §4.2.2.2 [body] If the Select ID is set to 1111b, all DRAM will execute the command/sequence.
D6-P064-0001 §4.2.2.2 [body] Mode, disabling functional modes, and when writing to the PDA Select Mode Register (MR82), the Select ID will be compared to the Enumeration ID to determine if the command/sequence will be executed.
D6-P064-0002 §4.2.2.2 [body] Entering PDA Enumeration Mode, disabling functional modes, and writing to the PDA Select Mode Register will be executed regardless of the Select ID value.
D6-P064-0003 §4.2.2.2 [body] As an example, the following sequence could be used to program unique PDA Select ID fields per device:
D6-P064-0004 §4.2.2.2 [body] 1) Send an MRW command with MR82[3:0] and the 'Select ID' opcodes with encoding 0000b
D6-P064-0005 §4.2.2.2 [body] 2) Send MRW commands with settings specific to device 0000b.
D6-P064-0006 §4.2.2.2 [body] This can be any number of MRWs.
D6-P064-0007 §4.2.2.2 [body] 3) Send an MRW command with MR82[3:0] and the 'Select ID' opcodes with encoding 0001b
D6-P064-0008 §4.2.2.2 [body] 4) Send MRW commands with settings specific to device 0001b.
D6-P064-0009 §4.2.2.2 [body] This can be any number of MRWs.
D6-P064-0011 §4.2.2.2 [body] 6) Send an MRW command with MR82[3:0] and the 'Select ID' opcodes with encoding 1111b to enable execution of all MRW commands and MRUDPATE sequences on all devices
D6-P065-0001 §4.2.3 [body] Only Background ZQ Calibration mode is supported in DDR6.
D6-P065-0002 §4.2.3 [body] In Background Calibration mode, calibration of the output driver and CA/DQ ODT impedance across process, temperature, and voltage occurs in the background of device operation and is designed to eliminate any need for coordination among channels within a single package.
D6-P065-0003 §4.2.3 [body] ZQ re-calibration may be required as the DDR6 SDRAM voltage and/or temperature changes due to changes in the system environment.
D6-P065-0004 §4.2.3 [body] ZQ calibration can only be performed when the VDDQ voltage is at or above its nominal value of 0.6V within the range allowed by the specification, otherwise the memory controller calibration shall halt the ZQ calibration.
D6-P065-0005 §4.2.3 [body] Changing CK, CS andCA ODT values and/or DQ, DQS ODT values will not alter the existing recalibration scheme, therefore there is no need for immediate recalibration.
D6-P065-0006 §4.2.3.1 [body] ZQ calibration is automatically performed by all DDR6 dies in a package during the initialization/powerup sequence before Td, as shown in Figure xx (Power-up, Initialization and Power-off Procedure).
D6-P065-0007 §4.2.3.1 [body] A ZQCal Latch commandoperation shall be initiated issued to all DDR6 dies that share a common clock, on or after Tg regardless of the state of the ZQ Update Flag (ZQUF).
D6-P065-0008 §4.2.3.1 [body] See Table 1 for calibration latency and timing.
D6-P065-0009 §4.2.3.1 [body] Asserting ZQ Reset will set the calibration values to their default setting.
D6-P065-0010 §4.2.3.1 [body] When ZQ Stop is enabled, the ZQ resource is available for use by other devices.
D6-P065-0011 §4.2.3.1 [body] DDR6 SDRAM package will have one or more ZQ initiator die(s) and ZQ target die(s).
D6-P065-0012 §4.2.3.1 [body] ZQ Interval MR setting is only applicable to ZQ Initiator die (MR7 OP[1]).
D6-P065-0013 §4.2.3.1 [body] These settings will be ignored by ZQ Target dies.
D6-P065-0014 §4.2.3.1 [body] After the power up initialization and reset sequences have been completed, ZQUF MR7 OP[0] =0B.
D6-P065-0015 §4.2.3.1 [body] DDR6 packages with more than one ZQ pin may include more than one ZQ Initiator die.
D6-P065-0016 §4.2.3.2 [body] In this mode, pull-down/ODT and pull-up calibration will be performed in the background and kept up to date by the DRAM.
D6-P065-0017 §4.2.3.2 [body] Re-calibration will be performed by the DDR6 SDRAM within the time interval, tZQINT, specified in MR7 OP[5:4].
D6-P065-0018 §4.2.3.2 [body] Pull-down/ODT calibration is controlled by each DDR6 die using an external ZQ resistor connected between VDDQ and a package ball or pin (ZQ resources).
D6-P065-0019 §4.2.3.2 [body] These ZQ resources may be shared among a number of DDR6 dies up to a maximum of NZQ.
D6-P065-0020 §4.2.3.2 [body] Calibration will be automatically performed as part of power-up/initialization and after RESET assertion.
D6-P065-0021 §4.2.3.2 [body] Subsequent re-calibration will be kept up to date by the DRAM.
D6-P065-0022 §4.2.3.2 [body] Self-arbitration by the DRAM insures that up to NZQ dies within a package can share a common external ZQ resistor and avoid conflicts.
D6-P065-0023 §4.2.3.2 [body] Noise immunity will not be compromised when sharing the external ZQ calibration resistor.
D6-P065-0024 §4.2.3.2 [body] When automatic pull-down/ODT calibration is complete, pull-up/Voh calibration will start automatically.
D6-P065-0025 §4.2.3.2 [body] At the completion of pull-down/ODT and pull-up/Voh calibration, MR7 OP[0] bit (ZQUF) will be set if the new calibration codes do not match the currently latched codes.
D6-P065-0026 §4.2.3.2 [body] An MRR of this bit will notify the memory controller that new calibration results are available and that a ZQCal Latch operation (following ZQCal Latch timing constraints) should be issued to ensure accurate calibration of Pull-down, ODT and Voh is consistently maintained.
D6-P065-0027 §4.2.3.2 [body] The setting of ZQUF is unique to each die regardless of configuration or sharing of the ZQ pin(s).
D6-P065-0028 §4.2.3.2 [body] Alternatively, the memory controller may choose not to monitor ZQUF and periodically initiate a ZQCal Latch operation by setting the appropriate functional mode in the MR6 register.
D6-P066-0001 §4.2.3.2 [body] Setting the MR7 OP[3] = ZQ Stop will halt all background calibration activity.
D6-P066-0002 §4.2.3.2 [body] Re-setting MR7 OP[3] to zero will immediately start a calibration sequence, where all DDR6 dies sharing the ZQ resource will recalibrate in a serial fashion.
D6-P066-0009 §4.2.3.2 [table_cell] MRR to check ZQUF
D6-P066-0014 §4.2.3.2 [body] Latching ZQ calibration results is accomplished with the MRUPDATE sequence for ZQ Latch during initialization and MRW command during runtime as shown in Figure 1.
D6-P066-0015 §4.2.3.2 [body] This command loads new calibration results into the pull-down/ODT devices and pull-up drivers.
D6-P066-0016 §4.2.3.2 [body] A ZQCal Latch command operation may be issuedinitiated anytime outside of power-down when there are no DQ operations pending or in progress.
D6-P066-0017 §4.2.3.2 [body] The results from the most-recently completed calibration will always be latched with each ZQCal Latch commandmode register writes through MRUPDATE or MRW.
D6-P066-0018 §4.2.3.2 [body] If CA/CS ODT is enabled, the CS shall maintain a deselect state during tZQLAT to allow CA/CSODT calibration settings to be updated.
D6-P066-0019 §4.2.3.2 [body] Operations that initiate DQ operations are always dis-allowed during tZQLAT.
D6-P066-0020 §4.2.3.2 [body] The DQ and CA/CSODT calibration values will not be updated until ZQCal Latch commandoperation is performed and tZQLAT has been met, with the following exceptions: ZQ calibration will automatically occur for each die on power up/initialization and after Reset_n pin assertion.
D6-P066-0021 §4.2.3.2 [body] A ZQCal Latch command operation may be issuedinitiated to all DDR6 dies with the shared clock, on or after Tg.
D6-P066-0022 §4.2.3.2 [body] When a ZQCal Latch commandoperation is executed the ZQUF bit will be reset to 0 before the expiration of tZQLAT.
D6-P066-0024 §4.2.3.2 [body] To maintain Pull-down/ODT calibration and Voh calibration:
D6-P066-0025 §4.2.3.2 [body] 1) Periodically, based on tZQINT, issue an MRR to MR7 to check the ZQUF OP[0] bit status for each DDR6 die.
D6-P066-0026 §4.2.3.2 [body] 2) If MR06 OP[0]= 00000001B, issue a ZQCal Latch commandfunctional mode register write.
D6-P066-0027 §4.2.3.2 [body] Repeat 1-2 as needed.
D6-P066-0028 §4.2.3.2 [body] Alternatively, the memory controller may choose not to monitor ZQUF and periodically issue ZQCal Latch commandoperation.
D6-P066-0029 §4.2.3.2 [body] In this case the most-recently-completed calibration results will always be latched and will be no older than tZQINT.
D6-P067-0001 §4.2.3.2 [body] It may be permissible for the memory controller to ignore ZQUF and not issue ZQCal Latch commandoperation if both of the following are true:
D6-P067-0002 §4.2.3.2 [body] 1) CA/CSODT and CK ODT are disabled
D6-P067-0003 §4.2.3.2 [body] 2) The DDR6 SDRAM is in an idle state, or in Self Refresh or Power Down mode.
D6-P067-0004 §4.2.3.2 [body] Re-calibration will still occur in the background to ensure accurate driver/ODT settings are available should they be needed.
D6-P067-0005 §4.2.3.2 [body] In this case, the memory controller should ensure a ZQCal Latch commandoperation is performed prior to resuming data traffic if ZQUF is set.
D6-P067-0006 §4.2.3.2 [body] When the memory controller de-asserts ZQ Stop it shall reset MR7 OP[3] to zero for all dies sharing the ZQ resource within 100ns as described in 1.2.1.1.
D6-P067-0007 §4.2.3.2 [body] The ZQUF will be updated and results from the recalibration can be latched after the appropriate tZQCAL time (tZQCAL4, tZQCAL8 or tZQCALTBD) has been satisfied.
D6-P067-0009 §4.2.3.3 [body] In Background Calibration mode, a ZQ Stop function is provided to enable other device(s) to share the ZQ resistor.
D6-P067-0010 §4.2.3.3 [body] This function is enabled by MR7 OP[3] =ZQ Stop.
D6-P067-0011 §4.2.3.3 [body] When another device needs to use the ZQ resistor, MR7 OP[3] shall be set to 1 for all DDR6 dies that share ZQ resources.
D6-P067-0012 §4.2.3.3 [body] This will halt background calibration operations within delay time tZQSTOP (see Table 1) for each DDR6 die.
D6-P067-0013 §4.2.3.3 [body] Once tZQSTOP has expired for all DDR6 dies that share the ZQ resource, other dies may use the resource.
D6-P067-0014 §4.2.3.3 [body] When the ZQ resource is no longer needed by the other die(s) the ZQ Stop MR bit should be reset to 0 to allow background calibrations to continue normally.
D6-P067-0015 §4.2.3.3 [body] When the ZQ Stop MR bit is reset to 0, periodic background calibrations will be re-started.
D6-P067-0016 §4.2.3.3 [body] Since ZQ Stop inhibits the DDR6 die from recalibrating, note that changing system conditions while the ZQ Stop MR bit is set may cause the DDR6 ZQ calibration accuracy to deviate from specification.
D6-P067-0017 §4.2.3.3 [body] To ensure continued accurate calibration as discussed in section 1.1.2.2, the ZQ Stop MR bit shall not be set for longer than tZQINT in background calibration mode.
D6-P067-0018 §4.2.3.3 [body] Stopping Background Calibration when VDDQ is Powered Off
D6-P067-0019 §4.2.3.3 [body] In Background Calibration mode, before entering Power-down mode, the calibration shall may be halted by setting ZQ Stop on initiator die.
D6-P067-0020 §4.2.3.3 [body] ZQ Stop may be issued on Target dies asynchronously or simultaneously.
D6-P067-0021 §4.2.3.3 [body] When Power-down mode is exited, ZQ Stop should be reset to 0 to re-enable background calibration.
D6-P067-0022 §4.2.3.3 [body] Resetting of ZQ Stop on the ZQ Initiator die will start background calibration(s) immediately.
D6-P067-0023 §4.2.3.3 [body] In Background Calibration mode, after expiration of the appropriate tZQCAL time (tZQCAL2, tZQCAL4, tZQCAL8 or tZQCALTBD) from resetting of ZQ Stop, the memory controller may check the ZQUF flags or may issue a ZQCal Latch commandoperation to each die sharing the ZQ resource.
D6-P067-0024 §4.2.3.3 [body] To guarantee recalibration of all dies sharing the ZQ resource within tZQCAL, the MR7 OP[3] ZQ Stop bit shall be reset for all Target die(s) sharing the ZQ resource either before, or no later than 100ns after, the MR7 OP[3] ZQ Stop bit is reset on the ZQ Initiator die.
D6-P068-0002 §4.2.3.3 [table_cell] Delay Time from ZQ Stop Bit Set to ZQ Resistor Available
D6-P068-0005 §4.2.3.3 [body] Setting the ZQ Reset MR bit resets the output impedance calibration to a default accuracy of ±30% across process, voltage, and temperature.
D6-P068-0006 §4.2.3.3 [body] The ZQ Reset command operation is also executed by writing MR7 OP[2] = 1b.
D6-P068-0007 §4.2.3.3 [body] The ZQ Reset command operation will also reset the ZQ Stop MR7 OP[3] and ZQUF MR7 OP[0] bits to 0.
D6-P068-0008 §4.2.3.3 [body] ZQ Reset will not change the state of the ZQ Interval MR bits MR7 OP[5:4].
D6-P068-0009 §4.2.3.3 [body] If CA/CS ODTis enabled, the CA bus shall maintain a deselect state during tZQRESET to allow CA/CSODT calibration settings to be updated.
D6-P068-0010 §4.2.3.3 [body] Operations that initiate DQ operations are always disallowed during tZQRESET.
D6-P068-0011 §4.2.3.3 [body] The ZQ Reset MR bit shall be reset to 0b by the DRAM after tZQRESET.
D6-P068-0012 §4.2.3.3 [body] To reset the ODT and output impedance in a multi-die package, the ZQ Reset function shall be issued to all dies regardless of ZQ Initiator or ZQ Target designation.
D6-P068-0013 §4.2.3.3 [body] The host shall ensure ZQ RESET and ZQ STOP mode register bits are not set up simultaneously.
D6-P068-0015 §4.2.3.3 [body] Up to NZQ DDR6 dies within a single package may connect to the same ZQ resistor.
D6-P068-0016 §4.2.3.3 [body] ZQ stop function is executed by writing MR7 OP[2] =1b and resetting MR7 OP[2] =0b is managed by Initiator die and may be issued to other die sharing a ZQ resource asynchronously or simultaneously.
D6-P068-0017 §4.2.3.3 [body] ZQCal Latch commands, when required, are necessary for each die.
D6-P068-0018 §4.2.3.3 [body] When multiple dies share a ZQ resource and a ZQ Initiator die is managing calibration, the MR7 OP[3] ZQ Stop bit shall be set to 0B for all ZQ Target die sharing the ZQ resource.
D6-P068-0019 §4.2.3.3 [body] No other arbitration considerations are required.
D6-P068-0021 §4.2.3.3 [body] Each DDR6 die within a package includes a single ZQ pin and associated ZQ calibration circuitry.
D6-P068-0022 §4.2.3.3 [body] Calibration value from this circuit will be generated and used according to the following protocol:
D6-P068-0023 §4.2.3.3 [body] • Setting of the ZQ Stop bit always has priority over background calibration processes
D6-P069-0001 §4.2.3.3 [body] • If the ZQ Stop MR bit is set to 1 while a background ZQ calibration is in progress, the background calibration will be interrupted
D6-P069-0002 §4.2.3.3 [body] • A background calibration will start immediately when the ZQ Stop MR bit is reset to 0
D6-P069-0003 §4.2.3.3 [body] • A background calibration will not start while the ZQ Stop MR bit is set to 1
D6-P069-0004 §4.2.3.3 [body] • The ZQUF bit will not be reset by setting of the ZQ Stop bit to 1
D6-P069-0005 §4.2.3.3 [body] • The ZQUF bit will be reset only by a ZQCal Latch commandoperation or ZQ Reset
D6-P069-0006 §4.2.3.3 [body] • The ZQUF bit will be set if calibration codes do not match the currently latched codes even when the die is in Self-refresh or Power-down mode, providing ZQ Stop is not set.
D6-P069-0008 §4.2.3.3 [body] To use the ZQ calibration function, a 120 ohm, ±1% tolerance external resistor shall be connected between the ZQ pin and VDDQ.
D6-P069-0009 §4.2.3.3 [body] The total capacitive loading on the ZQ pin shall be limited to CZQ_N.
D6-P070-0004 §4.2.3.3 [body] Command MRR MR7 Check Initiator die OP[1] (or) Issued to all dies
D6-P070-0005 §4.2.3.3 [body] Command MRW MR7 set ZQ Interval (or) issued to initiator die
D6-P070-0009 §4.2.3.3 [body] Check each die Wait time based on tZQINIT
D6-P071-0001 §4.2.4 [body] During DDR6 DRAM initial training, Data (DQ) bits are used to control the DRAM and receive feedback from it.
D6-P071-0002 §4.2.4 [body] At this stage, the host has no knowledge of the mapping between host DQ signals and DRAM DQ pins.
D6-P071-0003 §4.2.4 [body] The following sequence enables the host to discover the DQ mapping before training CA or DQ.
D6-P071-0004 §4.2.4 [body] The host shall perform mapping discovery for DQ [2:0].
D6-P071-0005 §4.2.4 [body] Mapping discovery for the remaining DQ bits at this stage, for different configurations (e.g., 2p6 or 1p6), is optional.
D6-P071-0006 §4.2.4.1 [body] DQ Swizzle Discovery Mode (DSDM) is entered by issuing an MRUPD sequence that writes OP[x] = 1b to mode register MRxx.
D6-P071-0007 §4.2.4.1 [body] Upon entering DSDM, the DRAM shall configure all DQ signals as outputs.
D6-P071-0008 §4.2.4.2 [body] While in DSDM, the host and DRAM shall follow the procedure below to discover the DQ bit mapping:
D6-P071-0009 §4.2.4.2 [body] 1) The host shall issue an MRUPD sequence to program the DQ Mapping Mode register (MRxy) with OP[x] = 1b, as defined in Table 1, to select and enable a single DQ bit.
D6-P071-0010 §4.2.4.2 [body] 2) In response, the DRAM shall drive the selected DQ bit to a static High level while driving all other DQ bits to a static Low level.
D6-P071-0011 §4.2.4.2 [body] The host shall sample the DQ signals and record the corresponding DQ mapping.
D6-P071-0012 §4.2.4.2 [body] 3) Steps 1 and 2 shall be repeated until the host has discovered the mapping for all DQ bits.
D6-P071-0013 §4.2.4.2 [body] 4) If the host writes an undefined DQ location (OP[6] and OP[7]) in MRxy, the DRAM shall stop driving all DQ.
D6-P071-0014 §4.2.4.2 [table_cell] NOTE 1: DQ mapping register 1 is required only when 1p12 configuration is enabled.
D6-P071-0015 §4.2.4.3 [body] To exit DQ Swizzle Discovery Mode (DSDM), the host shall issue an MRUPD sequence to program MRxx with OP[x] = 0B.
D6-P071-0016 §4.2.4.3 [body] After completing the required DQ mapping discovery and exiting DSDM, the host shall proceed to Chip Select Training Mode using the discovered DQ control bit mapping.
D6-P072-0001 §4.2.5.1 [body] DDR6 comes with Frequency Set Points (FSP) allowing the DDR6 SDRAM to be switched between 2 different operating frequencies, which can have different termination and Vref values.
D6-P072-0002 §4.2.5.1 [body] MRs from these 2 FSPs share the same register addresses.
D6-P072-0003 §4.2.5.1 [body] Frequency Set Point Write/Read (FSP-WR) controls which group of the 2 registers are being accessed.
D6-P072-0004 §4.2.5.1 [body] Frequency Set Point Operation (FSP-OP) indicates which of the 2 FSP is applied.
D6-P072-0005 §4.2.5.1 [body] Prior to an input clock frequency change event, the DRAM is already operating at FSP[x] based on FSP-OP[x], FSP[y] MRs can be updated by setting the FSP-WR[y] ahead of frequency change operation.
D6-P072-0006 §4.2.5.1 [body] Setting the FSP-OP[y] bit indicates to the DRAM that new FSP is going to be applied.
D6-P072-0007 §4.2.5.1 [body] Power Down Entry or Self Refresh Power Down Entry command is issued to the DRAM.
D6-P072-0008 §4.2.5.1 [body] DRAM applies FSP[y] MRs after entering into Power Down or Self Refresh Power Down state.
D6-P072-0009 §4.2.5.1 [body] Input clock frequency can be updated while the DRAM is in either Power Down or Self Refresh Power Down state.
D6-P072-0010 §4.2.5.1 [body] Once the new input clock frequency is stable, Power Down exit or Self Refresh Power Down Exit command is issued to the DRAM.
D6-P072-0025 §4.2.5.1 [body] DRAM operation is based on FSP[y] MRs
D6-P072-0026 §4.2.5.1 [body] DRAM operation is based on FSP[x] MRs DRAM operation is based on
D6-P072-0033 §4.2.5.1 [body] DDR6 SDRAM supports input clock frequency change under the following steps:
D6-P072-0034 §4.2.5.1 [body] 1) MRs of the FSP[y] used for the next frequency already contain the correct values used for the that frequency
D6-P072-0035 §4.2.5.1 [body] 2) FSP-OP[y] used for the next frequency is set indicating that new FSP[y] is going to be applied
D6-P072-0036 §4.2.5.1 [body] 3) Power Down Entry or Self Refresh Power Down Entry command is issued to the DRAM
D6-P072-0037 §4.2.5.1 [body] 4) DRAM applies FSP[y] MRs after entering to Power Down or Self Refresh Power Down
D6-P072-0038 §4.2.5.1 [body] 5) Input clock frequency can be updated
D6-P073-0001 §4.2.5.1 [body] 6) Power Down exit or Self Refresh Power Down Exit command is issued to the DRAM
D6-P074-0001 §4.2.5.2 [body] Host shall inform the DRAM of operating clock frequency via Frequency MR.
D6-P074-0002 §4.2.5.2 [body] At initialize sequence, Frequency MR shall be set by MRUPDATE command to the value matching to the Clock frequency on MR09[3:0].
D6-P074-0003 §4.2.5.2 [body] After the first Frequency MR is set, host shall sustain clock input without changing the frequency at least tDLYCAL.
D6-P074-0004 §4.2.5.2 [body] The 2nd FSP Frequency MR can be set without requiring clock frequency matching or a sustained clock signal for tDLYCAL.
D6-P074-0005 §4.2.5.2 [body] Only in MRUPD mode can the Frequency MR of the 2nd FSP be set using FSP-WR on MR00[2].
D6-P074-0006 §4.2.5.2 [body] Once the registers of Frequency MR are set, and the MRUPD mode is exited, the input clock frequency needs to match the value selected by FSP-OP.Related timing parameters are tCCD_L, tCCD_L_WR, tCCD_L_WR2, tCCD_dlr, tCCD_WR_dlr, nODTLon_WR and nODTLon_WR_NT.
D6-P075-0001 §4.2.5.2 [table_cell] NOTE 1: tCCD_dlr [nCK] = Max(12nCK, 2x(RU(2ns/(2xtCK))) defines the delay between READ to READ commands to different logical ranks.
D6-P076-0001 §4.2.5.2 [table_cell] NOTE 2: tCCD_WR_dlr [nCK] = Max(12nCK, 4x(RU(2ns/(4xtCK))) defines the delay between WRITE to WRITE commands to different logical ranks.
D6-P077-0001 §4.2.6.1 [body] Chip Select Training Mode (CSTM) is a method to facilitate the loopback of a sampled sequence of the Chip Select (CS) signal.
D6-P077-0002 §4.2.6.1 [body] Once this mode is enabled, the device actively samples and drives feedback, where the CS signal is only sampled on the rising edge of even Clock (CK) cycles.
D6-P077-0003 §4.2.6.1 [body] The host follows the CSTM procedure to align all phases of connected devices to achieve the maximum CS eye.
D6-P077-0004 §4.2.6.1 [body] CSTM has two (2) states which can be toggled by DQ[1:0]: Command (CMD) State and Train State.
D6-P077-0005 §4.2.6.1 [body] During Command State, only the MRUPDATE sequence is actively processed.
D6-P077-0006 §4.2.6.1 [body] The Host drives CA[5:0] low outside CMD State during CSTM.
D6-P077-0007 §4.2.6.1 [body] During Train State, every set of 34 CK rising edge samples (68 CK in total) will be included in a logical computation to determine the CSTM sample result that is sent back to the Host on the DQ2.
D6-P077-0008 §4.2.6.1 [body] CSTM includes making changes to VREFCS.
D6-P077-0009 §4.2.6.1 [body] VREFCS update and stabilization time depends on the VREF Current Generator (VRCG) for shorter delays.
D6-P077-0010 §4.2.6.1 [body] If VRCG is enabled via MRxx[b], tVREFCS_short or tVREFCS_long delay times are used, depending on the number of VREFCS steps being change.
D6-P077-0011 §4.2.6.1 [body] However, if VRCG is disabled, all VREFCS changes require the tVREFCS_weak delay.
D6-P077-0012 §4.2.6.2 [body] Prior to entering CSTM, MRUPDATE Mode must be enabled by the MCME command.
D6-P077-0013 §4.2.6.2 [body] VREFDQ, DQ/DQS ODT and DQ drive strength settings must be adjusted to values that allow slow speed operation.
D6-P077-0014 §4.2.6.2 [body] CSTM is entered and exited through MR05 (Functional Mode Register).
D6-P077-0015 §4.2.6.2 [body] Entry or Exit on either PSD (2p3 or 2p6 configuration) will apply the operation to both PSDs.
D6-P077-0016 §4.2.6.2 [body] Entry and Exit operations must wait for previous MRUPDATE operations to complete.
D6-P077-0017 §4.2.6.3 [body] To enter CSTM, the Host issues the MRUPDATE sequence to MR05[7:0]=024h with DQS differentially low prior to the 1st CS pulse of the MRUPDATE sequence.
D6-P077-0018 §4.2.6.3 [body] DQS must remain differentially low during CSTM entry.
D6-P077-0019 §4.2.6.3 [body] Once tMRUPD_Delay has been satisfied, CSTM has been entered in Command State.
D6-P078-0001 §4.2.6.4 [body] To exit CSTM, the Host issues the MRUPDATE sequence to MR05[7:0]=00h during Command State with DQS differentially low.
D6-P078-0002 §4.2.6.4 [body] During CSTM Exit, DQS must remain differentially low until tMRUPD_Delay is satisfied.
D6-P078-0003 §4.2.6.4 [body] After tMRUPD_Delay, CSTM mode is exited.
D6-P078-0017 §4.2.6.4 [body] Issuing an MRUPDATE sequence exit during the CMD state will exit both CSTM and MRUPDATE modes within tMRUPD_Delay.
D6-P078-0018 §4.2.6.5 [body] CSTM uses the DQ bus, sampled by DQS, for function control.
D6-P078-0019 §4.2.6.5 [body] DQ[1:0] are input pins latched by DQS for CSTM control logic.
D6-P078-0020 §4.2.6.5 [body] The host is required to drive identical waveforms on the DQ[1:0] pins of both PSDs.
D6-P078-0021 §4.2.6.5 [body] During CSTM, DQS_t/DQS_c are input only to sample DQ[1:0] as the function code.
D6-P078-0022 §4.2.6.5 [body] DQS is required to be differentially low except while sampling DQ[1:0].
D6-P078-0023 §4.2.6.5 [body] CSTM uses a four (4)eight (8) bit Function Code (FuncCode[37:0]), generated by foureight DQS rising pulses to sample DQ[1:0].
D6-P078-0024 §4.2.6.5 [body] Entering CSTM internally resets the DQS rising pulse count to 0 to prepare for counting foureight rising pulses.
D6-P078-0025 §4.2.6.5 [body] Each Function Code is the combined sample value of the 2nd, 4th, 6th, and 8th DQS differential rising edges (DQSR2, DQSR4, DQSR6, DQSR8, respectively) and the 4th DQS differential rising edge (DQSR4) as shown in the CSTM Function Code Mapping table.
D6-P078-0026 §4.2.6.5 [body] DQ2 is an output pin for the CSTM sample results, per PSD.
D6-P078-0027 §4.2.6.5 [body] All other DQ pins, if present, are unused during CSTM and are disabled.
D6-P079-0001 §4.2.6.5 [body] As shown in the figure below, the leading and trailing DQS differentially low timing (tDQSPreTrain and tDQSPost- Train, respectively) must be met before and after the DQS pulses.
D6-P079-0002 §4.2.6.5 [body] tFunc_Delay is the minimum time between Function Codes.
D6-P079-0003 §4.2.6.5 [body] Each DQS pulse must meet tDQSPWTrain on both high and low pulse.
D6-P079-0004 §4.2.6.5 [body] DQ[1:0] must meet setup and hold timing (tDSTrain and tDHTrain, respectively).
D6-P079-0005 §4.2.6.5 [body] The setup timing tDSTrain is defined as DQ[1:0] leading edge to DQSR2 and the hold timing tDHTrain is defined as DQSR2 to DQ[1:0] trailing edge.
D6-P079-0006 §4.2.6.5 [body] The same setup and hold timing requirements apply to DQSR4, DQSR6, and DQSR8.
D6-P079-0012 §4.2.6.5 [body] NOTE: CK and time breaks are not included.
D6-P079-0013 §4.2.6.6 [body] Command (CMD) State is the default state after CSTM entry.
D6-P079-0014 §4.2.6.6 [body] CMD state is MRUPDATE mode enabled with CSTM functions controlled by DQ[1:0] and DQS.
D6-P079-0015 §4.2.6.6 [body] Only the MRUPDATE sequence and DES command are valid in the CMD State.
D6-P079-0016 §4.2.6.6 [body] During CSTM, the MRUPDATE sequence is only used to trigger functions and to change shadow register bits related to CSTM settings such as VREF CS, CS ODT, CK ODT, DQ/DQS ODT, and DQ driver strength, as needed.
D6-P079-0017 §4.2.6.6 [body] Shadow register "Apply" is needed to update any of the normal mode register values for use during CSTM CMD State.
D6-P079-0018 §4.2.6.6 [body] The VREFCS generator references the VREF CS Mode Registers settings while in CMD State.
D6-P079-0019 §4.2.6.6 [body] Train State is used to adjust the VREF CS shadow registers setting, perform training sequence sampling, and output training results on DQ2.
D6-P079-0020 §4.2.6.6 [body] Train State uses CK ODT and CS ODT shadow register values, as well the VREF CS shadow register value (CA ODT and VREF CA shadow register values are also applied but have not impact since the CA bus is not used).
D6-P079-0021 §4.2.6.6 [body] In Train State, the command decoder is disabled and CA is required to be low all the time.
D6-P079-0022 §4.2.6.6 [body] VREFCS change functions will only apply to the VREF CS shadow register, which updates the VREFCS generator since it is referencing the shadow register while in Train State.
D6-P080-0001 §4.2.6.6 [body] The Host can apply the VREF CS shadow register value to VREF CS MR (MR1983[6:0]) per training results during CMD State or after exiting CSTM using the VREFCS and CS ODT Apply Functional Mode (MR05[xx]6[7:0]=04h).Shadow register values are not changed by entering or exiting CSTM, by entering CMD State, or by entering Train State.
D6-P080-0002 §4.2.6.6 [body] forces the return from Train State to CMD State, restoring use of the normal VREF CS Mode Register.
D6-P080-0003 §4.2.6.6 [body] Issuing the shadow register apply during CMD State or after exiting CSTM, switching from CMD State to Train State, and switching from Train State to CMD State, requires waiting the appropriate VREFCS update and stabilization delay which depends on the status of VRCG and the numbers of VREFCS steps being changed.
D6-P080-0004 §4.2.6.6 [body] DQ[1:0] are input pins during both CMD and Train States.
D6-P080-0005 §4.2.6.6 [body] DQ2 is off during CMD State.
D6-P080-0006 §4.2.6.6 [body] DQ2 remains off in Train State until the first time it is used as output pin during SMP function.
D6-P080-0007 §4.2.6.6 [body] DQS_t/DQS_c are input pins during both states.
D6-P080-0017 §4.2.6.6 [table_cell] Previously Set Value
D6-P080-0020 §4.2.6.6 [table_cell] Previously Set Value
D6-P080-0023 §4.2.6.6 [table_cell] Previously Set Value
D6-P080-0025 §4.2.6.6 [table_cell] Previously Set Value
D6-P080-0027 §4.2.6.6 [body] NOTE: 2: CK and time break are not included in the figure.
D6-P080-0028 §4.2.6.6 [body] The diagram is intended to serve as a reference for behavior rather than for timing purposes.
D6-P080-0029 §4.2.6.6 [body] NOTE: 3: MRUPDATE sequence to set VREFCS and CS ODT during CMD State is only for illustration purposes
D6-P080-0030 §4.2.6.6 [body] NOTE: 4: VREFCS and CS ODT registers are shown as updated to an appropriate previously set value as recommended in section XXX, Voltage Ramp and Device Initialization
D6-P080-0031 §4.2.6.7 [body] When a Func- Code[37:0] is received, the corresponding functions executes within tFunc_Delay.
D6-P080-0032 §4.2.6.7 [body] CSTM uses a four (4)eight (8) bit function code (FuncCode[37:0]) to execute training functions.
D6-P080-0033 §4.2.6.7 [body] Some functions can only be used in certain CSTM states.
D6-P080-0034 §4.2.6.7 [body] FuncCode[37:0] issued outside of an allowed state will be ignored.
D6-P080-0035 §4.2.6.7 [body] Any unused Func- Code[37:0] is RFU will be ignored.
D6-P081-0001 §4.2.6.7 [table_cell] Force “CMD State".
D6-P081-0002 §4.2.6.7 [table_cell] Use normal VREF and ODT MRs.
D6-P081-0003 §4.2.6.7 [table_cell] Force “Train State".
D6-P081-0004 §4.2.6.7 [table_cell] Use VREF and ODT shadow registers.
D6-P081-0005 §4.2.6.7 [table_cell] Set VREF CS shadow register to FuncCode[6:0]
D6-P081-0017 §4.2.6.7 [body] FuncCode[37:0]=48b'00000000: "Force CMD State (FCS)" function.
D6-P081-0018 §4.2.6.7 [body] The FCS function may be issued in both the CMD State and the Train State.
D6-P082-0001 §4.2.6.7 [body] FuncCode[37:0]=48b'00000001: "CK Phase Shift (CPS)" function.
D6-P082-0002 §4.2.6.7 [body] The CPS function will force an internal CK phase shift 90° (1 CK).
D6-P082-0003 §4.2.6.7 [body] This function is only allowed during CMD State.
D6-P082-0004 §4.2.6.7 [body] The Host uses the CPS function to align CK phase of all devices that share CK.
D6-P082-0021 §4.2.6.7 [body] NOTE: This diagram is only a high-level function reference for CPS function.
D6-P082-0022 §4.2.6.7 [body] NOTE: Host CK doesn't change before and after CPS, only the internal CK phase is changed by CPS function.
D6-P082-0023 §4.2.6.7 [body] FuncCode[37:0]=48b'00001000: "Force Train State (FTS)" function.
D6-P082-0024 §4.2.6.7 [body] The FTS function forces Train State and switches from using normal VREF and ODT MRs to using the VREF and ODT shadow registers.
D6-P082-0025 §4.2.6.7 [body] The FTS function can be issued in both the CMD State and Train State.
D6-P082-0026 §4.2.6.7 [body] During Train State, VREFCS is controlled by the VREF CS shadow register.
D6-P082-0027 §4.2.6.7 [body] FuncCode[3:0]=4b'1001: “VREFCS Step Down (VSD)" function.
D6-P082-0028 §4.2.6.7 [body] The VSD function forces the VREF_CS shadow register value to decrease by one step.
D6-P082-0029 §4.2.6.7 [body] If the register is at the minimum value, the VREFCS will remain at the minimum value if issued additional VSD functions.
D6-P082-0030 §4.2.6.7 [body] The VSD function can be issued in either CMD or Train state.
D6-P082-0031 §4.2.6.7 [body] FuncCode[3:0]=4b'1010: “VREFCS Step Up (VSU)" function.
D6-P082-0032 §4.2.6.7 [body] The VSU function forces the VREF_CS shadow register value to increase by one step.
D6-P082-0033 §4.2.6.7 [body] If the register is at the maximum value, the VREFCS will remain at the maximum value if issued additional VSU functions.
D6-P082-0034 §4.2.6.7 [body] The VSU function can be issued in either CMD or Train state.
D6-P082-0035 §4.2.6.7 [body] FuncCode[7:0]=8b'1VVVVVVV: "VREF Set Value (VSV)" function.
D6-P082-0036 §4.2.6.7 [body] The VSV function sets the VREF CS shadow register bits VREFCS[6:0] to FuncCode[6:0].
D6-P082-0037 §4.2.6.7 [body] Note: VREFCS does not use the VREF CS shadow register during the CMD state, so changes will not be applied until Train State is entered.
D6-P082-0038 §4.2.6.7 [body] Continuous VSD and VSU operation is possible if tFUNC_Delay between each operation is
D6-P083-0002 §4.2.6.7 [body] However, tThe Host must wait the required VREFCS update and settling delay after last VSU or VSD any VSV function.
D6-P083-0014 §4.2.6.7 [table_cell] Previously Set Value
D6-P083-0016 §4.2.6.7 [table_cell] Previously Set Value
D6-P083-0017 §4.2.6.7 [body] NOTE: 1:CK and time break are not included.
D6-P083-0018 §4.2.6.7 [body] The diagram is intended to serve as a reference for consecutive VSU/VSDVSV behavior and tVREFCS_short/long timing requirement.
D6-P083-0019 §4.2.6.7 [body] Any additional timing requirements not depicted in the figure must also be satisfied.
D6-P083-0020 §4.2.6.7 [body] NOTE: 2: VREF CS registers are shown initially updated to an appropriate previously set value as recommended in section XXX, Voltage Ramp and Device Initialization.
D6-P083-0021 §4.2.6.7 [body] FuncCode[37:0]=48b'00001100: “Switch DQ2 Output (SDO)" function.
D6-P083-0022 §4.2.6.7 [body] The SDO function will switch DQ2 to output training results from the internal CK phase 0 to the internal CK phase 180.
D6-P083-0023 §4.2.6.7 [body] After tDQ2Output is met, DQ2 will output the CK phase 180 CS training result.
D6-P083-0024 §4.2.6.7 [body] Issuing the SDO function when DQ2 is already outputting CK phase 180 will not have any new effect, however tDQ2Output still needs to be met.
D6-P083-0025 §4.2.6.7 [body] FuncCode[37:0]=48b'00001111: "Sample (SMP)" function.
D6-P083-0026 §4.2.6.7 [body] The SMP function performs the following operations to prepare for CS sampling: reset CS sample counter and logic, reset CK phase 0 and 180 internal training results to fail, and force DQ2 high.
D6-P083-0027 §4.2.6.7 [body] After the reset to CSTM delay (tRST2CSTM) is met, the CS pattern can be sampled.
D6-P083-0028 §4.2.6.7 [body] The Host uses a “1100" toggle pattern on CS, where each '1' or '0' represents a 2nCK long pulse.
D6-P083-0029 §4.2.6.7 [body] The CS is sampled on rising CK edges (internal CK phases 0 and 180).
D6-P083-0030 §4.2.6.7 [body] The Host sends 34 CS toggles (17 High and 17 Low pulses, each pulse is 2nCK wide, 68nCK in total).
D6-P083-0031 §4.2.6.7 [body] The first CS high sample starts the internal CK counting on the next CS high sample.
D6-P083-0032 §4.2.6.7 [body] Once counting begins, the sample and check CS will continue for the even CK edges over the next 64nCK.
D6-P083-0033 §4.2.6.7 [body] The CS high pulse width is checked on the internal CK phase 0 (Q0) and the CS low pulse width is checked on internal CK phase 180 (Q2).
D6-P083-0034 §4.2.6.7 [body] Passing samples are recorded as '0' and failing samples recorded as '1'.
D6-P083-0035 §4.2.6.7 [body] After counting 64nCK cycles, the 16 Q0 results (CS sample on CK phase 0 is expected to be high, when passing is recorded as "0") and the 16 Q2 results (CS sample on CK phase 180 is expected to be low, when passing is recorded as "0") are checked separately with a logical OR.
D6-P083-0036 §4.2.6.7 [body] The Q0 training result are output on DQ2, where a “0" indicates a pass and a "1" indicates failure.
D6-P083-0037 §4.2.6.7 [body] If a pass is detected, the output remains at "0" until switched to CMD State or next SMP/SDO function.
D6-P083-0038 §4.2.6.7 [body] The Host captures the Q0 pass/fail results after tDQ2Output, uses SDO function to switch DQ2 to output Q2 training results, and then captures the Q2 pass/fail results after tDQ2Output.
D6-P083-0039 §4.2.6.7 [body] The CS output result are reset by issuing the SMP function before sending out the next CS toggle pattern.
D6-P083-0040 §4.2.6.7 [body] Excessive CS pulses will not cause a new CS sampling unless a new SMP function has been issued.
D6-P083-0041 §4.2.6.7 [body] DQS is required to be differentially low during SMP function until all CS samples are
D6-P084-0001 §4.2.6.7 [body] done and tDQ2Output is met.
D6-P084-0002 §4.2.6.7 [body] Once the Host issues a new SMP function, DQ2 will be pulled to high and the internal training results will be reset to fail.
D6-P084-0014 §4.2.6.7 [body] Issuing MRUPDATE sequences or executing a new CSTM function must wait for a previous operation to complete.
D6-P084-0015 §4.2.6.7 [body] The only exception is an MRUPDATE sequence for CSTM Exit, which may be used to exit CSTM during CMD State with DQS differentially low at least tFunc_Delay plus the required VREFCS update and settling delay after the last DQS differential rising edge.
D6-P084-0016 §4.2.6.7 [body] The following figure is an example of the sequence to switch to CMD State and exit CSTM when DQS pulse count has been lost.
D6-P084-0017 §4.2.6.7 [body] The Host issues at least 816 DQS pulses with DQ[1:0]=00b, where each DQS pulse meets tFunc_Delay between rising edges.
D6-P084-0018 §4.2.6.7 [body] CSTM exit command must meet tFunc_Delay the required VREFCS update and settling delay (tVREFCS_long in this example) after the last DQS differential rising edge.
D6-P084-0019 §4.2.6.7 [body] CAs remain as "L", as the starting state may be the Train State.
D6-P084-0028 §4.2.6.8 [body] An example of the CSTM sequence is described below:
D6-P084-0029 §4.2.6.8 [body] 1) If not in MRUPDATE mode, then use MCME to enter MRUPDATE mode.
D6-P084-0030 §4.2.6.8 [body] 2) Complete the DQ Swizzling Discovery Mode (DSDM) operation to identify the host to DRAM DQ mapping.
D6-P084-0031 §4.2.6.8 [body] 3) Set DQS differentially low.
D6-P084-0032 §4.2.6.8 [body] Enter CSTM and wait tMRUPD_Delay.
D6-P084-0033 §4.2.6.8 [body] 4) Update the CS_ODT and VREF_CS Mode Register values, if required, and Apply.
D6-P084-0034 §4.2.6.8 [body] If required, update the CS ODT and VREF CS shadow register values and Apply to normal MR for use in CMD State.
D6-P084-0035 §4.2.6.8 [body] The VREF CS normal MR value must be valid in CMD State to allow the MRUPDATE sequence to execute properly.
D6-P085-0001 §4.2.6.8 [body] 5) Update the CS_ODT and VREF_CS shadow register values, if required.
D6-P085-0002 §4.2.6.8 [body] The VREFCS value must be valid to allow the MRUPDATE sequence to execute properly, and Train State will use the VREF CS shadow register value.
D6-P085-0003 §4.2.6.8 [body] If required, update the CS ODT and VREF CS shadow register values for use in Train State.
D6-P085-0004 §4.2.6.8 [body] 6) Switch from CMD State to Train State.
D6-P085-0005 §4.2.6.8 [body] The shadow registers are being used.
D6-P085-0006 §4.2.6.8 [body] Wait for tFunc_Delay and VREFCS update and settling delay.
D6-P085-0007 §4.2.6.8 [body] 7) Update the VREFCS setting via the VSU or VSD function.
D6-P085-0008 §4.2.6.8 [body] Wait VREFCS update and settling delay after the last VSU/VSD.
D6-P085-0009 §4.2.6.8 [body] 8) Issue the SMP function to prepare for sampling.
D6-P085-0010 §4.2.6.8 [body] After tFunc_Delay, DRAM is ready for CS sampling can begin and DQ2 output will be “1".
D6-P085-0011 §4.2.6.8 [body] 9) Perform CS training (VREFCS, CS timing): a) The Host sends the CS toggle pattern 110011001100..., 68nCK in total.
D6-P085-0012 §4.2.6.8 [body] b) The CS toggle pattern is sampled with the first CS high sampled by an even clock edge.
