D6-P085-0013 §4.2.6.8 [body] Counting starts on the second CS high sample and counts 32 samples (64nCK) before sending the output results on DQ2.
D6-P085-0014 §4.2.6.8 [body] Within the 64nCK sample window, all Q0 CS samples must pass for the DQ2 output to be “0".
D6-P085-0015 §4.2.6.8 [body] c) The Host reads DQ2 after tDQ2Output and records it, issues the SDO function to output Q2 training results, and again reads DQ2 after tDQ2Output and records it.
D6-P085-0016 §4.2.6.8 [body] All Q2 CS samples must pass for the DQ2 output to be “0".
D6-P085-0017 §4.2.6.8 [body] d) The Host calculates the Q0 and Q2 training results, where both values being “0" is considered a pass.
D6-P085-0018 §4.2.6.8 [body] (Both PSDs and both the Q0 and Q2 phases need to be “0" for the training is considered PASS.)
D6-P085-0019 §4.2.6.8 [body] 10) Repeat step 7 to 9 as necessary to complete the first round of CS training.
D6-P085-0020 §4.2.6.8 [body] Host should analyze training results to identify DRAMs into four CK phase groups based on the center of CS eye.
D6-P085-0021 §4.2.6.8 [body] Different groups' CS eye center should be about 1 tCK apart.
D6-P085-0022 §4.2.6.8 [body] If all DRAM happens to be in same group then, skip step 11.
D6-P085-0023 §4.2.6.8 [body] 11) Use the FCS command to switch to the CMD State, wait tFUNC_Delay and the VREFCS update and settling delay.
D6-P085-0024 §4.2.6.8 [body] 12) Choose one of the CK phase group devices as the target group.
D6-P085-0025 §4.2.6.8 [body] Issue CPS function to the other CK phase groups to adjust their CS eye mask center to align with target CK phase group.
D6-P085-0026 §4.2.6.8 [body] Each CPS function will shift CS eye by 1 nCK (90 degrees).
D6-P085-0027 §4.2.6.8 [body] Multi CPS functions may be needed on CK phase groups that are multiple tCK away from target group.
D6-P085-0028 §4.2.6.8 [body] 13) Repeat step 6 to 9 for another round of CS training with all devices even CK phase aligned.
D6-P085-0029 §4.2.6.8 [body] The Host can analyze the training results and apply the CS delay to the center of common CS eye window to achieve the max CS eye margin.
D6-P085-0030 §4.2.6.8 [body] 14) Use the FCS command to switch to the CMD State, wait tFUNC_Delay and the VREFCS update and settling delay.
D6-P085-0032 §4.2.6.8 [body] After tMRUPD_Delay, CS is “trained".
D6-P085-0033 §4.2.6.9 [body] The following explains how to align the internal CK phase of different DRAM.
D6-P085-0034 §4.2.6.9 [body] Fundamentally, the purpose of CSTM is to align the CS eye of each DRAM, which can be described with a 2D shmoo of the CS to CK delay on the X-axis and the VREFCS on the Y-axis.
D6-P085-0035 §4.2.6.9 [body] The CS to CK delay (CS rising edge to CK rising edge) is a common setting that impacts all shared signals, while the VREFCS can be used to center each CS eye properly.
D6-P085-0036 §4.2.6.9 [body] To maximize the CS margin, all CS eye should be centered and the CS to CK delay is adjusted to compensate for mismatch between the external and internal clocks.
D6-P085-0037 §4.2.6.9 [body] During CSTM, the Host calculates the CS to CK delay referencing a fixed Host clock phase.
D6-P085-0038 §4.2.6.9 [body] This chosen phase remains fixed during whole training procedure.
D6-P085-0039 §4.2.6.9 [body] The example below chooses the Host clock phase Q2 as the reference clock phase for the CS to CK delay calculation.
D6-P085-0040 §4.2.6.9 [body] The following figure shows four DRAMs (A, B, C, D) to explain the CK phase detection during the CS-CK sweep, with an assumption of a 0.25tCK CS to CK mismatch.
D6-P085-0041 §4.2.6.9 [body] The host start CS-CK sweeping with the delay at 0.
D6-P086-0001 §4.2.6.9 [body] external to internal mismatch, the CS to T0 CK delay is 0.25tCK.
D6-P086-0002 §4.2.6.9 [body] DRAM A's Q1 aligns to T0.
D6-P086-0003 §4.2.6.9 [body] To have CS centered at Q0, the CS-CK delay will be 1.75tCK which is the center of CS eye mask.
D6-P086-0004 §4.2.6.9 [body] DRAM B's Q2 aligns to T0, so the center of CS eye mask will be 0.75tCK.
D6-P086-0005 §4.2.6.9 [body] DRAM C's Q3 aligns to T0, so the center of CS eye mask will be -0.25tCK or 3.75tCK.
D6-P086-0006 §4.2.6.9 [body] And DRAM D's Q0 aligns to T0, so the center of CS eye mask will be -2.75tCK.
D6-P086-0007 §4.2.6.9 [body] In this example, host selects DRAM B as the target for all other DRAMs to align to.
D6-P086-0008 §4.2.6.9 [body] The Host issues the CPS function once to DRAM A, twice to DRAM D and three times to DRAM C.
D6-P086-0009 §4.2.6.9 [body] After CPS functions, all CS eyes are center aligned to DRAM B, which has internal Q0 aligned to T2 CK.
D6-P086-0010 §4.2.6.9 [body] The CS eye center 0.75tCK is the extra delay needed to center CS at T2 CK, which is Q0 of all DRAM.
D6-P086-0011 §4.2.6.9 [body] The Host labels the internal phase accordingly.
D6-P088-0001 §4.2.7 [body] VREFCS is defined as a ratio of the VDDQ voltage.
D6-P088-0002 §4.2.7 [body] VREFCS is independently controlled per PSD with host MRW to MR86[6:0] in normal operation, or by the VREF_CS shadow register with VREFCS Apply Functional Mode (MR06[2]=1) in MRUPDATE mode.
D6-P088-0003 §4.2.7.1 [table_cell] NOTE 1: A read (MRR) to this register places the contents of OP[x:x] on DQ[y:y].
D6-P088-0004 §4.2.7.1 [table_cell] Any RV bits and unused DQ's shall be set to '0'.
D6-P088-0005 §4.2.7.1 [table_cell] See the section on MRR Operation.
D6-P088-0006 §4.2.7.1 [table_cell] NOTE 2: There are two physical registers assigned to each bit of this MR parameter, designated set point 0 and set point 1.
D6-P088-0008 §4.2.7.1 [table_cell] NOTE 3: Control of VREFCS during CSTM is referred to section 4.2.6 (CSTM)
D6-P088-0009 §4.2.7.1 [table_cell] NOTE 4: The time required for VREFCS to reach the set level depends on the step size from the current level to the new level.
D6-P090-0001 §4.2.7.1 [table_cell] NOTE 1: These values may be used for MR83[6:0] to set the Vref CS levels in the DDR6 SDRAM.
D6-P090-0002 §4.2.7.1 [table_cell] NOTE 2: Absolute CS VREF low level (%code * VDDQ) must be higher than or equal to 75mV for normal operation.
D6-P090-0003 §4.2.7.1 [table_cell] Vref error is not include this calculation.
D6-P090-0004 §4.2.7.1 [table_cell] NOTE 3: Absolute Vref CS high level (%code * VDDQ) must be lower than or equal to VrefCS_Max for nor mal operation.
D6-P090-0005 §4.2.7.1 [table_cell] Vref error is not included this calculation.
D6-P090-0006 §4.2.7.1 [table_cell] Refer to the CS Rx Mask table for the VrefCS_Max value.
D6-P090-0007 §4.2.7.1 [table_cell] NOTE 4: Vref CS codes from 0000000 to 0000100 are able to be used only for testing purpose not for normal operation.
D6-P090-0008 §4.2.7.1 [table_cell] Vref CS accuracy are not guaranteed from 0000000 to 0000100.
D6-P090-0009 §4.2.7.1 [table_cell] NOTE 5: The Vref CS codes from 1110010 to 1111111 are provided to support cases where VDDQ is lower than the typical value, enabling the absolute Vref value to reach 0.4V.
D6-P090-0010 §4.2.7.1 [table_cell] When VDDQ is at its typical voltage, codes with an absolute Vref value of higher than 0.4V cannot be used.
D6-P090-0011 §4.2.7.1 [table_cell] This restriction also applies during training operations.
D6-P090-0012 §4.2.7.1 [table_cell] NOTE 6: The default VREF CS is defined by CS ODT strap value.
D6-P091-0001 §4.2.8.1 [body] The command address bus must be trained before nominal frequency operation.
D6-P091-0002 §4.2.8.1 [body] Command Address Training Mode (CATM) is a method to facilitate the loopback of a logical combination of the sampled CA [5:0] signals.
D6-P091-0003 §4.2.8.1 [body] In this mode, the CK_t/CK_c differential clock (CK) is running and the Chip Select (CS) qualifies when the CK samples the CA signals.
D6-P091-0004 §4.2.8.1 [body] CATM is performed per-CA bit and per even/odd CK phase to adjust the per-bit skew and duty cycle distortion.
D6-P091-0005 §4.2.8.1 [body] The Host sends a long CA burst, or multiple CA bursts, with CS high, and the CA pattern is captured on each CS qualified differential CK rising edge.
D6-P091-0006 §4.2.8.1 [body] CATM has two (2) states which can be toggled by DQ[1:0]: Command (CMD) State and Train State.
D6-P091-0007 §4.2.8.1 [body] During Command State, only the MRUPDATE sequence is actively processed.
D6-P091-0008 §4.2.8.1 [body] During Train State, the burst or bursts of CA data qualified by CS high will be sampled on every differential CK rising edge and compared with a PRBS pattern in a logical computation to determine the CATM output results for feedback to the Host.
D6-P091-0009 §4.2.8.1 [body] The controller and DRAM use matching PRBS generators to compare per even/odd CK phase and per-CA bit in parallel, and feedback is returned on DQ2.
D6-P091-0010 §4.2.8.1 [body] The internal VREFCA default level is suitable for MRUPDATE operation during initialization, but the VREFCA must be trained to achieve suitable receiver voltage margin for normal operation.
D6-P091-0011 §4.2.8.1 [body] The Host follows the CATM procedure to center the internal VREFCA in the CA data eye and to adjust timing of the CA signals to maximize CA timing margin.
D6-P091-0012 §4.2.8.1 [body] While in CATM, no functional commands are executed.
D6-P091-0013 §4.2.8.1 [body] The functional command interface is restored only after exiting this mode.
D6-P091-0014 §4.2.8.1 [body] Prior to entering the CATM, the DQ swizzling discovery mode must be completed, and CSTM must have been performed to align the CK phase to achieve the maximum CS eye.
D6-P091-0015 §4.2.8.1 [body] CATM includes making changes to VREFCA.
D6-P091-0016 §4.2.8.1 [body] VREFCA update and stabilization time of tVREFCA_short or tVREF- CA_long are used, depending on the number of VREFCA steps being change.
D6-P091-0017 §4.2.8.1 [body] During CATM, per-pin VREFCA offset is expected to be disabled.
D6-P091-0018 §4.2.8.1 [body] If previously enabled, the Host needs to disable any per-pin VREFCA offset before performing CATM.
D6-P091-0019 §4.2.8.2 [body] Prior to entering CATM, MRUPDATE Mode must be enabled by the MCME command.
D6-P091-0020 §4.2.8.2 [body] VREFDQ, DQ/DQS ODT and DQ drive strength settings must be adjusted to values that allow slow speed operation.
D6-P091-0021 §4.2.8.2 [body] CATM is entered and exited through MR05 (Functional Mode Register).
D6-P091-0022 §4.2.8.2 [body] Issuing the entry or exit on either PSD (2p3 or 2p6 configurations) will apply the operation to both PSDs.
D6-P091-0023 §4.2.8.2 [body] Entry and Exit operations must wait for previous MRUP- DATE operations to complete.
D6-P092-0001 §4.2.8.3 [body] To enter CATM, the Host issues the MRUPDATE sequence to MR05[7:0]=035h with DQS differentially low prior to or during the 1st CS pulse of the MRUPDATE sequence.
D6-P092-0002 §4.2.8.3 [body] DQS must remain differentially low during CATM entry.
D6-P092-0003 §4.2.8.3 [body] Once tMRUPD_Delay has been satisfied, CATM has been entered in the Command State.
D6-P092-0016 §4.2.8.4 [body] To exit CATM, the Host issues the MRUPDATE sequence to MR05[7:0] = 00h in the Command State with DQS differentially low.
D6-P092-0017 §4.2.8.4 [body] During CATM Exit, DQS must remain differentially low until tMRUPD_Delay is satisfied.
D6-P092-0018 §4.2.8.4 [body] After tMRUPD_Delay, CATM mode is exited.
D6-P092-0032 §4.2.8.4 [body] Issuing an MRUPDATE sequence exit during the CMD state will exit both CATM and MRUPDATE modes within tMRUPD_ Delay.
D6-P092-0033 §4.2.8.5 [body] CATM uses the DQ bus, sampled by DQS, for function control.
D6-P092-0034 §4.2.8.5 [body] DQ[1:0] are input pins latched by DQS for CATM control logic.
D6-P092-0035 §4.2.8.5 [body] The Host is required to drive identical waveforms on the DQ[1:0] pins of both PSDs.
D6-P093-0001 §4.2.8.5 [body] During CATM, DQS_t/DQS_c (DQS) are input only to sample DQ[1:0] as the function code.
D6-P093-0002 §4.2.8.5 [body] DQS is required to be differentially low except while sampling DQ[1:0].
D6-P093-0003 §4.2.8.5 [body] CATM uses a four (4)eight (8) bit Function Code (Func- Code[37:0]), generated by four (4)eight (8) DQS rising pulses to sample DQ[1:0].
D6-P093-0004 §4.2.8.5 [body] Entering CATM internally resets the DQS rising pulse count to 0 to prepare for counting the four rising pulses.
D6-P093-0005 §4.2.8.5 [body] Each Function Code is the combined sample value of the 2nd, 4th, 6th, and 8th differential DQS rising edges (DQSR2, DQSR4, DQSR6, DQSR8, respectively) and the 4th differential DQS rising edge (DQSR4) as shown the CATM Function Code Mapping table.
D6-P093-0006 §4.2.8.5 [body] DQ2 is an output pin for the CATM sample results, per PSD.
D6-P093-0007 §4.2.8.5 [body] All other DQ pins, if present, are unused during CATM and are disabled.
D6-P093-0008 §4.2.8.5 [body] As shown in the figure below, the leading and trailing DQS differentially low timing (tDQSPreTrain and tDQSPost- Train, respectively) must be met before and after the DQS pulses.
D6-P093-0009 §4.2.8.5 [body] tFunc_Delay is the minimum time between Function Codes.
D6-P093-0010 §4.2.8.5 [body] Each DQS pulse must meet tDQSPWTrain on both high and low pulse.
D6-P094-0001 §4.2.8.5 [body] DQ[1:0] must meet setup and hold timing (tDSTrain and tDHTrain, respectively).
D6-P094-0002 §4.2.8.5 [body] The setup timing tDSTrain is defined as DQ[1:0] leading edge to DQSR2 and the hold timing tDHTrain is defined as DQSR2 to DQ[1:0] trailing edge.
D6-P094-0003 §4.2.8.5 [body] The same setup and hold timing requirements apply to DQSR4, DQSR6, and DQSR8.
D6-P094-0009 §4.2.8.5 [body] NOTE: CK and time breaks are not included.
D6-P094-0010 §4.2.8.6 [body] Command (CMD) State is the default state after CATM entry.
D6-P094-0011 §4.2.8.6 [body] CMD state is MRUPDATE mode enabled with CATM functions controlled by DQ[1:0] and DQS.
D6-P094-0012 §4.2.8.6 [body] Only the MRUPDATE sequence and DES command are valid in the CMD State.
D6-P094-0013 §4.2.8.6 [body] During CATM, the MRUPDATE sequence is only used to trigger functions and to change shadow register bits related to CATM settings such as VREFCA, CA ODT, CK ODT, DQ/DQS ODT, and DQ drive strength, as needed.
D6-P094-0014 §4.2.8.6 [body] Shadow register "Apply" is needed to update any of the normal mode register values for use during CATM CMD State.
D6-P094-0015 §4.2.8.6 [body] The VREFCA generator references the VREF CA Mode Registers settings while in CMD State.
D6-P094-0016 §4.2.8.6 [body] Train State is used to adjust the VREFCA shadow registers setting, perform training sequence sampling, and output training results on DQ2.
D6-P094-0017 §4.2.8.6 [body] Train State uses CK_ODT, CS_ODT, CA_ODT shadow register values, as well the VREF CS and VREF CA shadow register values.
D6-P094-0018 §4.2.8.6 [body] In Train State, CA is required to be low except when sending the training sequence.
D6-P094-0019 §4.2.8.6 [body] VREFCA change functions will only apply to the VREF CA shadow register, which updates the VREFCA generator since it is referencing the shadow register while in Train State.
D6-P094-0020 §4.2.8.6 [body] Upon completion of CATM, Func- Code[37:0]=48b'00000000b forces the return from Train State to CMD State, restoring use of the normal VREF CA Mode Register.
D6-P094-0021 §4.2.8.6 [body] The Host can apply the VREF CA shadow register value to VREF CA MR (MR1A84[6:0]) per training results during CMD State or after exiting CATM using the VREF CA and CA ODT Apply Functional Mode (MR05[xy]6[7:0]=08h).Shadow register values are not changed by entering or exiting CATM, by entering CMD State, or by entering Train State.
D6-P094-0022 §4.2.8.6 [body] Issuing the shadow register Apply during CMD State or after exiting CATM, switching from CMD State to Train State, and switching from Train State to CMD State, requires waiting the appropriate VREFCA update and stabilization delay which depends on the number of VREFCA steps being changed.
D6-P095-0001 §4.2.8.6 [body] DQ[1:0] are input pins during both CMD and Train States.
D6-P095-0002 §4.2.8.6 [body] DQ2 is off during CMD State.
D6-P095-0003 §4.2.8.6 [body] DQ2 remains off in Train State until the first time it is used as output pin during SMP function.
D6-P095-0004 §4.2.8.6 [body] DQS_t/DQS_c are input pins during both states.
D6-P095-0015 §4.2.8.6 [table_cell] Previously Set Value
D6-P095-0018 §4.2.8.6 [table_cell] Previously Set Value
D6-P095-0021 §4.2.8.6 [table_cell] Previously Set Value
D6-P095-0023 §4.2.8.6 [table_cell] Previously Set Value
D6-P095-0025 §4.2.8.6 [body] NOTE: 2: CK and time breaks are not included in the figure.
D6-P095-0026 §4.2.8.6 [body] The diagram is intended to serve as a reference for behavior rather than for timing purposes.
D6-P095-0027 §4.2.8.6 [body] NOTE: 3: MRUPDATE sequence to set VREFCA and CA ODT during CMD State is only for illustration purposes
D6-P095-0028 §4.2.8.6 [body] NOTE: 4: VREF CA and CA ODT registers are shown as updated to an appropriate previously set value as recommended in section XXX, Voltage Ramp and Device Initialization
D6-P095-0029 §4.2.8.7 [body] CATM uses a four (4)eight (8) bit Function Code (FuncCode[37:0]) to execute training functions.
D6-P095-0030 §4.2.8.7 [body] When a Func- Code[37:0] is received, the corresponding functions executes within tFunc_Delay.
D6-P095-0031 §4.2.8.7 [body] Some functions can only be used in certain CATM states.
D6-P095-0032 §4.2.8.7 [body] FuncCode[37:0] issued outside of an allowed state will be ignored.
D6-P095-0033 §4.2.8.7 [body] Any unused Func- Code[37:0] is RFU and will be ignored.
D6-P096-0001 §4.2.8.7 [table_cell] Force “CMD State”.
D6-P096-0002 §4.2.8.7 [table_cell] Use normal VREF and ODT MRs.
D6-P096-0003 §4.2.8.7 [table_cell] Force “Train State”.
D6-P096-0004 §4.2.8.7 [table_cell] Use VREF and ODT shadow registers.
D6-P096-0005 §4.2.8.7 [table_cell] Set VREF CA shadow register to FuncCode[6:0]
D6-P096-0017 §4.2.8.7 [body] FuncCode[37:0]=48b'00000000: "Force CMD State (FCS)" function.
D6-P096-0018 §4.2.8.7 [body] The FCS function may be issued in both the CMD State and the Train State.
D6-P096-0019 §4.2.8.7 [body] FuncCode[37:0]=48b'00001000: "Force Train State (FTS)" function.
D6-P096-0020 §4.2.8.7 [body] The FTS function forces Train State and switches from using normal VREF and ODT MRs to using the VREF and ODT shadow registers.
D6-P096-0021 §4.2.8.7 [body] The FTS function can be issued in both the CMD State and Train State.
D6-P096-0022 §4.2.8.7 [body] During Train State, VREFCA is controlled by the VREF CA shadow register.
D6-P096-0023 §4.2.8.7 [body] FuncCode[3:0]=4b'1001: “VREFCA Step Down (VSD)” function.
D6-P096-0024 §4.2.8.7 [body] The VSD function forces the VREF_CA shadow register value to decrease by one step.
D6-P096-0025 §4.2.8.7 [body] If the register is at the minimum value, the VREFCA will remain at the minimum value if issued additional VSD functions.
D6-P096-0026 §4.2.8.7 [body] The VSD function can be issued in either CMD or Train state.
D6-P097-0001 §4.2.8.7 [body] FuncCode[3:0]=4b'1010: “VREFCA Step Up (VSU)” function.
D6-P097-0002 §4.2.8.7 [body] The VSU function forces the VREF_CA shadow register value to increase by one step.
D6-P097-0003 §4.2.8.7 [body] If the register is at the maximum value, the VREFCA will remain at the maximum value if issued additional VSU functions.
D6-P097-0004 §4.2.8.7 [body] The VSU function can be issued in either CMD or Train state.
D6-P097-0005 §4.2.8.7 [body] FuncCode[7:0] = 8b'1VVVVVVV: "VREF Set Value (VSV)" function.
D6-P097-0006 §4.2.8.7 [body] The VSV function sets the VREF CA shadow register bits VREFCA[6:0] to FuncCode[6:0].
D6-P097-0007 §4.2.8.7 [body] Note: VREFCA does not use the VREF CA shadow register during the CMD state, so changes will not be applied until Train State is entered.
D6-P097-0008 §4.2.8.7 [body] Continuous VSD and VSU operation is possible if tFUNC_Delay between each operation is met.
D6-P097-0009 §4.2.8.7 [body] However, tThe Host must wait the required VREFCA update and settling delay after last VSU or VSD any VSV function.
D6-P097-0022 §4.2.8.7 [table_cell] Previously Set Value
D6-P097-0024 §4.2.8.7 [table_cell] Previously Set Value
D6-P097-0025 §4.2.8.7 [body] NOTE: 1:CK and time break are not included on purpose.
D6-P097-0026 §4.2.8.7 [body] The diagram is intended to serve as a reference for consecutive VSU/VSDVSV behavior and tVREFCA_short/long timing requirement.
D6-P097-0027 §4.2.8.7 [body] Any additional timing requirements not depicted in the figure must also be satisfied.
D6-P097-0028 §4.2.8.7 [body] NOTE: 2: VREF CA registers are shown initially updated to an appropriate previously set value as recommended in section XXX, Voltage Ramp and Device Initialization.
D6-P097-0029 §4.2.8.7 [body] FuncCode[37:0]=48b'00001111: “Sample (SMP)” function.
D6-P097-0030 §4.2.8.7 [body] The SMP function performs the following operations to prepare for CA sampling: reset the LFSR, reset the CATM internal training results to fail, force DQ2 high, and enable CS high qualified sampling of the CA burst or bursts.
D6-P097-0031 §4.2.8.7 [body] After the reset to CATM delay (tRST2CATM) is met, the (first) CA burst can be sampled.
D6-P097-0032 §4.2.8.7 [body] The Host sends the correct PRBS pattern on the CA bus, qualified by CS high.
D6-P097-0033 §4.2.8.7 [body] The first cycle of the CA burst with CS high must be sent by the Host corresponding to a phase 0° CK rising edge, as previously identified through CSTM.
D6-P097-0034 §4.2.8.7 [body] The DRAM will sample the CA burst on this CK differential rising edge and each subsequent differential rising edge of CK until CS is sampled low.
D6-P097-0035 §4.2.8.7 [body] The first phase 0° CK rising edge with CS low terminates the burst; only CA samples latched prior to this CK rising edge will be included in the burst pattern comparison.
D6-P097-0036 §4.2.8.7 [body] The Host may use any CA burst length that is a non-zero multiple of 4 nCK.
D6-P097-0037 §4.2.8.7 [body] The CA sampling may be broken into multiple CS high qualified CA bursts, with each burst separated by "gaps" in which CS is low.
D6-P097-0038 §4.2.8.7 [body] In this case, each CA burst length must be a multiple of 4nCK and must be sent corresponding to a phase 0° CK rising edge.
D6-P097-0039 §4.2.8.7 [body] The LFSR is not advanced during any CS low "gap" period.
D6-P098-0001 §4.2.8.7 [body] For each sample of the CA bus, the sampled value is compared against the expected value from the internal PRBS generator.
D6-P098-0002 §4.2.8.7 [body] Passing samples are recorded as '0' and failing samples recorded as '1'.
D6-P098-0003 §4.2.8.7 [body] These results are accumulated across the full all bursts per CA bit and even/odd phase using a logical ‘OR’ operation.
D6-P098-0004 §4.2.8.7 [body] Note, "even" phase corresponds to both 0° and 180º phases of CK; "odd" phase corresponds to both 90° and 270° phases of CK.
D6-P098-0005 §4.2.8.7 [body] This yields 10 separate feedback bits if CA5 is not enabled: 5 CA lanes x 2 even/odd phases.
D6-P098-0006 §4.2.8.7 [body] If CA5 is enabled, this increases to 12 separate feedback bits: 6 CA lanes x 2 even/odd phases.
D6-P098-0007 §4.2.8.7 [body] See the table below for the feedback sequence.
D6-P098-0008 §4.2.8.7 [body] DQS is required to be differentially low during the CA burst or bursts.
D6-P098-0009 §4.2.8.7 [body] After the (last) CA burst is terminated and tCATM2FBO is met, the DRAM is ready to output feedback results.
D6-P098-0010 §4.2.8.7 [body] After this point, whenever the DRAM receives FuncCode[37:0]=48b'00000101 it will execute the Feedback Output (FBO) function.
D6-P098-0011 §4.2.8.7 [body] On the first execution of the FBO function, the first feedback bit on DQ2 is CA0_E, with the results being available tADR after the last rising DQ edge of the FBO function.
D6-P098-0012 §4.2.8.7 [body] A feedback bit value of ‘0’ indicates the entire burst passed for that CA bit and phase.
D6-P098-0013 §4.2.8.7 [body] A feedback bit value of ‘1’ indicates a fail for that CA bit and phase at some point during the burst.
D6-P098-0014 §4.2.8.7 [body] On the next execution of the FBO function, DQ2 will output the next feedback bit, CA0_O, and so on for each iteration of the FBO function until the final feedback has been output.
D6-P098-0015 §4.2.8.7 [body] If additional FBO functions are executed after the final feedback bit has been output, the final feedback bit will continue to be output.
D6-P098-0016 §4.2.8.7 [body] The Host can reset the feedback results and the LFSR for the PRBS pattern by issuing the SMP function again, enabling a new CS qualified sampling of the CA bus.
D6-P099-0001 §4.2.8.7 [table_cell] NOTE 1: If CA5 is disabled, bit 9 will be the final feedback bit.
D6-P099-0002 §4.2.8.7 [table_cell] NOTE 2: Bits 10 and 11 will only be output if CA5 is enabled.
D6-P099-0003 §4.2.8.7 [table_cell] If CA5 is enabled, bit 11 will be the final feedback bit.
D6-P099-0020 §4.2.8.7 [body] NOTE: 1: CA burst of PRBS pattern shown for CA5 enabled.
D6-P099-0021 §4.2.8.7 [body] NOTE: 2: SMP and FBO function four cycle sequences shown compressed for illustration purposes.
D6-P099-0022 §4.2.8.7 [body] tRST2CATM and tCATM2FBO are measured from the last rising DQS edge of the respective function.
D6-P099-0023 §4.2.8.7 [body] NOTE: 3: Feedback results shown for CA5 enabled (12 values total, CA0_E through CA5_O).
D6-P100-0001 §4.2.8.7 [body] NOTE: 4: SMP and FBO functions are shown as a combined 8-bit FuncCode on DQ[1:0] with only the first and final DQS rising edges shown for readability.
D6-P100-0002 §4.2.8.7 [body] Please see the Example Timing for Functional Codes figure above for additional timing details.
D6-P100-0030 §4.2.8.7 [body] NOTE: 1: CA burst of PRBS pattern shown for CA5 enabled.
D6-P100-0031 §4.2.8.7 [body] NOTE: 2: SMP and FBO function four cycle sequences shown compressed for illustration purposes.
D6-P100-0032 §4.2.8.7 [body] tRST2CATM and tCATM2FBO are measured from the last rising DQS edge of the respective function.
D6-P100-0033 §4.2.8.7 [body] NOTE: 3: Feedback results shown for CA5 enabled (12 values total, CA0_E through CA5_O).
D6-P100-0034 §4.2.8.7 [body] NOTE: 4: SMP and FBO functions are shown as a combined 8-bit FuncCode on DQ[1:0] with only the first and final DQS rising edges shown for readability.
D6-P100-0035 §4.2.8.7 [body] Please see the Example Timing for Functional Codes figure above for additional timing details.
D6-P100-0036 §4.2.8.7 [body] Issuing MRUPDATE sequences or executing a new CATM function must wait for a previous operation to complete.
D6-P100-0037 §4.2.8.7 [body] The only exception is an MRUPDATE sequence for CATM Exit, which may be used to exit CATM during CMD State with DQS differentially low at least tFunc_Delay plus the required VREFCA update and settling delay after the last DQS differential rising edge.
D6-P100-0038 §4.2.8.7 [body] The following figure is an example of the sequence to switch to CMD State and exit CATM when DQS pulse count has been lost.
D6-P100-0039 §4.2.8.7 [body] The Host issues at least 816 DQS pulses with DQ[1:0]=00b, where each DQS pulse meets tFunc_Delay between rising edges.
D6-P100-0040 §4.2.8.7 [body] CATM exit command must meet tFunc_Delay plus the required VREFCA update and settling delay after the last DQS differential rising edge.
D6-P100-0041 §4.2.8.7 [body] CAs remain as "L", as the starting state may be the Train State.
D6-P100-0053 §4.2.8.8 [body] For CATM, the Host and the DRAM use a Pseudo Random Binary Sequence (PRBS) generated by a 24-bit Galois Linear Feedback Shift Register (LFSR).
D6-P100-0054 §4.2.8.8 [body] This sequence generator will be used on the Host to generate the bit stream for the CA burst.
D6-P100-0055 §4.2.8.8 [body] The DRAM will use the same sequence generator to generate the expected data and then compare
D6-P101-0001 §4.2.8.8 [body] the received data with the expected data to determine if the CA bits were transferred successfully from the Host to the DRAM.
D6-P101-0002 §4.2.8.8 [body] The feedback results from comparison per CA bit and CK phase will be sent back to the Host.
D6-P101-0003 §4.2.8.8 [body] The polynomial to be used for the 24-bit Galois LFSR is x^24 + x^22 + x^21 + x^19 + x^17 + x^14 + x^10 + x^4 + 1.
D6-P101-0004 §4.2.8.8 [body] The figures below show the implementation of the PRBS generator using a 24-bit shift register and XOR gates, as well as the mapping of LFSR bits to the CA bus.
D6-P101-0005 §4.2.8.8 [body] Error checking on the CA burst is performed in 4nCK units (corresponding to 20 bits if CA5 is disabled or 24 bits if CA5 is enabled).
D6-P101-0006 §4.2.8.8 [body] The LFSR updates to a new 24-bit value every 4nCK.
D6-P101-0007 §4.2.8.8 [body] Note that the same bits are mapped to CA[4:0] whether CA5 is enabled or not.
D6-P101-0095 §4.2.8.8 [body] The PRBS generator must be initialized with the same default seed to ensure the same pattern is generated on both the Host and DRAM.
D6-P101-0096 §4.2.8.8 [body] This seed is chosen to initialize the PRBS generator such that it generates an alternating 1 and 0 sequence on the CA bus.
D6-P101-0097 §4.2.8.8 [body] The initial 8 values seen on the CA bus when the PRBS generator is run with an example default seed of 24'h4894AC are shown in the table below, where L0 is the first data pattern driven from the host and the first PRBS state for comparison.
D6-P102-0001 §4.2.8.8 [body] The PRBS generator will start from the default seed value programmed in MRxa-MRxc MRD8-MRDA whenever the LFSR is reset by the SMP function.
D6-P102-0002 §4.2.8.9 [body] An example of the CATM sequence is described below:
D6-P102-0003 §4.2.8.9 [body] 1) If not in MRUPDATE mode, then use MCME to enter MRUPDATE mode.
D6-P102-0004 §4.2.8.9 [body] 2) If not previously completed, perform the DQ Swizzling Discovery Mode (DSDM) operation to identify the Host to DRAM DQ mapping.
D6-P102-0005 §4.2.8.9 [body] 3) If not previously completed, perform CS Training Mode to align all CK phases to achieve the maximum CS eye.
D6-P102-0006 §4.2.8.9 [body] 4) Set DQS differentially low.
D6-P102-0007 §4.2.8.9 [body] Enter CATM and wait tMRUPD_Delay.
D6-P102-0008 §4.2.8.9 [body] 5) Update the CA_ODT and VREF_CA Mode Register values, if required, and Apply.
D6-P102-0009 §4.2.8.9 [body] If required, update the CA ODT and VREF CA shadow register values and Apply to normal MR for use in CMD State.
D6-P102-0010 §4.2.8.9 [body] The VREF CA normal MR value must be valid in CMD State to allow the MRUPDATE sequence to execute properly.
D6-P102-0011 §4.2.8.9 [body] 6) Update the CA_ODT and VREF_CA shadow register values, if required.
D6-P102-0012 §4.2.8.9 [body] The VREFCA value must be valid to allow the MRUPDATE sequence to execute properly, and Train State will use the VREF_CA shadow register value.
D6-P102-0013 §4.2.8.9 [body] If required, update the CA ODT and VREF CA shadow register values for use in Train State.
D6-P102-0014 §4.2.8.9 [body] 7) Switch from CMD State to Train State.
D6-P102-0015 §4.2.8.9 [body] The shadow registers are being used.
D6-P102-0016 §4.2.8.9 [body] Wait for tFunc_Delay and VREFCA update and settling delay.
D6-P102-0017 §4.2.8.9 [body] 8) Update the VREFCA setting via the VSU or VSDVSV function.
D6-P102-0018 §4.2.8.9 [body] Wait VREFCA update and settling delay after the last VSU/VSDVSV.
D6-P102-0019 §4.2.8.9 [body] 9) Issue the SMP function to prepare for sampling.
D6-P102-0020 §4.2.8.9 [body] After tRST2CATM, The DRAM is ready for CA sampling.
D6-P102-0021 §4.2.8.9 [body] 10) Perform CA training (VREFCA, CA timing): a) The Host sends the CA burst or bursts of the PRBS pattern with CS high.
D6-P102-0022 §4.2.8.9 [body] b) The CA burst pattern(s) is(are) sampled on each CS high qualified CK differential rising edge, comparing each bit with the expected PRBS pattern and accumulating the feedback results per CA bit and even/odd CK phase using a logical ‘OR’ operation.
D6-P102-0023 §4.2.8.9 [body] A resulting value of ‘1’ indicates a fail while a ‘0’ indicates a
D6-P103-0001 §4.2.8.9 [body] Note: "even" phase corresponds to both 0° and 180º phases of CK; "odd" phase corresponds to both 90° and 270° phases of CK.
D6-P103-0003 §4.2.8.9 [body] c) After terminating the (last) burst and waiting tCATM2FBO, issue the FBO function repeatedly to output each feedback bit successively on DQ2.
D6-P103-0004 §4.2.8.9 [body] d) Calculate the full training results.
D6-P103-0005 §4.2.8.9 [body] When the results for all CA bits and CK phases are ‘0’ for both PSDs, the training is considered a pass.
D6-P103-0006 §4.2.8.9 [body] 11) Repeat step 8 to 10 as necessary to complete the first round of CA training.
D6-P103-0007 §4.2.8.9 [body] 12) Use the FCS command to switch to the CMD State, wait tFUNC_Delay and the VREFCA update and settling delay.
D6-P103-0009 §4.2.8.9 [body] After tMRUPD_Delay, CA is “trained”.
D6-P103-0012 §4.2.8.9 [table_cell] NOTE: 1: tVREFCA_long is for a change in VREFCA voltage of at least two steps.
D6-P103-0013 §4.2.8.9 [table_cell] NOTE: 2: tVREFCA_short is for a change in VREFCA voltage of a single step.
D6-P104-0001 §4.2.9.1 [body] Rx CK/DQS/CA/CS in DDR6 shall have a Continuous-Time Linear Equalizer (CTLE) to compensate channel loss for high frequency operation by providing increased gain at higher frequencies, offering a simple and effective solution for DDR6.
D6-P104-0002 §4.2.9.1 [body] Rx CK/DQS/CA/CS CTLE training shall be performed after CSTM and CATM sequence.
D6-P104-0003 §4.2.9.2 [table_cell] NOTE 1: Rx CTLE is an optional feature in DDR6.
D6-P104-0004 §4.2.9.2 [table_cell] MR1D[0] indicates host whether Rx CTLE is supported or not
D6-P104-0005 §4.2.9.2 [table_cell] NOTE 2: Refer to the vendor data sheets for more information regarding Rx CTLE Control Settings.
D6-P104-0006 §4.2.9.2 [table_cell] NOTE 3: Since CTLE circuits cannot be typically by-passed, a disable option is not provided.
D6-P104-0007 §4.2.9.2 [table_cell] The optimized setting will be given by a vendor, which should be noted that the settings are not specifically linear in relationship to the given setting, so the host can walk through all the provided options and use the setting that works best in their environment.
D6-P105-0001 §4.2.9.3 [table_cell] NOTE 1: Rx CTLE is an optional feature in DDR6.
D6-P105-0002 §4.2.9.3 [table_cell] MR1D[0] indicates host whether Rx CTLE is supported or not
D6-P105-0003 §4.2.9.3 [table_cell] NOTE 2: Refer to the vendor data sheets for more information regarding Rx CTLE Control Settings.
D6-P105-0004 §4.2.9.3 [table_cell] NOTE 3: Since CTLE circuits cannot be typically by-passed, a disable option is not provided.
D6-P105-0005 §4.2.9.3 [table_cell] The optimized setting will be given by a vendor, which should be noted that the settings are not specifically linear in relationship to the given setting, so the host can walk through all the provided options and use the setting that works best in their environment.
D6-P105-0006 §4.2.9.4 [table_cell] MR1D[0]: RX_CTLE_SUPPORT Rx CTLE support indicator 0: Not supported 1: Supported
D6-P105-0007 §4.2.9.4 [table_cell] NOTE 1: Rx CTLE is an optional feature in DDR6.
D6-P105-0009 §4.2.9.4 [table_cell] MR1D[0] indicates host whether Rx CTLE is supported or not.
D6-P106-0001 §4.2.10 [body] DDR6 adopts Per-pin VREFCA training to compensate for CA input offset due to SOC transmitter mismatch, DRAM termination mismatch, and DRAM CA path mismatch.
D6-P106-0002 §4.2.10 [body] Per-pin VREFCA training allows host to program the reference VREF level to an optimum point for each single-ended CA receiver.
D6-P106-0003 §4.2.10 [body] Per-pin VREFCA training is performed after CATM exit.
D6-P106-0004 §4.2.10 [body] The offset levels among optimized VREFCA levels for each CA pin, which is obtained from VREFCA training in CATM, shall be applied as the per-pin VREFCA offset.
D6-P107-0001 §4.2.10.1 [table_cell] NOTE 1: This register controls the VREFCA levels for Frequency-Set-Point[0]
D6-P107-0002 §4.2.10.1 [table_cell] NOTE 2: A read (MRR) to this register places the contents of OP[x:x] on DQ[x:x].
D6-P107-0003 §4.2.10.1 [table_cell] Any RFU bits and unused DQs will be read 0B.
D6-P107-0005 §4.2.10.1 [table_cell] NOTE 3: There are two physical registers assigned to each bit of this MR parameter, designated set point 0 and set point 1.
D6-P107-0007 §4.2.10.1 [table_cell] NOTE 4: The time required for VREFCA to reach the set level depends on the step size from the current level to the new level.
D6-P107-0009 §4.2.10.1 [table_cell] NOTE 5: VREFCA can be adjusted by either MRW(MR84[6:0]) in normal mode (not MRUPDATE mode) or VREF_CA shadow register with VREFCA Apply Functional Mode (MR06[3]=1B) in MRUPDATE mode.
D6-P107-0010 §4.2.10.1 [table_cell] NOTE 6: Control of VREFCA during CATM is referred to section 4.2.8 (CATM)
D6-P107-0011 §4.2.10.1 [table_cell] NOTE 7: Refer to Table 2 for the detailed VREFCA setting values.
D6-P109-0001 §4.2.10.1 [table_cell] NOTE 1: These values may be used for MR1A84[6:0] to set the VREFCA levels in the DDR6 SDRAM.
D6-P109-0002 §4.2.10.1 [table_cell] NOTE 2: Absolute VREFCA low level (%code * VDDQ) must be higher than or equal to 75mV for normal operation.
D6-P109-0003 §4.2.10.1 [table_cell] VREFCA error is not included this calculation.
D6-P109-0004 §4.2.10.1 [table_cell] NOTE 3: Absolute VREFCA high level (%code * VDDQ) must be lower than or equal to VREFCA max, which refers to values in Table xx (CA Rx mask specification), for normal operation.
D6-P109-0005 §4.2.10.1 [table_cell] VREFCA error is not included this calculation.
D6-P109-0006 §4.2.10.1 [table_cell] NOTE 4: VREFCA codes from 0000000B to 0000100B are able to be used only for testing purpose not for normal operation.
D6-P109-0007 §4.2.10.1 [table_cell] VREFCA accuracy are not guaranteed from 0000000B to 0000100B.
D6-P109-0008 §4.2.10.1 [table_cell] NOTE 5: The VREFCA codes from 1110010B to 1111111B are provided to support cases where VDDQ is lower than the typical value, enabling the absolute VREFCA value to reach 0.4V.When VDDQ is at its typical voltage, codes with an absolute VREFCA value of higher than 0.4V cannot be used.
D6-P109-0009 §4.2.10.1 [table_cell] This restriction also applies during training operations.
D6-P109-0010 §4.2.10.1 [table_cell] NOTE 6: The default VREFCA is defined by CA ODT strap value.
D6-P109-0011 §4.2.10.2 [body] MR03[3] has 1-bit to support Pseudo-Split Die (PSD) IndicatorMR80[0] indicates the physical PSD, and Per-pin VREFCA training is performed per each PSD independently.
D6-P113-0001 §4.2.11.1 [body] The DDR6 SDRAM supports a ‘write leveling’ feature to allow the controller to compensate CK to WRITE DQS timing skew.
D6-P113-0002 §4.2.11.1 [body] The DDR6 Write Leveling Training also allows for an unmatched path between CK and DQS within the DRAM, and thus supports an internal Write Leveling Training flow to account for the difference in internal delays.
D6-P113-0003 §4.2.11.1 [body] The DDR6 DRAM also provides a programmable timing in its write logic, controlled by the Write Leveling Internal Cycle Alignment (WICA) mode register (MRy), which provides a means for improved performance of the device’s receiver.
D6-P113-0004 §4.2.11.1 [body] The proper setting of this register shall be determined by the memory controller, either as described in the sections below, or by some other means.
D6-P113-0005 §4.2.11.1 [body] This delay setting is specific to each DRAM, its Write Preamble setting, and the operating frequency being used.
D6-P113-0006 §4.2.11.1 [body] PDA select ID is allowed while in WL training mode to program settings specific to each DRAM.
D6-P113-0007 §4.2.11.1 [body] Once the proper setting has been determined for a given Write preamble setting, and operating frequency, that setting would be subsequently restored to the DRAM after reset, power-cycle, or return to a previously used operating frequency.
D6-P113-0008 §4.2.11.1 [body] The memory controller can use the ‘write leveling’ feature and feedback from the DDR6 DRAM to adjust the controller’s differential DQS (DQS_t - DQS_c) output delay to align to the phase and cycle that corresponds to the Write Latency (WL) delay after the WRITE command.
D6-P113-0009 §4.2.11.1 [body] The memory controller involved in the leveling must have an adjustable delay setting on the DQS pins to align the rising edge of differential DQS with the timing at the receiver that is the pin-level Write Latency (External Write Leveling Training), and to align DQS with the internal DRAM Write Latency timing point (Write Leveling Internal Cycle Alignment).
D6-P113-0010 §4.2.11.1 [body] The internal DRAM Write Latency timing point may be skewed from the pin-level Write Latency timing point.
D6-P113-0011 §4.2.11.1 [body] The host will minimize this skew (tDQSoffset) through the Write Leveling Training flow.
D6-P113-0012 §4.2.11.1 [body] Since the system and DIMM delays vary, the DRAM will support the ability for the host to align the DQS timings with a pin-level Write Latency differential CK (CK_t-CK_c) edge.
D6-P113-0013 §4.2.11.1 [body] This alignment is referred to as External Write Leveling.
D6-P113-0014 §4.2.11.1 [body] Once the DQS host timings are aligned at the DRAM Write Latency timing, the internal DRAM timings are optimized for lowest power and internal delay.
D6-P113-0015 §4.2.11.1 [body] This is accomplished when the host enables the Internal Write Timing setting in MRx.
D6-P113-0016 §4.2.11.1 [body] In order to compensate for the difference in delay, the host will execute an Internal Write Leveling Training sequence, which includes sweeping the Write Leveling Internal Cycle Alignment (WICA) settings in MRy (Write Leveling Internal Cycle Alignment Operation) and then finalizing the differential DQS phase and offset (Write Leveling Internal Phase Alignment and Final Host DQS Timing Operation).
D6-P113-0017 §4.2.11.1 [body] During Write Leveling Training (both External and Internal), the DQS pattern shall include the full programmed write preamble and only the first toggle of the normal data burst sequence.
D6-P113-0018 §4.2.11.1 [body] The DRAM will sample the Internal Write Leveling Pulse relative to the first DQS toggle of the data burst sequence (last rising differential DQS edge sent by the host) and asynchronously feed back the result of this sample on the DQ bus, transitioning between tWLOmin and tWLOmax.
D6-P113-0019 §4.2.11.1 [body] If the DQ bus output is LOW at tWLOmax, the Internal Write Leveling Pulse signal was sampled while it was deasserted (LOW).
D6-P113-0020 §4.2.11.1 [body] Likewise, if the DQ bus output is HIGH at tWLOmax, the Internal Write Leveling Pulse signal was sampled while it was asserted (HIGH).
D6-P113-0021 §4.2.11.1 [body] The sampled feedback state will remain on the DQ bus until a subsequent Write Leveling sample changes the state, or until Write Leveling Training is exited.
D6-P113-0022 §4.2.11.1 [body] The Internal Write Leveling Pulse is generated in response to a WRITE command, and held statically low otherwise.
D6-P113-0023 §4.2.11.1 [body] To perform the Write Leveling training, the controller repeatedly sends a WRITE command, delays DQS, and monitors the DQ feedback after tWLOmax until a transition from 0 to 1 is detected, indicating alignment with the Internal Write Leveling Pulse.
D6-P113-0024 §4.2.11.1 [body] During Internal Write Leveling Training flows, the host will apply an offset to the starting point or the final setting of the DQS signals.
D6-P113-0025 §4.2.11.1 [body] The offsets are referred to as WL_ADJ_start and WL_ADJ_end.
D6-P113-0026 §4.2.11.1 [body] This will minimize the tDQSoffset variation across different DRAM devices.
D6-P113-0027 §4.2.11.1 [body] The WL_ADJ_start and WL_ADJ_end values depend on the tWPRE setting.
D6-P114-0001 §4.2.11.1 [body] When External and Internal Write Leveling Training flows are complete and the final WL_ADJ_end offset has been applied to the DQS timings, the DQS is phase aligned and cycle aligned for write operations.
D6-P114-0002 §4.2.11.1 [body] During the training sequence, the DRAM in Write Leveling training mode will apply ODT to the strobes in the same P R O P O S E D DDR6 Write Leveling Item # 1897.67 Page 3 of 8 way as for functional operation.
D6-P114-0003 §4.2.11.1 [body] All non-target ranks (which will not be in Write Leveling Training Mode) will apply ODT as defined for functional operation.
D6-P114-0004 §4.2.11.1 [body] Prior to executing the DDR6 Write Leveling Training Flow, the DRAM tWPRE value must be configured to the functional operation setting.
D6-P114-0005 §4.2.11.1 [body] NOTE: DQS ODT is based on a DQS PARK Mode and is not enabled and disabled with DQ ODT timings.
D6-P114-0006 §4.2.11.2 [body] The MR fields for Write Leveling Training, Internal Write Timing, and Write Leveling Internal Cycle Alignment are part of MRx and MRy.
D6-P114-0007 §4.2.11.2 [body] To enter Write Leveling Training Mode, the controller shall program MRx:OP[a]=1.
D6-P114-0008 §4.2.11.2 [body] Likewise, to exit Write Leveling Training Mode, the controller will program MRx:OP[a]=0.
D6-P114-0009 §4.2.11.2 [body] For pseudo split die, the Write Leveling internal cycle alignment may be performed on either pseudo split die.
D6-P114-0010 §4.2.11.2 [body] Once leveling is completed on the selected pseudo split die, the host shall apply the resulting WICA training values into the WICA mode register shared by both pseudo split dies, ensuring consistent alignment within a single die.
D6-P114-0011 §4.2.11.2 [body] The Internal Write Timing, once enabled (MRx:OP[b]=1), shall remain enabled through the Internal Write Leveling Training flow and for functional operation.
D6-P114-0012 §4.2.11.2 [body] The host is responsible for incrementing the Write Leveling Internal Cycle Alignment setting until the Internal Write Leveling Pulse is pulled early enough to align with the differential DQS signal that was previously aligned with the pin-level Write Latency timing.
D6-P114-0013 §4.2.11.2 [body] The Write Leveling Internal Cycle Alignment setting only applies when the Internal Write Timing is enabled.
D6-P114-0014 §4.2.11.3 [body] When Write Leveling Mode is enabled (MRx:OP[a]=1) and the Internal Write Timing is disabled (MRx:OP[b]=0), the DRAM will have an Internal Write Leveling pulse that indicates how the DQS signals shall be aligned to match the pin-level Write Latency timings.
D6-P114-0015 §4.2.11.3 [body] The rising edge of the Internal Write Leveling Pulse will align to the rising edge of the differential CK signal that coincides with Write Latency (WL), as shown in the following diagrams.
D6-P114-0016 §4.2.11.3 [body] The differential DQS signals driven by the controller during leveling mode must be terminated by the DRAM based on ranks populated.
D6-P114-0017 §4.2.11.3 [body] Similarly, the DQ bus driven by the DRAM must also be terminated at the controller.
D6-P114-0018 §4.2.11.3 [body] All data bits shall carry the leveling feedback to the controller across the DRAM configurations 2p3, 2p6, 1p6 and 1p12.
D6-P114-0019 §4.2.11.3 [body] The following diagram shows the timing sequence to enter Write Leveling Training Mode, operation during Write Leveling Training Mode (with Internal Write Timings disabled), and the timing sequence to exit Write Leveling Training mode.
D6-P114-0020 §4.2.11.3 [body] An MRW command is sent to enable Write Leveling Training Mode.
D6-P114-0021 §4.2.11.3 [body] Prior to sending the MRW command to enable Write Leveling Training Mode, the host memory controller must drive the DQS signals differentially low.
D6-P114-0022 §4.2.11.3 [body] After tWLPEN time, the controller can send a WRITE command, followed by strobe pulses.
D6-P114-0023 §4.2.11.3 [body] The DQS signals shall always drive differentially low, except during the WRITE preamble and burst strobe sequence.
D6-P114-0024 §4.2.11.3 [body] There is no restriction as to how early the strobe pulses are sent, so long as they are no earlier than WL/2 after the WRITE command.
D6-P114-0025 §4.2.11.3 [body] The DRAM will sample the Internal Write Leveling Pulse relative to the first DQS toggle of the data burst sequence and asynchronously feed back the result of this sample on the DQ bus, transitioning between tWLOmin and tWLOmax.
D6-P114-0026 §4.2.11.3 [body] While in Write Leveling Training mode, the host controller may send ACT and PRE commands.
D6-P114-0027 §4.2.11.3 [body] The DRAM will ignore these commands.
D6-P114-0028 §4.2.11.3 [body] The address associated with the ACT and the WRITE commands may be any value.
D6-P115-0001 §4.2.11.3 [body] the Internal Write Leveling Pulse alignment during External Write Leveling Training, and the DQ sample feedback timing.
D6-P115-0038 §4.2.11.3 [body] The Memory controller initiates Write Leveling mode of all DRAMs in a rank by setting MRx:OP[a]=1.
D6-P115-0039 §4.2.11.3 [body] When entering write leveling mode, the DQ pins are in undefined driving mode.
D6-P115-0040 §4.2.11.3 [body] Since the controller levels one rank at a time, all non-target ranks will set Write Leveling Mode to disabled.
D6-P115-0041 §4.2.11.3 [body] The Controller may assert non-target ODT through the normal WRITE command protocol.
D6-P115-0042 §4.2.11.3 [body] DQS_RTT_PARK termination will apply to the DQS_t and DQS_c signals.
D6-P115-0043 §4.2.11.3 [body] The Controller shall drive DQS differentially LOW prior to sending the MRW command to enable Write Leveling Training Mode.
D6-P115-0044 §4.2.11.3 [body] The WRITE command must occur after a delay of tWLPEN relative to when the MRW enabled Write Leveling Training Mode.
D6-P115-0045 §4.2.11.3 [body] The DRAM will sample the Internal Write Leveling Pulse and asynchronously feed back the result of this sample on the DQ bus, transitioning between tWLOmin and tWLOmax.
D6-P115-0046 §4.2.11.3 [body] The controller can sample the state of any DQ bit after tWLOmax.
D6-P115-0047 §4.2.11.3 [body] Based on the sampled state, the controller decides to increment or decrement the DQS delay setting and launches the next WRITE command with associated DQS_t/DQS_c pulse (or pulse sequence) after some time, which is controller dependent.
D6-P115-0048 §4.2.11.3 [body] Once a 0 to 1 transition on the DQ bus is detected, the controller locks DQS delay setting and external write leveling is achieved for the device.
D6-P115-0049 §4.2.11.3 [body] Write Leveling Training may be executed by issuing consecutive Write commands after entering Write Leveling Mode has been enabled (no requirement to exit and reenter between each Write command).
D6-P115-0050 §4.2.11.3 [body] The minimum Write to Write command spacing during Write Leveling Mode is tWL_Write
D6-P116-0001 §4.2.11.3 [body] and is defined as “max(WL, last DQS differential toggle) + tWLO(max)+ 2nCK”.
D6-P116-0002 §4.2.11.3 [body] An example of consecutive Write commands given during Write Leveling Training is shown in the following figure:
D6-P116-0020 §4.2.11.4 [body] After the external Write Leveling Training step is completed, and after the host DQS signals have been aligned to the pin-level Write Latency timing, the host will apply a negative offset (WL_ADJ_start) to the DQS timing.
D6-P116-0021 §4.2.11.4 [body] The WL_ADJ_start offset moves the DQS timing prior to the Internal Write Leveling Pulse (causing LOW observed on the DQ pins), and it is dependent on the tWPRE setting (shown in table below).
D6-P116-0022 §4.2.11.4 [body] This will be a reference point for aligning the Internal Write Leveling Pulse via the WICA settings in MRy.
D6-P116-0023 §4.2.11.4 [body] The Internal Cycle Alignment training begins when an MRW command is issued to enable Internal Write Timing in MRx (keeping OP[a]=1 and setting MRx:OP[b]=1).
D6-P116-0024 §4.2.11.4 [body] The host sweeps the Write Leveling Internal Cycle Alignment (WICA) delay settings in MRy, starting with the 0tCK offset default.
D6-P116-0025 §4.2.11.4 [body] Increasing the WICA mode register offset setting speeds up the timing of the WRITE command by reducing the WRITE Command to Internal Write Pulse delay until the DQS is aligned with the internal Write Leveling Pulse assertion.
D6-P116-0026 §4.2.11.4 [body] Alignment results in a logic HIGH on the DQ pins.
D6-P117-0002 §4.2.11.4 [table_cell] Offset that the host applies to the DQS_t/DQS_c timing just after external Write Leveling -3.5 tCK WL_ADJ_start alignment to Write Latency and prior the internal cycle alignment training.
D6-P117-0004 §4.2.11.4 [table_cell] Offset that the host applies to the DQS_t/DQS_c timing after final phase alignment to the rising edge of the Write 4.0 tCK Leveling Internal WL_ADJ_end Pulse.
D6-P117-0006 §4.2.11.4 [table_cell] This will center the Write Leveling Internal Pulse rising edge within the preamble window.
D6-P117-0007 §4.2.11.4 [body] The timing diagrams in the following figure demonstrate Write Leveling Training operation when Internal Write Timings are enabled and the Internal Cycle Alignment is set such that the Internal Write Leveling Pulse has not yet reached the host DQS toggles.

D6-P118-0032 §4.2.11.4 [body] An example of Write Leveling Training internal Cycle Alignment executed by issuing consecutive Write commands after entering Write Leveling Mode has been enabled (no requirement to exit and reenter between each Write command) is shown in the following figure.
D6-P118-0086 §4.2.11.5 [body] Once the Internal Cycle Alignment is achieved, the host shall perform a final fine sweep of the DQS timings with Internal Write Timing enabled (MRx:OP[b]=1) to establish an even closer phase alignment to the rising edge of the
D6-P119-0001 §4.2.11.5 [body] internal Write Leveling Pulse.
D6-P119-0002 §4.2.11.5 [body] Once this is complete, the host will then add a positive offset of WL_ADJ_end (dependent upon tWPRE) to the DQS timings.
D6-P119-0003 §4.2.11.5 [body] This results in a tDQSoffset between -1tCK and +1tCK with measurement adjustments of tWLS/tWLH, placing the rising edge of the Internal Write Leveling Pulse within the preamble.
D6-P119-0004 §4.2.11.5 [body] After the WL_ADJ_end offset has been applied, the host will disable Write Leveling Training Mode (MRx:OP[a]=0).
D6-P119-0005 §4.2.11.5 [body] The Internal Write Timing will remain enabled and the Internal Cycle Alignment setting will retain the coarse setting that was trained.
D6-P119-0006 §4.2.11.5 [body] After every reset, the host must either restore these settings or execute the full Write Leveling Training flow.

D6-P120-0002 §4.2.11.6 [body] When the DRAM is in Write Leveling Mode, the DQS_c/DQS_t termination (DQS_RTT_PARK) and the Command and Control termination (RTT_CA, RTT_CK, RTT_CS) will be the same as for functional operation.
D6-P120-0003 §4.2.11.6 [body] The DQ signals will not be terminated in the DRAM, but instead will be driving values to the controller.
D6-P120-0004 §4.2.11.6 [body] The host controller will apply termination for the DQ signals.

D6-P121-0001 §4.2.12 [body] DDR6 supports per-pin VREFDQ offset based on a common VREFDQ setting.
D6-P121-0002 §4.2.12 [body] Per-pin VREFDQ can adjust input common mode level which is distributed to each pin.
D6-P123-0001 §4.2.12.2 [body] Per-pin VREFDQ offset is controlled independently per each Pseudo Split Die (PSD).
D6-P125-0001 §4.2.13.1 [body] DRAM shall support a Host Receiver Training function similar to Write Training Mode, to maintain robust link performance, especially in systems that employ multiple operating modes to enhance I/O scalability and reduce training time.
D6-P125-0002 §4.2.13.1 [body] This DRAM feature enables the host to perform receiver data training including internal receiver drift compensation and maintain optimal signal integrity throughout system operation.
D6-P125-0003 §4.2.13.2 [body] HRTM can select the pattern type between user defined pattern vs PRBS with MR setting (MRDD).
D6-P125-0004 §4.2.13.2 [body] The PRBS mode provides an independent (different pattern) per DQ lane using a 24-bit Fibonacci LFSR.
D6-P125-0005 §4.2.13.2 [body] One 24-bit F-LFSR generator will support x6 (max) DQ lanes.
D6-P125-0006 §4.2.13.2 [body] The polynomial for the Fibonacci LFSR is x^24 + x^20 + x^11+ x^5 + 1.
D6-P125-0007 §4.2.13.2 [body] For the Host DQ Receiver drift improvements, the DRAM shall drive user defined pattern and/or LFSR pattern during bus idle time.
D6-P125-0008 §4.2.13.2 [body] Figure 1 and Figure 2 show the details of left shift Fibonacci LFSR hardware and 24-bit PRBS pattern.
D6-P125-0014 §4.2.13.3 [body] To enter Host Receiver Training Mode (HRT), the host memory controller shall issue a Mode Register Write (MRW) command targeting MR05 OP[4:0] = 10010B, with the HRT Mode entry opcode.
D6-P125-0015 §4.2.13.3 [body] When the DRAM is in Receiver Training mode, commands are still actively processed.
D6-P125-0016 §4.2.13.3 [body] To exit HRT Mode, the host memory controller shall issue an MRW command to MR5 OP[7:0] = 00000000B, with the HRT Mode Exit opcode.
D6-P125-0017 §4.2.13.4 [body] Host to configure mode register with read pattern or LFSR details and this training is entered by issuing an MRW command.
D6-P125-0018 §4.2.13.4 [body] Host to issue a READ command, which causes the DDR6 DRAM to drive the MR content depending on
D6-P126-0001 §4.2.13.4 [body] the mode register settings (LFSR vs Read pattern).
D6-P126-0002 §4.2.13.4 [body] The timing of data return and strobe sequence match a regular Read operation which supports back-to-back read the user read pattern / LFSR data.
D6-P126-0003 §4.2.13.4 [body] HRT mode is exited by issuing an MRW command.
D6-P126-0004 §4.2.13.4 [body] In this training mode, the only valid commands are READ and MRW.
D6-P126-0006 §4.2.13.4 [table_cell] 1b0: Disable 1b1: Reset to default both LFSR and 5b-Staging Flip flops (auto clears after resetting)

D6-P127-0002 §4.2.13.5 [body] DDR6 DRAM devices implement a Host Receiver Training (HRT) function that drives training patterns onto the DQ signal pins to the host and in case of Write Training Mode (WTM), it receives the LFSR pattern from the host on the DQ pins.
D6-P127-0003 §4.2.13.5 [body] The output pattern shall be sourced either from a user-defined pattern or from an internally generated Linear Feedback Shift Register (LFSR) sequence.
D6-P127-0004 §4.2.13.6 [body] An independent 24-bit Fibonacci LFSR generator provides patterns for up to 6 DQ lanes.
D6-P127-0005 §4.2.13.6 [body] The source of the DQ output pattern shall be selected via MRDD:OP[0], where this field selects between LFSR output and the user-defined read pattern resource.
D6-P127-0006 §4.2.13.6 [body] The MRDD[3] field shall be used to reset the LFSR seed value and associated staging flip-flops.
D6-P127-0007 §4.2.13.6 [body] Upon reset, the 5-bit staging flip-flops shall be initialized to their default value of 11111.
D6-P127-0008 §4.2.13.6 [body] MRD7 is used to assign Pattern Inversion per DQ lane.
D6-P127-0009 §4.2.13.6 [body] If MRDD[0] is set to 1b0, MRD8to MRDAcontains the user-defined pattern.
D6-P127-0010 §4.2.13.6 [body] If MRDD[0] is set to 1b1 and MRxx[2] is set to 1b0, then the training shall use LFSR pattern generator and the MRD8 to MRDA registers allow users to select LFSR Seed register UI23-0.
D6-P127-0011 §4.2.13.6 [body] These LFSR seed /user pattern, inversion registers are per PSD and common for both Write training mode and Host receiver training modes.
D6-P127-0012 §4.2.13.6 [body] NOTE: For the 2p3 configuration, only DQ[2:0] from the LFSR output shall be used; all remaining DQ bits shall be ignored.
D6-P127-0013 §4.2.13.6 [body] For the 1p6 configuration, only DQ[5:0] from PSD0 shall be used.
D6-P127-0014 §4.2.13.6 [body] For the 2p6 and 1p12 configurations, DQ[5:0] from PSD0 and DQ[11:6] from PSD1 shall be used.
D6-P129-0001 §4.2.13.6 [body] NOTE: If a per DQ lane based pattern registers are required, then replicate MRxy<2:0> per DQ lane
D6-P131-0001 §4.2.14.1 [body] DDR6 SDRAM provides Offset Calibration training to reduce an intrinsic offset of DQ Rx.
D6-P131-0002 §4.2.14.1 [body] It is required for the DRAM to meet its receiver eye mask spec and required to operate the training every power-up and initialization training sequence to cope with the change of SDRAM operating condition.
D6-P131-0003 §4.2.14.1 [body] Offset calibration is enabled by MR05[4:0]=10100 and this can be issued in either normal mode or MRUPDATE Mode.
D6-P131-0004 §4.2.14.1 [body] When the SDRAM starts the training, DQ channel, where all the DRAM dies sharing the DQ, can be floated or can be biased toward VSS.
D6-P131-0005 §4.2.14.1 [body] DQ ODTs shall be automatically enabled to values determined by the SDRAM to be suitable for proper operation after MR05[4:0]=10100 is issued for starting an Offset calibration training.
D6-P131-0006 §4.2.14.1 [body] Calibration will be performed by the DDR6 SDRAM automatically within the time interval, tOSCAL, and the calibration results will also be automatically updated within tOSCAL.
D6-P131-0007 §4.2.14.1 [body] In any case, DQ operations are disallowed during the DQ RX offset calibration.
D6-P131-0008 §4.2.14.1 [body] After calibration is done, offset calibration is disabled by MR05[4:0]=0h as this calibration is not self-clearing.
D6-P131-0009 §4.2.14.1 [body] To reset the offset calibration to a default value, the Rx offset reset MR bit (OCC RESET) needs to be asserted (MR13[7]=1).
D6-P131-0010 §4.2.14.1 [body] In any case, operations that initiate DQ operations are always disallowed during tMRD after MR13[7] asserted.
D6-P131-0011 §4.2.14.1 [body] The OCC RESET MR bit shall be reset to 0b by the DRAM after tMRD.
D6-P131-0012 §4.2.14.2 [body] Below is DQ RX Offset Calibration Training sequence.
D6-P131-0013 §4.2.14.2 [body] 1) Issue a Rx Offset Cal.
D6-P131-0014 §4.2.14.2 [body] Start (MR05[4:0]=10100) for starting a DQ RX Offset Calibration Training
D6-P131-0015 §4.2.14.2 [body] 2) Wait tOSCAL until the DDR6 SDRAM completes the Offset Calibration
D6-P131-0016 §4.2.14.2 [body] 3) Issue a Rx Offset Cal.
D6-P131-0017 §4.2.14.2 [body] Exit (MR05[4:0]=0h) for exiting a DQ RX Offset Calibration Training
D6-P131-0022 §4.2.14.2 [table_cell] (Offset Cal Start)
D6-P132-0001 §4.2.14.2 [table_cell] MRW Offset Cal.
D6-P133-0001 §4.2.15 [body] Read preamble training supports read leveling of the host receiver timings.
D6-P133-0002 §4.2.15 [body] This mode supports MRR transactions that access the Read Training Pattern and cannot be used during any other data transactions.
D6-P133-0003 §4.2.15 [body] Just like Read Training Pattern, Read Preamble Training needs to be entered with CRC disabled.
D6-P133-0004 §4.2.15 [body] Read preamble training changes the read strobe behavior such that the strobes are always driven by the DRAM, and only toggle during a MR programmed preamble, postamble and the actual burst of the read data.
D6-P133-0005 §4.2.15 [body] This mode enables the host to detect the timing of when the first data and associated strobe is returned after a read command.
D6-P133-0006 §4.2.15.1 [body] Read Preamble Training Mode is exited by setting MR05:OP[7:0] = 0.
D6-P133-0007 §4.2.15.1 [body] The DRAM enters Read Preamble Training Mode by setting MR05:OP[4:0] = 10011B using MRW command.
D6-P133-0008 §4.2.15.1 [body] Read Preamble Training should not be disturbed by an ACT command until the completion of the training.
D6-P133-0009 §4.2.15.2 [body] Once the DRAM is placed in Read Preamble Training Mode, the only data transactions supported are MRR commands.
D6-P133-0010 §4.2.15.2 [body] All non-data commands, such as MRW, are still supported in this mode.
D6-P133-0011 §4.2.15.2 [body] Once READ Preamble Training is enabled, the device will drive DQS_t LOW and DQS_c HIGH within tSDOn and remain at these levels until an MRR command is issued.
D6-P133-0012 §4.2.15.2 [body] Once the MRR command is issued to MRyz, the device will drive DQS_t/DQS_c after CL-tRPRE RL-tRPRE-(Read_D- QS_Pre-shift),where tRPRE is programmed in MR0C:OP[4:2] register, like a normal READ burst with the Read DQS pre-shift setting MR0C:OP[1:0] programmed in MR0C applied.
D6-P133-0013 §4.2.15.2 [body] During read preamble training, a programmed DQS preamble/postamble setting will be used.
D6-P133-0014 §4.2.15.2 [body] In response to the MRR to the designated Read Training Pattern Address, the device must also drive the DQ pattern as per the Read Pattern configuration while in this mode.
D6-P133-0015 §4.2.15.2 [body] The MRR commands may be sequenced to enable back to back bursts on the DQ bus.
D6-P133-0016 §4.2.15.2 [body] Read preamble training mode is exited within tSDOff after setting mode register MR05:OP[7:0] = 0.
D6-P133-0017 §4.2.15.2 [body] Figure 1 illustrates the timing of the strobe being driven differentially low after Read Preamble Training Mode is enabled.
D6-P133-0018 §4.2.15.2 [body] It includes the strobe timing sequence following a MRR command issued to access the Read Training Pattern.
D6-P133-0019 §4.2.15.2 [body] The sequence shows a programmed preamble/postamble consisting of 2tCK static-low followed by 2tCK toggling (MR0C: OP[4:2] = 001), and a programmed 0.5tCK postamble (MR0C: OP[7:6] = 00).
D6-P133-0020 §4.2.15.2 [body] Table 1 specifies the timing requirements for programmed preamble/postamble (2tck static low followed by 2tck toggle preamble and 0.5tck postamble) applicable to Read Preamble Training Mode.
D6-P135-0001 §4.2.16 [body] DDR6 SDRAM supports Mode Register-adjustable Read (RD) DCA to allow the memory controller to adjust the DRAM’s internally generated read clock tree and DQ duty cycle to compensate for systemic duty cycle errors of all DQS and DQs.
D6-P135-0002 §4.2.16 [body] The RD DQS DCA is located in the read clock tree (or equivalent place), following the clock synchronization phase correction scheme.
D6-P135-0003 §4.2.16 [body] The controller can adjust the duty cycle through all the DCA mode registers and can determine the optimal Mode Register setting for DCA in multiple different ways.
D6-P135-0004 §4.2.16 [body] In case of 4-phase internal clocks, for example, since QCLK(90˚)/IBCLK(180˚)/QBCLK(270˚) are adjusted based on ICLK(0˚), the controller can first confirm that the first BL is synchronized with ICLK(0˚) through clock sync., and then perform the full DCA training operation which needs to have an even number of MRR(or Read) - MRR(or Read) timing to avoid confusion whether the first BL is synchronized with ICLK(0˚) or IBCLK(180˚).
D6-P135-0005 §4.2.16.1 [body] The global DCA is applied for all DQS and DQs within the entire PSD of the DRAM die.
D6-P135-0006 §4.2.16.1 [body] The step range between the step 0 to 7(-7) is as follows.
D6-P135-0007 §4.2.16.1 [body] The difference of actual value between step N and step N+1 is defined to be within a spec range, since the variation of duty cycle by changing DCA code is not linear.
D6-P135-0012 §4.2.16.1 [table_cell] NOTE 1: These values are guaranteed by design
D6-P135-0013 §4.2.16.1 [table_cell] NOTE 2: Total range means the range from step 0 to step +7(-7)
D6-P136-0001 §4.2.16.2 [body] In case of the read clock tree used single/two-phase clock(s) scheme, the duty-cycle ratio of the DQS can be adjusted directly according to the internal clock(s) controlled by the DCA code.
D6-P136-0002 §4.2.16.2 [body] Note that tDQSCK is not changed by DCA code change.
D6-P136-0003 §4.2.16.2 [body] Using a 2-phase clock scheme, the rising edge of the 0° clock is the reference edge, while the 180° clock is adjusted based on 0° clock.
D6-P136-0004 §4.2.16.2 [body] The rising edge of 0° clock is for even burst bit data, and the rising edge of 180° clock is for odd burst bit data.
D6-P136-0005 §4.2.16.2 [body] The global DCA adjustment uses the “RD DCA for single/two-phase clock(s)” mode register bits, MR40:OP[7:4].
D6-P136-0006 §4.2.16.2 [body] A positive DCA adjustment results in a larger duty cycle ratio, while a negative DCA adjustment results in a smaller duty cycle ratio.
D6-P136-0007 §4.2.16.2 [body] In addition to the global DCA adjustment, a RD per-pin DCA adjustment allows an additional step range of -3 to +3, per DQS/DQ.
D6-P136-0008 §4.2.16.2 [body] The per-pin DCA adjustment is additive to the global DCA adjustment, as shown in Table 2.
D6-P136-0009 §4.2.16.2 [body] Like the global DCA adjustment, the actual step size for the per-pin DCA adjustment cannot be defined since the variation of duty cycle by changing DCA code is not linear, however the per-pin DCA adjustment will be approximately the same as the global DCA adjustment.
D6-P137-0001 §4.2.16.2 [body] Mode register OP bits associated with the IBCLK and QBCLK may not be supported on the DRAM with a 2-phase clock scheme.
D6-P138-0001 §4.2.16.3 [body] In case of the read clock tree used 4-phase clocks scheme, the even and odd duty-cycle ratio of the DQS can be respectively adjusted since the internal 4-phase clocks can be independently controlled by the DCA code.

D6-P152-0001 §4.2.17.1 [body] Initial DQ link training is critical with respect to the link performance with multiple modes to enhance IO scaling and training time in system.
D6-P152-0002 §4.2.17.1 [body] DDR6 DRAM requires pattern capability, better pattern type that increases robustness for signal integrity interactions.
D6-P152-0003 §4.2.17.1 [body] The Write Training Mode provides the ability to select two pattern types with and reports error count on a per-pin for system level optimization.
D6-P152-0004 §4.2.17.1 [body] The two pattern types are LFSR independent per-pin and user pattern.
D6-P152-0005 §4.2.17.2 [body] WTM can select the pattern type between clockuser patternvs LFSR seed with MR setting (MRDE).
D6-P152-0006 §4.2.17.2 [body] The LFSR mode provides an independent (different pattern) per lane using a single 24-bit Fibonacci LFSR per Pseudo Split Die (PSD).
D6-P152-0007 §4.2.17.2 [body] The user (Ex: Clock) pattern would operate at the DQ rate for another option for system optimization on drift terms like Rx DQS2DQ.
D6-P152-0008 §4.2.17.2 [body] The polynomial for the Fibonacci LFSR is x24 + x20 + x11+ x5 + 1.
D6-P152-0009 §4.2.17.2 [body] Each mode provides a per pin error count logging.
D6-P153-0001 §4.2.17.2 [body] Tap[Y]) produces Tap[Z] from same polynomial, whereas X and Y are usually < 24, Z is a large integer (1 to 224-1) and random looking across (X,Y) combos.

D6-P154-0004 §4.2.17.4 [body] To enter Write Training Mode (WTM), the host memory controller shall issue a Mode Register Write (MRW) command targeting MR05 OP[4:0] = 10001B, with the Write Training Mode entry opcode.
D6-P154-0005 §4.2.17.4 [body] When the DRAM is in Write Training mode, commands are still actively processed.
D6-P154-0006 §4.2.17.4 [body] To exit Write Training Mode, the host memory controller shall issue an MRW command to MR5 OP[7:0] = 00000000B, with the Write Training Mode Exit opcode.
