D6-P155-0003 §4.2.17.5 [body] The source of the DQ output pattern shall be selected via MRDE:OP[0], where this field selects between LFSR output and the user-defined pattern resource.
D6-P155-0004 §4.2.17.5 [body] An independent 24-bit Fibonacci LFSR generator provides patterns for up to 6 DQ lanes.
D6-P155-0005 §4.2.17.5 [body] The MRDE[3] field shall be used to reset the LFSR seed value and associated staging flip-flops.
D6-P155-0006 §4.2.17.5 [body] Upon reset, the 5-bit staging flip-flops shall be initialized to their default value of 11111.
D6-P155-0007 §4.2.17.6 [body] Each DQ lane shall implement phase error status registers to report WTM phase error information.
D6-P155-0008 §4.2.17.6 [body] DRAM shall allocate four Mode Registers (MRDF, MRE0, MRE1, and MRE2) for each DQ lane to report either consolidated even/odd phase errors or individual 4-phase errors, as illustrated in Figure 4.
D6-P155-0009 §4.2.17.6 [body] The WTM error reporting mode shall be selected through a Mode Register Write (MRW) to MRDE:OP[1], which determines whether the error status is reported as even/odd phase errors or as individual 4-phase errors.
D6-P155-0011 §4.2.17.6 [body] The DRAM shall consolidate phase error information as follows:
D6-P155-0012 §4.2.17.6 [body] • Even phase errors: MRDF and MRE0 are combined and reported through MRDF.
D6-P155-0013 §4.2.17.6 [body] • Odd phase errors: MRE1 and MRE2 are combined and reported through MRE1.
D6-P155-0014 §4.2.17.6 [body] Error status shall indicate the presence of any error in the following UIs:
D6-P156-0004 §4.2.17.6 [body] The DRAM shall report all four phase error registers (MRDF, MRE0, MRE1, and MRE2) independently.
D6-P156-0005 §4.2.17.6 [body] DRAM shall allocate 4 bits to report the error status for Ph0, Ph1, Ph2, and Ph3, respectively.
D6-P156-0006 §4.2.17.6 [body] Error status shall indicate the presence of any error in the following UIs:
D6-P157-0001 §4.2.18 [body] DDR6 SDRAM supports a mode-register-adjustable Write(WR) DCA feature that enables the memory controller to adjust the DRAM's internal WR DQS tree duty cycle, enhancing DRAM WR margin.
D6-P157-0002 §4.2.18 [body] The DQS is shared across different PSDs(Pseudo Split Die).
D6-P157-0003 §4.2.18 [body] Therefore, the DCA code is determined based on the optimal value for all DQs within the DRAM die.
D6-P157-0004 §4.2.18 [body] The memory controller can adjust the DQS duty cycle through the WR DCA mode registers and can determine the optimal Mode Register setting for WR DCA.
D6-P157-0005 §4.2.18 [body] This function adjusts the SDRAM internal DQS duty cycle (Static).
D6-P157-0006 §4.2.18.1 [body] The step range between the step 0 to 7(-7) is as follows.
D6-P157-0007 §4.2.18.1 [body] The difference of actual value between step N and step N+1 is defined to be within a spec range, since the variation of duty cycle by changing DCA code is not linear.
D6-P157-0012 §4.2.18.1 [table_cell] NOTE 1: These values are guaranteed by design
D6-P157-0013 §4.2.18.1 [table_cell] NOTE 2: Total range means the range from step 0 to step +7(-7)
D6-P157-0018 §4.2.18.2 [body] In case of the DQS tree used single/two-phase DQS scheme, the duty-cycle ratio of all DQS per device can be adjusted directly according to the internal DQS controlled by the DCA code.
D6-P157-0019 §4.2.18.2 [body] Using a 2-phase DQS scheme, the rising edge of the 0° internal DQS is the reference edge, while the 180° internal DQS is adjusted based on 0° internal DQS.
D6-P157-0020 §4.2.18.2 [body] The WR DCA adjustment uses the “DCA for single/two-phase DQS” mode register bits, MR3E:OP[7:4].
D6-P157-0021 §4.2.18.2 [body] A positive DCA adjustment results in a larger duty cycle ratio, while a negative DCA adjustment results in a smaller duty cycle ratio.
D6-P158-0001 §4.2.18.2 [body] Mode register OP bits associated with the IB-DQS and QB-DQS may not be supported on the DRAM with a 2-phase DQS scheme.
D6-P158-0007 §4.2.18.3 [body] In case of the DQS tree used 4-phase DQS scheme, the even and odd duty-cycle ratio of all DQS per device can be respectively adjusted since the internal 4-phase DQS can be independently controlled by the DCA code.
D6-P159-0002 §4.2.18.4 [table_cell] NOTE 2: These settings can only be applied if MR3E:OP[1:0] =01 or 10.
D6-P160-0002 §4.2.18.4 [table_cell] NOTE 2: These settings can only be applied if MR3E:OP[1:0] =10 (10: WR DCA supported for 4-phase internal DQS)
D6-P161-0001 §5 [body] DDR6 SDRAM state diagram provides a simplified illustration of allowed state transitions and the related commands to control them.
D6-P161-0002 §5 [body] For a complete definition of the device behavior, the information provided by the state diagram should be integrated with the truth tables and timing specification.
D6-P161-0003 §5 [body] The truth tables provide complementary information to the state diagram; they clarify the device behavior and the applied restrictions when considering the actual state of all banks.
D6-P162-0001 §5 [body] For the command definition, see section xx Initialization and Training, and section xx Command and Operation.
D6-P162-0023 §5 [body] See Sub-State Diagram
D6-P163-0014 §5 [body] NOTE: 1: From the Self-Refresh state the device can enter Power-Down, MRR and MRW states.
D6-P163-0015 §5 [body] NOTE: 2: In IDLE state, all banks are pre-charged.
D6-P163-0016 §5 [body] NOTE: 3: In the case of a MRW command to enter a training mode, the state machine will not automatically return to the IDLE state at the conclusion of training.
D6-P163-0018 §5 [body] NOTE: 4: This simplified State Diagram is intended to provide an overview of the possible state transitions and the commands to control them.
D6-P163-0019 §5 [body] In particular, situations involving more than one bank, the enabling or disabling of on-die termination, and some other events are not captured in full detail.
D6-P163-0020 §5 [body] NOTE: 5: States that have an "automatic return" and can be accessed from more than one prior state (e.g., MRW from either idle or Active states) will return to the state from when they were initiated (e.g., MRW from Idle will return to Idle).
D6-P163-0021 §5 [body] NOTE: 6: The RESET_n pin can be asserted from any state, and will cause the SDRAM to go to the Reset State.
D6-P163-0022 §5 [body] The diagram shows RESET state applied from the Power-On as an example, but the Diagram should not be construed as a restriction on RESET_n.
D6-P164-0001 §6.1 [body] Mode Registers (MRs) are arranged as Direct-Mapped Shared Mode Registers and Paged PSD Mode Registers.
D6-P164-0002 §6.1 [body] Direct-Mapped Shared MRs can be accessed (written to and read from) both PSD interfaces.
D6-P164-0003 §6.1 [body] Paged PSD MRs can only be accessed from the respective PSD interface in 2pX configurations.
D6-P164-0004 §6.1 [body] PSD MRs can be accessed using control bits in 1pX configurations, as described in this section, the MRUPDATE section, and the Command Truth Table.
D6-P164-0005 §6.1 [body] The Direct-Mapped Shared and Paged PSD Mode Register spaces contain up to 128 Mode Registers each, selectable by seven Mode Register Addresses, MRA[6:0].
D6-P164-0006 §6.1 [body] MRA[7] selects between the Direct-Mapped Shared and Paged PSD spaces, with MRA[7]=0 for Direct-Mapped Shared MRs and MRA[7]=1 for Page PSD MRs.
D6-P164-0007 §6.1 [body] The Paged Mode Register space may contain more than one page, which are selectable by MR00[1:0] if defined.
D6-P164-0008 §6.1 [body] The default page is zero (0) for both PSDs.
D6-P164-0009 §6.1 [body] Each Mode Register contains a byte-wide payload.
D6-P164-0010 §6.1 [body] All unused (RV) bits in a defined Mode Register and all undefined (RV) Mode Registers will be read as a zero (0).
D6-P164-0011 §6.2 [body] Mode registers can have the following attributes, which are shown in the mode register definition tables.
D6-P164-0012 §6.2 [table_cell] This Mode Register can be read or written by software.
D6-P165-0001 §6.2 [table_cell] This Mode Register can be read by software.
D6-P165-0002 §6.2 [table_cell] Writes (MRWs) have no effect.
D6-P165-0003 §6.2 [table_cell] These bits are reserved for future expansion.
D6-P165-0004 §6.2 [table_cell] They may not be physically implemented and shall read zero to ensure forward and backward compatibility.
D6-P165-0005 §6.2 [table_cell] This Mode Register is reset to the default only by power cycle, not Reset.
D6-P165-0006 §6.2 [table_cell] This attribute can be combined with RW.
D6-P165-0007 §6.2 [body] Mode Register definition tables must follow these rules to ensure they are machine-readable:
D6-P165-0008 §6.2 [body] • In the Attribute column, commas must be used to separate attributes.
D6-P165-0009 §6.2 [body] • In the Default column, “0” and “1” may be used for single-bit values.
D6-P165-0010 §6.2 [body] For all other widths, hex values must be used with an “h” suffix, as in “3h” or “1Ah”.
D6-P165-0011 §6.2 [body] • In the Description column, names of individual Mode Registers must be in all caps, using underscores in place of spaces, following the address, bit range, and a colon.
D6-P165-0012 §6.2 [body] • The address in the Description column must not use brackets around the page number, and the page number uses two digits with leading zero.
D6-P165-0013 §6.2 [body] This is the only place where these brackets are not used for the page number.
D6-P165-0014 §6.3 [body] Shared and PSD Mode Registers access differs based on DRAM configuration.
D6-P166-0001 §6.3 [body] For 2pX configurations, both PSDs can access the Shared MRs, however each PSD can only access its corresponding PSD MRs.
D6-P167-0001 §6.3 [body] For 1pX configurations, the enabled PSD can access the Shared MRs, as well as both physical PSD MRs using CA bit encoding in the MRW and MRR commands and in MRUPATE Mode.
D6-P168-0001 §6.4 [body] Functions or modes (MR Functional Modes) to be executed are defined by a decoded combination of MR05 [7:0] and MR06, as shown in the MR05 and MR06 description s.
D6-P168-0002 §6.4 [body] The target Functional Mode is entered or executed within tMRD after an MRW or the last cycle of an MRUPDATE sequence.
D6-P168-0003 §6.4 [body] The MR05 description will also indicate if a Functional Mode can only be enabled while in MRUPDATE Mode.
D6-P168-0004 §6.4 [body] Functional Modes may be self-clearing or may require to be explicitly disabled, as indicated in the MR05 description.
D6-P168-0005 §6.4 [body] MR05 and MR06 is a are RW register s, allowing the state of the function to be read by an MRR command, if the MRR command is permitted while the Functional Mode is executing.
D6-P168-0006 §6.4 [body] If a Functional Mode is self-clearing, the MRR will return to 00h upon completion.
D6-P168-0007 §6.4 [body] Setting MR05[7:0]=00h or MR06[7:0]=00h will disable a the respective Functional Mode, unless it is self-clearing.
D6-P168-0008 §6.4 [body] Unless explicitly stated that overlap of a self-clearing function by another function is permitted, only one Functional Mode may be entered/executed at a time.

D6-P257-0001 §6.8 [body] This DRAM Scratch Pad Mode register is used by the host controller to read back Control Words from the RCD.
D6-P257-0002 §6.8 [body] Control Words are the RCD equivalent of the DRAM Mode registers.
D6-P257-0003 §6.8 [body] The DRAM implements MR47 as a simple read/write register, writable via an MRW to address MR47 and readable via an MRR to address MR47.

D6-P258-0001 §7.1.1 [body] Operation or timing that is not specified is illegal, and after such an event and in order to guarantee proper operation, the device must be reset or power-cycled and then restarted through the specified initialization sequence before normal operation can continue.
D6-P261-0001 §7.1.1 [table_cell] NOTE 1: Commands are two (2), four (4) or six (6) clock cycles long in CA 1N mode.
D6-P261-0002 §7.1.1 [table_cell] They are defined by the states of CS of clocks pairs C0/C1, C2/C3, C4/C5, and by the states of CA[54:0] of clocks C0, C1, C2, C3, C4, C5.
D6-P261-0003 §7.1.1 [table_cell] CS is 2N pulse width and center aligned to clocks C0, C2 and C4.
D6-P261-0004 §7.1.1 [table_cell] CS is “Don’t Care” at clocks C1, C3, and C5.
D6-P261-0005 §7.1.1 [table_cell] In CA 2N mode, the CS shall operate identically to CA 1N mode and the command lengths shall be doubled relative to 1N mode, corresponding to four (4), eight (8) or twelve (12) clock cycles long.

D6-P261-0007 §7.1.1 [table_cell] NOTE 3: Bank Group addresses BG[3:0] and Bank address BA determine which bank is to be operated upon.

D6-P261-0010 §7.1.1 [table_cell] PSD=H indicates the given command shall be executed at the other PSD whose CAs are not connected to the host. "Ignore" is supported on 2p3 die configuration only for Read-9 and Partial Write operation and used when Ignore feature is enabled (MR0E[0]=1MRxx:OP[y]=1B).

D6-P262-0001 §7.1.2 [body] 2N Mode shall allow the system to provide increased setup and hold margin on the Command/Address (CA) bus.
D6-P262-0002 §7.1.2 [body] 1N Mode shall be the default operating mode for DDR6 SDRAM devices.
D6-P262-0003 §7.1.2 [body] MCME command is used to enter into multi-cycle CS MRUPDATE sequence.
D6-P262-0004 §7.1.2 [body] MRUPDATE sequence shall be used to change mode register MRxx:OP[x] to set 1N vs 2N mode of operation.
D6-P262-0005 §7.1.2 [body] In 1N Mode, the DDR6 CA interface shall support command lengths of 6 cycles, 4 cycles, and 2 cycles.
D6-P262-0006 §7.1.2 [body] In 2N Mode, the duration of each supported command shall be doubled relative to 1N Mode, corresponding to 12 cycles, 8 cycles, and 4 cycles, respectively.
D6-P262-0007 §7.1.2 [body] The Chip Select (CS) function shall operate identically in both 1N and 2N modes.
D6-P262-0008 §7.1.2 [body] The CS# pulse width shall be 2nCK in both modes.
D6-P263-0001 §7.2.1 [body] Implementation of an internal 4-phase clock requires synchronization (sync) to enable the Host to start commands aligned to an even rising edge of the differential CK_t/CK_c external clock (CK).
D6-P263-0002 §7.2.1 [body] Internally, the four clock phases, 0°, 90°, 180° and 270°, are represented by Q0, Q1, Q2 and Q3, respectively, where each internal phase period equals four external CK cycles.
D6-P263-0003 §7.2.1 [body] Commands are only registered on Q0 or Q2, with CS being 2N centered on Q0 or Q2, and the corresponding command/address (CA) bits registered per CK as needed based on the command duration.
D6-P263-0004 §7.2.1 [body] The Host is responsible for labeling these quadruples during the CK toggle initiation period of CSTM or CK Sync Operation, prior to issuing commands (exceptions noted in the following section).
D6-P263-0005 §7.2.1 [body] 4-cycle commands associated with data input or output (i.e., WRITE, READ, and MRR) can only start on Q0.
D6-P263-0006 §7.2.1 [body] The MCME command can be started on any internal clock phase.
D6-P263-0047 §7.2.2 [body] During operation, the internal clock phase must be synchronized to the Host clock phase to properly decode commands (this is the "normal" or "CK Sync" state).
D6-P263-0048 §7.2.2 [body] When the CK Sync state is off, DES and the MCME command are allowed, the MRUPDATE Mode sequence may be executed, and the CK Sync Operation may be performed.
D6-P263-0049 §7.2.3 [body] The CK Sync state is set to "off' by following conditions:
D6-P263-0053 §7.2.3 [body] Following an MCME command, the CK Sync Off Functional Mode is executed while in MRUPDATE Mode with MR05[7:0]=04h.
D6-P263-0054 §7.2.3 [body] After the tCKSync_Off delay is met, the CK Sync State will be “Sync Off”.
D6-P263-0055 §7.2.3 [body] The off state does not
D6-P264-0001 §7.2.3 [body] impact MRUPDATE Mode.
D6-P264-0002 §7.2.3 [body] CK Sync Off function is a self-clearing function once the CK Sync Off operation has been performed, allowing other MR functional modes to be executed.
D6-P264-0015 §7.2.3 [body] The CK Sync Off Functional Mode is executed from one PSD, but the Sync Off affects both PSDs.
D6-P264-0016 §7.2.3 [body] Once in Sync Off, either PSD can be used to perform the CK Sync Operation, however the "other" PSD must remain unused until tCKSync is satisfied.
D6-P264-0017 §7.2.4 [body] Any time the internal clock phase alignment with the Host clock phase is unknown, a clock synchronization operation is required before issuing commands.
D6-P264-0018 §7.2.4 [body] CSTM can be used to perform this alignment, however a shorter CK Sync Operation is also provided when only the phase alignment is needed, including following MRUPDATE Mode exit when a CK Sync Off was issued.
D6-P264-0019 §7.2.4 [body] The CK Sync Operation procedure is as follows:
D6-P264-0020 §7.2.4 [body] • If needed, enter MCME on either PSD.
D6-P264-0021 §7.2.4 [body] Issue CK Sync Off Functional Mode, MR05[7:0]=04h, and wait tCKSync_Off.
D6-P264-0022 §7.2.4 [body] Exit MUPDATE Mode.
D6-P264-0023 §7.2.4 [body] • Issue CA[5:0]=00h with CS low for tCKSync_CAPre to meet the CA bus stable at low time.
D6-P264-0024 §7.2.4 [body] • Issue CA[5:0]=00h with CS high for tCKSync_CSPre to ensure CS is latched to release clock gating for the CA buffers.
D6-P264-0025 §7.2.4 [body] • Keep CS high for tCKSync_CSPost while issuing a single (1) cycle with CA[5:0]=18h, followed by CA[5:0]=00h.
D6-P264-0026 §7.2.4 [body] The cycle of CA[5:0]=18h must align with desired Host 0° CK phase (Q0).
D6-P264-0027 §7.2.4 [body] When CA4 or CA3 is captured as ‘H’, along with CA5 and CA[2:0] as "L", the internal clock is set to 0° to align with Host clock phase.
D6-P265-0001 §7.2.4 [body] • Issue CA[5:0]=00h with CS low for tCKSync_CAPost.
D6-P265-0002 §7.2.4 [body] After tCKSync is met, the CK Sync operation is done and normal operation is resumed on bth PSDs.
D6-P265-0040 §7.2.4 [table_cell] NOTE 1: The EVEN function rounds up to the next even integer.
D6-P265-0041 §7.2.4 [table_cell] For example, EVEN(4ns/156.25ps) = EVEN(25.6) = 28.
D6-P265-0042 §7.2.4 [body] After the CK Sync Operation, the internal clock phase is align to the Host clock phase, and the command decoder is ready for any valid command.
D6-P265-0043 §7.2.4 [body] Since CA Parity is not used during the CK Sync Operation, and if there are any concerns about Host phase tracking, an example method to detect internal clocking phase misalignment utilizes the CA parity check function is provided.
D6-P265-0044 §7.2.4 [body] In this example, the Host can issue 3 consecutive NOP commands to detect if there is any phase misalignment when CA parity is enabled.
D6-P265-0045 §7.2.4 [body] An example is shown in the figure below, where the first (1st) NOP has CA3 “L” and CA4 “H” for C0 and C1, the second (2nd) NOP has both CA3 and CA4 “L” for C0 and C1, and the third (3rd) NOP has CA3 “H” and CA4 “L” for C0 and C1.
D6-P265-0046 §7.2.4 [body] If the internal phase is 90° different from the Host, it will cause a CA parity fail (CA parity check must be enabled).
D6-P266-0001 §7.2.4 [body] If the CK Sync operation wasn't successful, the Host needs to execute the CK Sync Operation again.
D6-P267-0001 §7.3.1 [body] The ACTIVATE command is used to open (or activate) a row in a particular bank for a subsequent access.
D6-P267-0002 §7.3.1 [body] The value on the BG[3:0] selects the bankgroup; BA inputs selects the bank within the bankgroup, and the address provided on the appropriate CA pins for R[18:0] to select the row (see Command Truth Table).
D6-P267-0003 §7.3.1 [body] This row remains active (or open) until a precharge command is issued to that bank or a precharge all command is issued.
D6-P267-0004 §7.3.1 [body] A bank must be precharged before opening a different row in the same bank.
D6-P267-0005 §7.3.1 [body] The ACTIVATE command is required to be applied before any READ or WRITE operation can be executed.
D6-P267-0006 §7.3.1 [body] The SDRAM can accept a READ command at tRCD_R after the ACTIVATE command is issued, and a WRITE command at tRCD_W (or tRCD_W2 for non read-modify-write [1p6, 2p6, 1p12]) after the ACTIVATE command is issued.
D6-P267-0007 §7.3.1 [body] The bank active and precharge times are defined as tRAS and tRP respectively.
D6-P267-0008 §7.3.1 [body] The minimum time interval between ACTIVATE commands to the same bank is determined by the RAS cycle time of the SDRAM (tRC).
D6-P267-0009 §7.3.1 [body] Bank-to-bank command timing for ACTIVATE commands uses two different timing parameters, depending on whether the banks are in the same or different bank group.
D6-P267-0010 §7.3.1 [body] tRRD_S (short) is used for timing between banks located in different bank groups.
D6-P267-0011 §7.3.1 [body] Consecutive ACTIVATE commands, allowed to be issued at tRRD_S, are restricted to a maximum of four within the time period tFAW (four activate window).
D6-P267-0012 §7.3.1 [body] tRRD_L (long) is used for timing between banks located in the same bank group.
D6-P267-0013 §7.3.1 [body] NOTE: – The following diagrams are just examples and the intention is to show the CK clock to CA is center-aligned.
D6-P267-0014 §7.3.1 [body] Expectation is the DRAM will have a delay from CK input to CA sampling that will need to be aligned to the center of the CA bit.
D6-P268-0001 §7.3.1 [body] NOTE: 1: DES commands are shown for ease of illustration; other commands may be valid at these times.
D6-P268-0011 §7.3.1 [body] NOTE: 1: DES commands are shown for ease of illustration; other commands may be valid at these times.
D6-P269-0001 §7.3.1 [body] NOTE: 1: DES commands are shown for ease of illustration; other commands may be valid at these times.
D6-P269-0027 §7.3.1 [body] NOTE: 1: DES commands are shown for ease of illustration; other commands may be valid at these times.
D6-P269-0028 §7.3.1 [body] NOTE: 2: tRRD_S: ACTIVATE to ACTIVATE Command period: Applies to consecutive ACTIVATE Commands to different Bank Groups
D6-P269-0029 §7.3.1 [body] NOTE: 3: tRRD_L: ACTIVATE to ACTIVATE Command period: Applies to consecutive ACTIVATE Commands to different Banks of the same Bank Group.
D6-P269-0030 §7.3.1.1 [body] Sequential ACTIVATE commands are restricted to a maximum of four (4) within a rolling window determined by the tFAW timing parameter.
D6-P269-0031 §7.3.1.1 [body] A REFdb command counts toward the four activate count within the tFAW window.
D6-P269-0032 §7.3.1.1 [body] ACT-ACT-ACT-ACT or ACT-ACT-ACT-REFdb is allowed within tFAW, but ACT-ACT-ACT-ACT-REFdb is not allowed.
D6-P270-0001 §7.3.2.1 [body] The PRECHARGE command is used to deactivate the open row in a particular bank or the open row in all banks.
D6-P270-0002 §7.3.2.1 [body] The bank(s) shall be available for subsequent row activation a specified time (tRP) after the PRECHARGE command is issued.
D6-P270-0003 §7.3.2.1 [body] Once a bank has been precharged, it is in the idle state and must be activated prior to any READ or WRITE commands being issued to that bank.
D6-P270-0004 §7.3.2.1 [body] Issuing a PRECHARGE command to a bank in idle state is allowed—the classic case is some bank being in idle state when an All-Bank PRECHARGE command is issued.
D6-P270-0005 §7.3.2.1 [body] tRP period needs to be satisfied before issuing next commands such as Activate, Self-refresh Entry, and Refresh, even if the target bank of the PRECHARGE command is already in the idle state.
D6-P270-0006 §7.3.2.1 [body] The tRP period shall be determined by the last PRECHARGE command issued to the bank.
D6-P270-0007 §7.3.2.1 [body] The precharge-to-precharge delay is defined by tPPD in the core timing tables.
D6-P270-0008 §7.3.2.1 [body] tPPD applies to any combination of PRECHARGE commands (PREpb, PREdb, PREqb, PREab).
D6-P270-0009 §7.3.2.1 [body] To ensure that DDR6 devices can meet the instantaneous current demands, the row-Precharge time for an all-bank Precharge (tRPab) is longer than the per-bank Precharge time (tRPpb).
D6-P270-0010 §7.3.2.2 [body] DDR6 will support three different PRECHARGE commands: Per-Bank PRECHARGE (PREpb), Dual-Bank(/Quad-Bank) PRECHARGE (PREdb/PREqb) and All-Bank PRECHARGE (PREab).
D6-P270-0011 §7.3.2.2 [body] See the Command Truth Table for Encodings.
D6-P270-0012 §7.3.2.2 [body] The Per-Bank PRECHARGE command (PREpb) applies precharge to a specific bank defined by BA in a specific bank group defined by BG[3:0], while an All-Bank PRECHARGE applies precharge to all banks in all bank groups.
D6-P270-0013 §7.3.2.2 [body] The Dual-Bank PRECHARGE command (PREdb) applies precharge to specific bank pairs, as defined in Table 1.
D6-P270-0014 §7.3.2.2 [body] The Quad-Bank PRECHARGE command (PREqb) is an extension of PREdb, applying precharge to two sets of bank pairs simultaneously.
D6-P270-0015 §7.3.2.2 [body] PREqb uses the same bank pair mappings as the REFdb command in Table 1, but it operates across both PSDs at once.
D6-P270-0016 §7.3.2.2 [body] This command is unique to 1p6 devices and replaces PREdb.
D6-P270-0017 §7.3.2.2 [body] Note: PREdb and PREqb follow the same bank pair mappings as the REFRESH commands.
D6-P271-0001 §7.3.2.2 [body] A PRECHARGE command following a READ command to the same bank can be issued after tRTP has been satisfied.
D6-P271-0002 §7.3.2.2 [body] However, PRECHARGE to the same bank cannot be issued until after tRAS is also satisfied.
D6-P271-0003 §7.3.2.2 [body] A new bank ACTIVATE command can be issued to the same bank after the row precharge time (tRP) has elapsed, see the below figure.
D6-P271-0008 §7.3.2.2 [body] NOTE: DES commands are shown for ease of illustration; other commands may be valid at these times.
D6-P272-0001 §7.3.2.2 [body] DDR6 SDRAM devices write data to the memory array in prefetch multiples.
D6-P272-0002 §7.3.2.2 [body] The minimum Write-to-Precharge time for commands to the same bank is tWTP clock cycles.
D6-P272-0022 §7.3.2.2 [body] NOTE: DES commands are shown for ease of illustration; other commands may be valid at these times.
D6-P273-0001 §7.3.2.3 [body] When a READ or WRITE command is issued to the DDR6 SDRAM, the AP bit (CA3/C3) can be set to enable the activated bank to automatically begin the precharge operation at the earliest possible moment after the read or write operation.
D6-P273-0002 §7.3.2.3 [body] If AP is LOW when the READ or WRITE command is issued, then the normal read or write operation is executed and the bank remains activated at the completion of the operation.
D6-P273-0003 §7.3.2.3 [body] If AP is HIGH when the READ or WRITE command is issued, the Auto-Precharge function is engaged.
D6-P273-0004 §7.3.2.3 [body] This feature enables the precharge operation to be partially or completely hidden when performing a read or write operation (dependent upon read or write latency), thus improving system performance for random data access.
D6-P273-0005 §7.3.2.3 [body] READ with Auto-Precharge or WRITE with Auto-Precharge commands may be issued after tRCD_R or tRCD_W, respectively, has been satisfied.
D6-P273-0006 §7.3.2.3 [body] The DRAM bases the internal tRAS on tWTP or tRTP in MRxx to schedule the internal Precharge.
D6-P273-0007 §7.3.2.3 [body] tRC needs to be satisfied prior to issuing subsequent ACTIVATE commands to the same bank.
D6-P273-0008 §7.3.2.3 [body] The following figure shows an example of Meeting internal tRAS timings.

D6-P273-0026 §7.3.2.3 [body] Note that the diagrams in the Precharge Operation sections are just examples and the intention is to show CK aligned
D6-P274-0001 §7.3.2.3 [body] to the center of the CA bits.
D6-P274-0002 §7.3.2.3 [body] The expectation is that the DRAM will have a delay from CK input to CA sampling that will need to be aligned to the center of the CA bit.
D6-P274-0011 §7.3.2.3 [body] NOTE: 1: DES commands are shown for ease of illustration; other commands may be valid at these times.
D6-P274-0012 §7.3.2.3 [body] NOTE: 2: tWTR_S starts at the Seventh rising edge of CK_t after WL + BL/2 from Write command.

D6-P274-0029 §7.3.2.3 [body] NOTE: 2: tWTR_L starts at the Seventh rising edge of CK_t after WL + BL/2 from Write command.
D6-P274-0031 §7.3.2.3 [body] If AP is HIGH when a READ command is issued, the READ with Auto-Precharge function is engaged.
D6-P274-0032 §7.3.2.3 [body] After the read operation, an internal precharge operation starts with a time delay.
D6-P275-0001 §7.3.2.3 [body] After a READ with Auto-Precharge command, an ACTIVATE command can be issued to the same bank if the following two conditions are both satisfied:
D6-P275-0002 §7.3.2.3 [body] 1) The RAS Precharge time (tRP) has been satisfied from the clock at which the internal precharge operation began, which is estimated as nRTP after the READ with Auto-Precharge command.
D6-P275-0003 §7.3.2.3 [body] 2) The RAS cycle time (tRC) from the previous bank activation has been satisfied.
D6-P275-0008 §7.3.2.3 [body] NOTE: DES commands are shown for ease of illustration; other commands may be valid at these times.
D6-P275-0010 §7.3.2.3 [body] If AP is HIGH when a WRITE command is issued, the WRITE with Auto-Precharge function is engaged.
D6-P275-0011 §7.3.2.3 [body] The device starts an Auto-Precharge on the rising edge nWTP cycles after the completion of the write operation.
D6-P276-0001 §7.3.2.3 [body] WRITE with Auto-Precharge, an ACTIVATE command can be issued to the same bank if the following conditions are met:
D6-P276-0002 §7.3.2.3 [body] 1) The RAS Precharge time (tRP) has been satisfied from the clock at which the internal precharge operation began, which is estimated as nWTP + after the WRITE with Auto-Precharge command.
D6-P276-0003 §7.3.2.3 [body] 2) The RAS cycle time (tRC) from the previous bank activation has been satisfied.
D6-P276-0019 §7.3.2.3 [body] NOTE: DES commands are shown for ease of illustration; other commands may be valid at these times.
D6-P277-0001 §7.4.1 [body] After a bank has been activated, the Read Operation causes DDR6 SDRAM to retrieve and output data stored in its array.
D6-P277-0002 §7.4.1 [body] The Read Operation is initiated by the Read Command during which the beginning column address and Bank/Bank group address for the data to be retrieved from the array is provided.
D6-P277-0003 §7.4.1 [body] The data is driven by the SDRAM on its DQ pins CAS Read Latency (RL) cycles after the Read Command along with the proper waveform on the Read DQS (RDQS) outputs.
D6-P277-0004 §7.4.1 [body] Read Latency is defined and measured from final cycle of the Read command to the first effective rising DQS (excluding read preamble).
D6-P277-0005 §7.4.1 [body] DDR6 SDRAM requires a DQS post-amble after completing all read DQ bursts.
D6-P277-0006 §7.4.1 [body] The length of DQS post-amble can be set by mode register.
D6-P277-0007 §7.4.1.1 [body] A read command is initiated with CS, and CA[4:0] asserted to the proper state at the rising and falling edges of CK, as defined by the Command Truth Table, Table XXX.
D6-P277-0008 §7.4.1.1 [body] The read latency (RL) is defined from the 4th rising edge of the CK_t of the command that starts a read command to the rising edge of the CK_t from which (tDQS2offset + tDQS2DQo) is measured.
D6-P277-0009 §7.4.1.1 [body] The first valid data is available of RL * tCK + (tDQss (Max) + tDQS2DQo) or RL * tCK + (tDQS2ss (Min) + tDQS2DQO) after the CK_t rising edge of a read command.
D6-P277-0010 §7.4.1.1 [body] The DQ-data is valid for tQW (DQ output window) and the controller is required to periodically train its internal capture clock to stay centered in the tQW window to compensate for timing changes due to temperature and voltage variation.
D6-P277-0011 §7.4.1.1 [body] Burst data is captured by the controller on successive edges of DQS until the 24 bit data burst is complete.
D6-P277-0012 §7.4.1.1 [body] The DQS is required to remain active (toggling) for tRPST (DQS postamble) after the completion of the burst read.
D6-P277-0019 §7.4.1.1 [body] NOTE: 1: DES commands are shown for ease of illustration; other commands may be valid at these times.
D6-P278-0001 §7.4.1.1 [body] NOTE: 3: The DQ signal is shown as “Don’t Care” before the first Read data bit indicating DFE is disabled.
D6-P278-0002 §7.4.1.1 [body] When DFE is enabled, the DQ signal shall be low for a minimum of 8UI prior to the first Read data bit for proper DFE synchronization.
D6-P278-0003 §7.4.1.1 [body] If inter-amble is only 2tCK, the DRAM will drive the DQ signal low for 4UI prior to the first Read data bit for proper DFE.
D6-P278-0004 §7.4.1.2 [body] The following figures show timing diagram of back-to-back read operations where the 2nd Read command issued at tCCD_S.

D6-P278-0062 §7.4.1.2 [body] If inter-amble is only 2tCK, the DRAM will drive the DQ signal low for 4UI prior to the first Read data bit for proper DFE.
D6-P278-0114 §7.4.1.2 [body] NOTE: 1: DES commands are shown for ease of illustration; other commands may be valid at these times.
D6-P279-0002 §7.4.1.2 [body] NOTE: 4: The DQ signal is shown as “Don’t Care” before the first Read data bit indicating DFE is disabled.
D6-P279-0003 §7.4.1.2 [body] When DFE is enabled, the DQ signal shall be low for a minimum of 8UI prior to the first Read data bit for proper DFE synchronization.
D6-P279-0004 §7.4.1.2 [body] If inter-amble is only 2tCK, the DRAM will drive the DQ signal low for 4UI prior to the first Read data bit for proper DFE.
D6-P279-0005 §7.4.1.3 [body] When the delay between two Read commands is slightly larger than tCCD_S, the second Read command must be delayed to the Modulus 2tCK command slot.
D6-P279-0006 §7.4.1.3 [body] In the case of only a 2tCK delay, the SoC sends 2tCK worth of DQS toggles regardless of the PRE and POST options.
D6-P279-0007 §7.4.1.3 [body] When the delay between the two Reads is 4tCK or larger, the behavior depends on the selected PRE and POST options.
D6-P279-0008 §7.4.1.3 [body] In Figure 4, tRPRE = 4tCK and tRPST = 0.5tCK fit within the 4tCK gap on the Read channel.
D6-P279-0009 §7.4.1.3 [body] If tRPRE = 6tCK and tRPST = 0.5tCK (not shown), the DRAM would toggle for the full 4tCK gap.
D6-P279-0010 §7.4.1.3 [body] Figure 4 illustrates a within-PSD example, but the same concept can be extended to PSD-to-PSD scenarios as well.
D6-P279-0067 §7.4.1.3 [body] NOTE: 1: DES commands are shown for ease of illustration; other commands may be valid at these times.
D6-P279-0070 §7.4.1.3 [body] NOTE: 4: The DQ signal is shown as “Don’t Care” before the first Read data bit indicating DFE is disabled.
D6-P279-0071 §7.4.1.3 [body] When DFE is enabled, the DQ signal shall be low for a minimum of 8UI prior to the first Read data bit for proper DFE synchronization.
D6-P279-0072 §7.4.1.3 [body] If inter-amble is only 2tCK, the DRAM will drive the DQ signal low for 4UI prior to the first Read data bit for proper DFE.
D6-P279-0073 §7.4.1.4 [body] The timing that a Write command following a Read command is issued on WCK Always on mode is below.
D6-P279-0074 §7.4.1.4 [body] Refer to clause 8.3.1:Read to Write Timing (tRTW) for detail on Read to Write timing.
D6-P280-0001 §7.4.1.4 [body] When a write command follows a read command, the minimum gap between two commands is defined as tRTW.
D6-P280-0002 §7.4.1.4 [body] tRTW is defined from the 4th rising edge of the CK_t that starts a read command to the 4th rising edge of the CK_t that starts the write command.
D6-P280-0060 §7.4.1.4 [body] NOTE: 1: DES commands are shown for ease of illustration; other commands may be valid at these times.
D6-P280-0063 §7.4.1.4 [body] NOTE: 4: The DQ signal is shown as “Don’t Care” before the first Read data bit indicating DFE is disabled.
D6-P280-0064 §7.4.1.4 [body] When DFE is enabled, the DQ signal shall be low for a minimum of 8UI prior to the first Read data bit for proper DFE synchronization.
D6-P280-0065 §7.4.1.4 [body] If inter-amble is only 2tCK, the DRAM will drive the DQ signal low for 4UI prior to the first Read data bit for proper DFE.
D6-P281-0001 §7.4.1.5 [table_cell] NOTE 1: There are two physical registers assigned to each bit of this MR parameter, designated set point 0 and set point 1.
D6-P281-0003 §7.4.1.5 [table_cell] NOTE 2: MR0C[1:0] = 00 supported over all frequency ranges.
D6-P281-0005 §7.4.1.5 [table_cell] NOTE 4: MR0C[4:2] = 0000 supported over all frequency ranges.
D6-P281-0008 §7.4.1.5 [body] The Read DQS strobe for the SDRAM requires a preamble prior to the first latching edge (the rising edge of Read DQS_t with data "valid"), and it requires a postamble after the last latching edge.
D6-P281-0009 §7.4.1.5 [body] The preamble and postamble lengths are set via mode register writes (MRW).
D6-P282-0001 §7.4.1.5 [body] Preamble/postamble behavior such as static (no-toggle), toggling or combination of static and toggle, and its length can be set by mode register.
D6-P282-0003 §7.4.1.5 [body] DDR6 SDRAM supports programmable option to drive Read DQS early by nCK for low power host architecture to compensate extra delay on the path of Read DQS propagation path compared to DQ.
D6-P284-0001 §7.4.1.6 [body] The DQ Preamble is the defined state of the DQ bus right before the normal data packets are transfered in order to keep the bus stable for the DFE.
D6-P284-0002 §7.4.1.6 [body] In DDR6, this is defined as the amount time in clocks that the DQ bus is kept low.
D6-P284-0003 §7.4.1.6 [body] As like regular preambles, the DQ Preamble will not force Read or Write gaps when doing back to back transactions.
D6-P284-0004 §7.4.1.6 [body] DQ bursts will always take priority over DQ Preambles.
D6-P284-0006 §7.4.1.6 [body] The number of cycles for Read DQ Preamble is configured via MRxx.
D6-P284-0009 §7.4.1.6 [body] NOTE: Figure not available from ballot sponsor.
D6-P285-0002 §7.4.1.6 [body] The number of cycles for Write DQ Preamble is configured via the SPD.
D6-P285-0003 §7.4.1.6 [body] The Write DQ Preamble is currently limited to 8UI/4tCK but additional ranges may be defined for future speed bins.
D6-P285-0006 §7.4.1.6 [body] NOTE: Figure not available from ballot sponsor.
D6-P286-0001 §7.4.2 [body] After a bank is activated, the Write operation stores input data into the DDR6 SDRAM array.
D6-P286-0002 §7.4.2 [body] It begins with a Write command, which specifies the starting column address and the Bank/Bank Group address.
D6-P286-0003 §7.4.2 [body] Data is driven onto the DQ inputs after CAS Write Latency (CWL) cycles from the Write command, accompanied by the appropriate DQS waveform.
D6-P286-0004 §7.4.2 [body] WL is measured from the last cycle of the Write command to the first valid rising edge of DQS (excluding the write preamble).
D6-P286-0005 §7.4.2 [body] After all write bursts, DDR6 SDRAM requires a DQS postamble, whose length is programmable via a mode register.
D6-P286-0006 §7.4.2.1 [body] A WRITE command is initiated with CS, and CA[4:0] asserted to the proper state at the rising and falling edges of CK, as defined by the Command Truth Table, Table XXXX.
D6-P286-0007 §7.4.2.1 [body] The write latency (WL) is defined from the 4th rising edge of the CK_t that starts a write command to the rising edge of the CK_t from which (tDQS2offset+ tDQS2D) is measured.
D6-P286-0008 §7.4.2.1 [body] The first valid “latching” edge of DQS is required to be driven WL * tCK + tDQss (Max) or WL * tCK -tDQss(Min) after the rising edge of CK_t that completes a write command.
D6-P286-0009 §7.4.2.1 [body] The DDR6 SDRAM uses an un-matched DQS-DQ path for lower power, so the DQS is required to arrive at the SDRAM ball prior to the DQ signal by the amount of tDQS2DQi.
D6-P286-0010 §7.4.2.1 [body] The DQS is required to be trained to arrive at the DQ pad center-aligned with the DQ-data.
D6-P286-0011 §7.4.2.1 [body] The DQ-data is required to be held for tDIVW (data input valid window) and the DQS is required to be periodically trained to stay centered in the tDIVW window to compensate for timing changes due to temperature and voltage variation.
D6-P286-0012 §7.4.2.1 [body] Burst data is captured by the SDRAM on successive edges of DQS until the 24 data burst is complete.
D6-P286-0013 §7.4.2.1 [body] DQS is required to remain active for tWPST (DQS postamble) after the completion of the burst WRITE.
D6-P286-0014 §7.4.2.1 [body] Pin input timings are measured relative to the crosspoint of CK_t and CK_c.
D6-P286-0025 §7.4.2.1 [body] NOTE: 1: DES commands are shown for ease of illustration; other commands may be valid at these times.
D6-P287-0002 §7.4.2.1 [body] NOTE: 3: BL=24, tWPRE=8nCK, tWPST=2.5nCK(Toggle).
D6-P287-0003 §7.4.2.1 [body] NOTE: 4: The DQ signal is shown as “Don’t Care” before the first Write data bit indicating DFE is disabled.
D6-P287-0004 §7.4.2.1 [body] When DFE is enabled, the DQ signal shall be low for a minimum of 8UI prior to the first Write data bit for proper DFE synchronization.

D6-P288-0055 §7.4.2.4 [body] When the delay between two Write commands is greater than tCCD_S, the second Write command must move to the next 4tCK modulus Write command slot.

D6-P289-0002 §7.4.2.4 [body] As a result, the combined tWPST and tWPRE is still larger than the inter-amble gap, so DQS will toggle for 8tCK before the data of the second Write command.

D6-P289-0046 §7.4.2.5 [body] When a read command follows a write command, the minimum gap between two commands is defined as min(tWTR_S/L).
D6-P289-0047 §7.4.2.5 [body] tWTR_L (Same BG) / tWTR_S (Different BG) is measured from the 4th rising edge of the CK_t command that satisfies tWTR_L (Same BG) / tWTR_S (Different BG) respectively after a write command to the following read command.

D6-P291-0001 §7.4.2.6 [table_cell] 000B: 4*tCK static, 2*tCK toggle (default) 001B: 4*tCK static, 4*tCK toggle All others are RFU
D6-P291-0002 §7.4.2.6 [table_cell] NOTE 1: There are two physical registers assigned to each bit of this MR parameter, designated set point 0 and set point 1.
D6-P291-0003 §7.4.2.6 [table_cell] Only the registers for the set point determined by the state of the FSP-WR bit (MRyy OP[a]) will be written with an MRW command to this MR address.
D6-P291-0004 §7.4.2.6 [table_cell] NOTE 2: There are two physical registers assigned to each bit of this MR parameter, designated set point 0 and set point 1.
D6-P291-0005 §7.4.2.6 [table_cell] The device will operate only according to the values stored in the registers for the active set point, i.e., the set point determined by the state of the FSP-OP bit (MRyy OP[b]).
D6-P291-0006 §7.4.2.6 [table_cell] The values in the registers for the inactive set point will be ignored by the device, and may be changed without affecting device operation.
D6-P291-0012 §7.4.2.6 [body] DDR6 SDRAM supports a programmable write pre-amble and post-amble.
D6-P291-0013 §7.4.2.6 [body] The Write DQS strobe for the SDRAM requires a pre-amble prior to the first latching edge (the rising edge of Write DQS_t with data "valid"), and it requires a post-amble after the last latching edge.
D6-P291-0014 §7.4.2.6 [body] The pre-amble and post-amble lengths are set via mode register writes (MRW).
D6-P291-0015 §7.4.2.6 [body] Preamble/postamble behavior such as static (no-toggle), toggling or combination of static and toggle, and its length can be set by mode register.
D6-P292-0001 §7.4.2.6 [body] Write pre-amble is configured as 4*tCK static, 2*tCK toggle or 4*tCK static, 4*tCK toggle via MRxx:OP[1:0].
D6-P292-0002 §7.4.2.6 [body] Write post-amble is configured as 2.5*tCK or 4.5*tCK via MRxx:OP[3:2].
D6-P293-0001 §7.4.3 [body] The DQS strobe requires a preamble prior to the first latching edge (the rising edge of DQS_t with data valid) and a postamble after the last latching edge.
D6-P293-0002 §7.4.3 [body] The preamble and postamble options are set via MRW commands.
D6-P293-0003 §7.4.3 [body] Additionally, the postamble- and preamble-configured size does not force the host to add command gaps in the command interval just to satisfy postamble or preamble settings (that is, preamble = 4 tCK + postamble = 2.5tCK does not force tCCD +6).
D6-P293-0004 §7.4.3 [body] Regardless of READ or WRITE interambles, any time the Postambles of one transaction would overlap the beginning of the next transactions preamble, the interamble will be composed of only full toggles.
D6-P293-0005 §7.4.3.1 [body] In READ-to-READ operations with tCCD = BL/2, postamble for the first command and preamble for the second command disappear to create consecutive DQS latching edges for seamless burst operations.
D6-P293-0006 §7.4.3.1 [body] In the case of READ-to-READ operations with a command interval of tCCD +1, tCCD +2, etc., the toggles will always continue if the postambles and preambles overlap.
D6-P294-0001 §7.4.3.1 [body] Static preambles will only be used if there is a gap that exceeds the sum of both selected preamble & postamble.

D6-P297-0001 §7.4.3.1 [body] preambles/intermables/postambles but how the two PSDs share a single DQS.

D6-P297-0005 §7.4.3.1 [body] NOTE: Figure not available from ballot sponsor.
D6-P297-0006 §7.4.3.2 [body] In WRITE-to-WRITE operations with tCCD=BL/2, postamble for the first command and preamble for the second command disappear to create consecutive DQS latching edges for seamless burst operations.
D6-P297-0007 §7.4.3.2 [body] In the case of WRITE-to-WRITE operations with a command interval of tCCD+1, tCCD+2, etc., the toggles will always continue if
D6-P298-0001 §7.4.3.2 [body] the postambles and preambles overlap.
D6-P298-0002 §7.4.3.2 [body] Static preambles will only be used if there is a gap that exceeds the sum of both selected preamble and postamble.
D6-P299-0001 §7.4.4 [body] Latencies related to Read timing are measured from the 4th rising edge of CK_t of the Read command.
D6-P299-0002 §7.4.4 [body] The Read Latencies depend on the settings of 1p6/1p12 device, Read Link protection and Command blocking Disable/Enable.
D6-P299-0003 §7.4.4 [body] WL is calculated to be RL-4tCK.
D6-P299-0004 §7.4.4 [body] For each MR08:OP[5:0] setting, only the data rate ranges shown are supported.
D6-P299-0005 §7.4.4 [table_cell] NOTE 1: Parity latency (PL) is calculated per each data rate in the case of CMD blocking enabled by CA Parity.
D6-P300-0001 §7.4.4 [table_cell] NOTE 1: DDR6 SDRAM device should not be operated at a frequency above the upper frequency limit, or below the lower frequency limit, shown for each RL or WL value.
D6-P301-0001 §7.4.4 [table_cell] NOTE 1: DDR6 SDRAM device should not be operated at a frequency above the upper frequency limit, or below the lower frequency limit, shown for each RL or WL value.
D6-P301-0008 §7.4.4 [table_cell] NOTE 8: Please refer to the Table 1 that describes Parity Latency in the case of Command blocking enabled.
D6-P302-0001 §7.4.5.1 [body] DDR6 SDRAM supports a Metadata feature for Write and Read operations.
D6-P302-0002 §7.4.5.1 [body] In this mode, the data and Metadata are written/read in parallel to/from the array.
D6-P302-0003 §7.4.5.1 [body] This feature supports the MD_OFF, MD_8b and MD_16b modes.
D6-P302-0004 §7.4.5.1 [body] The modes can be chosen by MRxx OP[y] at initialization.
D6-P302-0005 §7.4.5.1 [body] MD_16b refers to the provision of 16 bits of metadata for every 128b of data.
D6-P302-0006 §7.4.5.1 [body] In a 2p3 device, MD_8b refers to the provision of 4 bits of metadata for every 64b of data and MD_16b refers to the provision of 8 bits of metadata for every 64b of data.
D6-P302-0007 §7.4.5.1 [body] The metadata bits will be placed in the existing location of metadata as defined in the data packet for both the Read and Write operations
D6-P302-0008 §7.4.5.2 [body] The Metadata feature is implemented through carveout, where columns in the array are reserved for storing the metadata bits.
D6-P302-0009 §7.4.5.2 [body] The number of columns reserved will depend on the metadata mode being selected.
D6-P302-0010 §7.4.5.2 [body] The DDR6 SDRAM shall powerup in the Default Mode (MRxx OP[y:z] =00) mode.
D6-P302-0011 §7.4.5.2 [body] In the modes where metadata is enabled, the metadata bits are part of the codeword of the on-die ECC engine.
D6-P302-0012 §7.4.5.2 [table_cell] NOTE: Access to the remaining column address space is prohibited
D6-P302-0014 §7.4.5.2 [body] In this mode, the entire addressable column space is available for storing data.
D6-P302-0015 §7.4.5.2 [body] This mode is selected by setting MRxx OP[y:z]=11.
D6-P302-0017 §7.4.5.2 [body] In the MD_8b mode, the DDR6 SDRAM provides 8 bits of metadata for every 128 bits of data.
D6-P302-0018 §7.4.5.2 [body] This mode is selected by setting MRxx OP[y:z]=01.In this mode Column addresses C [5:0] =0x00-0x3B is available for data and access to the remaining column space is prohibited.
D6-P303-0002 §7.4.5.2 [body] In the MD_16b mode, the DDR6 SDRAM provides 16 bits of metadata for every 128 bits of data.
D6-P303-0003 §7.4.5.2 [body] This mode is selected by setting MRxx OP[y:z]=10.In this mode Column addresses C [5:0] =0x00-0x37 is available for data and access to the remaining column space is prohibited.
D6-P303-0004 §7.4.5.3 [body] The Metadata mode can be selected by setting the MRxx OP[y:z] to the required state at initialization.
D6-P303-0006 §7.4.5.3 [body] When the metadata mode is toggled, a delay of tMDMode_Select_Delay is required, before any command is issued to the SDRAM.
D6-P303-0008 §7.4.5.3 [body] Any background training shall be restarted.
D6-P303-0009 §7.4.5.3 [body] DDR6 SDRAM shall be in idle state when the Metadata mode is selected.
D6-P303-0010 §7.4.5.3 [body] The CA bus shall maintain a DESELECT state during tMDMode_Select_Delay
D6-P303-0011 §7.4.5.3 [body] Upon the selection of the Metadata mode, the DDR6 SDRAM shall reset ACI Complete Flag (MRxx OP[y]=0) until a full array Activation Counter Initialization (ACI) is completed by the host to set the Activation counter bits to a known state.
D6-P303-0012 §7.4.5.4 [body] The DDR6 SDRAM shall powerup in a Default Mode as shown in Table 1.In the Default mode Activate commands shall not be issued to the DRAM and any issued Activates are illegal.
D6-P303-0013 §7.4.5.4 [body] Illegal Activates shall be blocked by the DRAM.
D6-P303-0014 §7.4.5.4 [body] Once a Metadata mode is selected by the user the Mode Register bits used in selecting the Metadata mode are locked and cannot be rewritten unless a RESET is issued.
D6-P303-0015 §7.4.5.4 [body] Activate Commands are enabled once tMDMode_Select Delay has elapsed.
D6-P303-0017 §7.4.5.4 [body] The following sequence pertaining to Metadata shall be used.
D6-P303-0018 §7.4.5.4 [body] 1) DDR6 SDRAM powers up into the Default Mode.
D6-P303-0019 §7.4.5.4 [body] Any issued Activates shall be blocked by the DDR6 SDRAM
D6-P303-0020 §7.4.5.4 [body] Activate Commands shall not be issued.
D6-P303-0021 §7.4.5.4 [body] 2) The Metadata Mode is selected and MRxx:OP[y:z] set to something other than Default Mode.
D6-P303-0022 §7.4.5.4 [body] This action locks the MRxx:OP[y:z] and prevents the MRxxOP[y:z] bits from being rewritten.
D6-P303-0023 §7.4.5.4 [body] The CA bus shall maintain a DESELECT state during the tMDMode_Select_Delay time
D6-P303-0024 §7.4.5.4 [body] 3) Activate commands can be issued to the DRAM once tMDMode_Select_Delay has elapsed.
D6-P304-0001 §7.5.1 [body] Two refresh commands are supported: All-bank REFRESH (REFab) and Dual-bank REFRESH (REFdb).
D6-P304-0002 §7.5.1 [body] These commands are available for 2p3 and 2p6 configuration devices.
D6-P304-0003 §7.5.1 [body] For 1p6 configuration devices, the Dual-bank REFRESH command becomes a Quad-bank REFRESH (REFqb) command.
D6-P304-0004 §7.5.1 [body] An All-bank REFRESH command (REFab) refreshes one or more rows in all banks as indicated by the internal row refresh counter.
D6-P304-0005 §7.5.1 [body] A "refresh" to a row means the row is activated, all bits on that row are sensed and restored (refreshed) to the sensed state, and the row is precharged.
D6-P304-0006 §7.5.1 [body] A Dual-bank REFRESH command (REFdb) refreshes one or more rows in two banks as indicated by bank group addresses BG[2:0] and the bank address BA.
D6-P304-0007 §7.5.1 [body] A Dual-bank REFRESH command (REFdb) can be issued in any BG[2:0] and BA order, with the internal bank counter being incremented with each REFdb command.
D6-P304-0008 §7.5.1 [body] The order of REFdb commands may be sequential round robin: (0,16)-(1,17)-(2,18)-(3,19)-(4,20)-(5,21)-(6,22)-(7,23)-(8,24)-(9,25)-(10,26)-(11,27)-(12,28)-(13,29)-(14,30)-(15,31) .
D6-P304-0009 §7.5.1 [body] The order of REFdb commands may be random, for example, the REFdb commands may be issued in the following order: (0,16)-(9,25)-(8,24)-(2,18)-(1,17)-(10,26)-(3,19)-(12,28)-(4,20)-(11,27)-(13,29)-(6,22)-(5,21)-(15,31)-(7,23)-(14,30) .
D6-P304-0010 §7.5.1 [body] After all banks have been refreshed using REFdb commands, the internal bank counter is reset or rolls over to 0, and the internal row refresh counter will be incremented.
D6-P304-0011 §7.5.1 [body] The controller can send another set of REFdb commands in the same order or a different order.
D6-P304-0012 §7.5.1 [body] It is illegal to send a REFdb command to the same bank unless all other banks have been refreshed using the REFdb command.
D6-P304-0013 §7.5.1 [body] The bank counter starts with the first REFdb command after a synchronization event.
D6-P304-0014 §7.5.1 [body] A Quad-bank REFRESH command (REFqb) has the same behavior as the REFdb command, but is performed on the same two banks in both PSDs for a single REFqb command.
D6-P304-0015 §7.5.1 [body] The bank counter is synchronized between the controller and DRAM by resetting it to 0.
D6-P304-0016 §7.5.1 [body] Resetting occurs upon issuing a RESET_n or at every exit from Self-Refresh.
D6-P304-0017 §7.5.1 [body] The REFab command also is a counter synchronization event and resets the bank counter to 0.
D6-P304-0018 §7.5.1 [body] Entering Self-Refresh or issuing a REFab command can be done at any valid time without cycling through all banks using the REFdb (or REFqb) command.
D6-P304-0019 §7.5.1 [body] After the bank counter is synchronized, the controller can issue REFdb (or REFqb) commands in any order as described in the previous paragraphs.
D6-P304-0020 §7.5.1 [body] A REFab command issued when the bank counter is not zero will reset the bank counter to 0 and refreshes are performed to all banks as needed and as indicated by the internal row refresh counter.
D6-P304-0021 §7.5.1 [body] If another refresh command (REFab or REFdb or REFqb) is issued after the REFab command, then it uses an incremented value of the internal row refresh counter.
D6-P304-0022 §7.5.1 [body] The table below shows examples of both bank and refresh counter increment behavior.
D6-P306-0001 §7.5.1 [table_cell] NOTE 1: REFdb and REFqb cannot be mixed since they are supported by different device configurations.
D6-P306-0002 §7.5.1 [table_cell] NOTE 2: Refresh Bank # is per PSD.
D6-P306-0003 §7.5.1 [body] A bank shall be idle before it can be refreshed.
D6-P306-0004 §7.5.1 [body] The controller must track the bank pair being refreshed by the REFdb (or REFqb) command.
D6-P306-0005 §7.5.1 [body] The REFdb (or REFqb) command must not be issued to the device until the following conditions are met:
D6-P306-0006 §7.5.1 [body] • tRFCab has been satisfied after the prior REFab command.
D6-P306-0007 §7.5.1 [body] • tdbR2dbR has been satisfied after the prior REFdb command (or tqbR2qbR has been satisfied after the prior REFqb command).
D6-P306-0008 §7.5.1 [body] • tRP has been satisfied after the prior PRECHARGE command to that bank.
D6-P306-0009 §7.5.1 [body] • tRRD has been satisfied after the prior ACTIVATE command (if applicable, for example after activating a row in a different bank than the one affected by the REFdb or REFqb command).
D6-P306-0010 §7.5.1 [body] The target bank pair is inaccessible during the REFdb (or REFqb) command cycle time tRFCdb (or tRFCqb), however, other banks not being refreshed within the device are still accessible.
D6-P307-0001 §7.5.1 [body] the non-refreshing banks can receive an ACTIVATE or PRECHARGE command, be maintained in an active state, or be accessed by a READ or a WRITE command.
D6-P307-0002 §7.5.1 [body] When the REFdb (or REFqb) command cycle has completed, the affected bank pair will be in the idle state.
D6-P307-0003 §7.5.1 [body] After issuing REFdb (or REFqb), these conditions must be met:
D6-P307-0004 §7.5.1 [body] • tRFCdb (or tRFCqb) must be satisfied before issuing a REFab command.
D6-P307-0005 §7.5.1 [body] • tRFCdb (or tRFCqb) must be satisfied before issuing an ACTIVATE command to the same bank or bank pair.
D6-P307-0006 §7.5.1 [body] • tdbR2act (or tqbR2act) must be satisfied before issuing an ACTIVATE command to a different non-refreshing bank.
D6-P307-0007 §7.5.1 [body] • tdbR2dbR (or tqbR2qbR) must be satisfied before issuing another REFdb (or REFqb) command.
D6-P307-0008 §7.5.1 [body] A REFab command issues a REFRESH command to all banks.
D6-P307-0009 §7.5.1 [body] All banks shall be idle when REFab is issued (for instance, by issuing a PRECHARGE-ALL command prior to issuing the REFab command).
D6-P307-0010 §7.5.1 [body] REFab also synchronizes the bank counter to 0.
D6-P307-0011 §7.5.1 [body] The REFab command must not be issued to the device until the following conditions have been met:
D6-P307-0012 §7.5.1 [body] • tRFCab has been satisfied following the prior REFab command.
D6-P307-0013 §7.5.1 [body] • tRFCdb (or tRFCqb) has been satisfied following the prior REFdb (or REFqb) command.
D6-P307-0014 §7.5.1 [body] • tRP has been satisfied following the prior PRECHARGE command.
D6-P307-0015 §7.5.1 [body] When an all-bank refresh cycle has completed, all banks will be idle.
D6-P307-0017 §7.5.1 [body] • tRFCab must be satisfied before issuing an ACTIVATE command.
D6-P307-0018 §7.5.1 [body] • tRFCab must be satisfied before issuing a REFab or REFdb or REFqb command.
D6-P308-0001 §7.5.1 [table_cell] NOTE 1: A bank shall be in the idle state before it is refreshed, so following an ACTIVATE command REFab is prohibited; REFdb (or REFqb) is supported only if it affects a bank that is in the idle state.
D6-P308-0002 §7.5.1 [table_cell] NOTE 2: "Same bank" and "different bank" here indicate "same bank pair" and “different bank pair", respectively.
D6-P308-0003 §7.5.1 [table_cell] Please refer to "Refresh Bank#" row in previous Table - Bank and Refresh counter increment behavior for further information.

D6-P309-0001 §7.5.1 [body] NOTE: 3: If "Any command" is an ACT command, the end point of tRFCab is last edge (C5) of CK_t.
