D6-P309-0029 §7.5.1 [body] NOTE: 2: Operation to banks other than the banks being refreshed are supported during tRFCdb period.
D6-P309-0054 §7.5.1 [body] NOTE: 1: PREdb is only shown for illustration.
D6-P309-0055 §7.5.1 [body] The requirement to issue the REFqb command is that the banks receiving the REFqb command are in an idle state (precharged and tRP satisfied).
D6-P309-0056 §7.5.1 [body] NOTE: 2: Operation to banks other than the banks being refreshed are supported during tRFCqb period.
D6-P309-0057 §7.5.1 [body] The maximum average refresh interval (tREFI) requirement depends on the device’s junction temperature (Tj), as indicated by bits MR4:OP[4:0].
D6-P309-0058 §7.5.1 [body] When REFab commands are issued and the temperature is Tj ≤ 85°C, the maximum average refresh interval is tREFI.
D6-P309-0059 §7.5.1 [body] When REFab commands are issued and the temperature is 85°C < Tj ≤ 95°C, the maximum average refresh interval is tREFI/2.
D6-P309-0060 §7.5.1 [body] The device includes a method for the host to indicate when REFRESH commands are being issued at the tREFI/2 refresh interval rate.
D6-P309-0061 §7.5.1 [body] The Refresh Interval Rate (RIR) indicator bit, MR4:OP[5], can be set if the host supports the refresh interval rate indication as part of the REFRESH command using C2:CA4.
D6-P309-0062 §7.5.1 [body] When enabled (MR4:OP[5]=1), the host issues REFab commands at tREFI with C2:CA4=L when Tj ≤ 85°C, and the host issues REFab commands at tREFI/2 with C2:CA4=H (Tj any allowable temperature).
D6-P309-0063 §7.5.1 [body] When issuing Dual-bank REFRESH (REFdb) or Quad-bank REFRESH (REFqb) commands, the maximum average refresh interval is divided by 16 (tREFI or tREFI/2), depending on the MR4 temperature requirement.
D6-P310-0001 §7.5.1 [body] tREFI is based on the 8192 REFRESH commands per bank that need to be issued within the baseline tREFW=32ms refresh period on the device.
D6-P310-0002 §7.5.1 [body] In general, REFRESH commands need to be issued every tREFI interval.
D6-P310-0003 §7.5.1 [body] To allow for improved efficiency in scheduling and switching between tasks, some flexibility in the absolute refresh interval is provided.
D6-P310-0004 §7.5.1 [body] A maximum of two (2) REFRESH commands can be postponed during operation.
D6-P310-0005 §7.5.1 [body] In the case that 2 REFRESH commands are postponed in a row, the resulting maximum interval between the surrounding REFRESH commands is limited to 3 x tREFI.
D6-P310-0006 §7.5.1 [body] At any given time, a maximum of 3 REFab commands can be issued within 1x tREFI.
D6-P310-0007 §7.5.1 [body] Self-Refresh may be entered with a maximum of 2 REFRESH commands being postponed.
D6-P310-0008 §7.5.1 [body] After exiting Self-Refresh with one or more REFRESH commands postponed, additional REFRESH commands may be postponed to the extent that the total number of postponed REFRESH commands (before and after the Self-Refresh) will never exceed 2.
D6-P310-0009 §7.5.1 [body] During Self-Refresh, the number of postponed REFRESH commands does not change.
D6-P310-0010 §7.5.1 [body] The following figure describes postponed All-bank REFRESH commands.
D6-P311-0005 §7.5.1.1 [table_cell] NOTE 1: Refresh for each pseudo-split die is independent of the other pseudo-split die.
D6-P311-0006 §7.5.1.1 [table_cell] Power delivery in the user's system should be verified to make sure the DC operating conditions are maintained when multiple pseudo-split die are refreshed simultaneously.
D6-P311-0007 §7.5.1.1 [table_cell] NOTE 2: 1x refresh rate (tREFW = 32ms) is available for junction temperatures at or below 85°C, based on MR4.
D6-P311-0008 §7.5.1.1 [table_cell] Refer to MR4:OP[4:0] for refresh rate details.
D6-P312-0001 §7.5.2 [body] The Self-Refresh (SREF) command operation retains data in the device without external Refresh commands.
D6-P312-0002 §7.5.2 [body] While in SREF with Full Power-Down, the device retains data without external clockingDDR6 SDRAM even when the rest of the system is powered down and external clocking is disabled.
D6-P312-0003 §7.5.2 [body] The device has a built-in timer to accommodate the SREF operation.
D6-P312-0004 §7.5.2 [body] While in SREF, the device adjusts its internal average periodic refresh interval as needed, based on the on-die temperature sensor.
D6-P312-0005 §7.5.2 [body] The internal periodic refresh interval adjustment (increasing, decreasing or staying constant) does not require any external control.
D6-P312-0006 §7.5.2 [body] The device enters Self-Refresh through the Self-Refresh Entry (SRE) command.
D6-P312-0007 §7.5.2 [body] SRE is issued by asserting CS for two consecutive clock cycles is a 2-cycle command with CA[2:0] = 000b during the first cycle and CA[2:0] = 100b during the second cycle.
D6-P312-0008 §7.5.2 [body] If the SRE command is issued with CA[3] HIGH during the second cycle, Self-Refresh is initiated with Full Power-Down.
D6-P312-0009 §7.5.2 [body] For 2p3 and 2p6 devices, a SRE command without Full Power-Down may be issued to each PSD independently.
D6-P312-0010 §7.5.2 [body] Before issuing the SRE command, the target PSD shall be idle with all banks precharged.
D6-P312-0011 §7.5.2 [body] DDR6 SDRAM can operate in Self-Refresh across standard and elevated temperature ranges, managing its own power consumption as the operating temperature changes.
D6-P312-0012 §7.5.2 [body] For proper Self-Refresh operation, power supply pins (VDD, VDDQ, and VPP) shall remain at valid operating levels.
D6-P312-0013 §7.5.2 [body] During Self-Refresh without Full Power-Down, all input pins of the PSD are activated and ODT states are maintained according to the state of the mode registers, as well as NT ODT.
D6-P312-0014 §7.5.2 [body] If not in Full Power-Down, the device may accept the following commands during Self-Refresh after tSRED is met: PDE, PDX, SRX, MRR, MRW, DES, NOP, and MCME (including MRUPDATE mode).
D6-P312-0015 §7.5.2 [body] All defined training modes in Chapter 4 are supported during Self-Refresh.
D6-P312-0016 §7.5.2 [body] Training modes must be properly exited and all required exit timing delays must be satisfied before issuing SRX.
D6-P312-0017 §7.5.2 [body] The minimum time that the device is required to remain in Self-Refresh is tSR.
D6-P312-0018 §7.5.2 [body] The device exits Self-Refresh through the Self-Refresh Exit (SRX) command.
D6-P312-0019 §7.5.2 [body] SRX is issued by asserting CS for two consecutive clock cycles is a 2-cycle command with CA[2:0] = 000b during the first cycle and CA[2:0] = 110b during the second cycle.
D6-P312-0020 §7.5.2 [body] If SRX is issued while the device is not in Self-Refresh, the command will not execute.
D6-P312-0021 §7.5.2 [body] For 2p3 and 2p6 devices, SRX shall be issued to each PSD independently.
D6-P312-0022 §7.5.2 [body] Once SRX is registered, only MRR, MRW, and MCME (including MRUPDATE mode) commands are allowed until tXSR is satisfied.
D6-P312-0023 §7.5.2 [body] The use of Self-Refresh introduces the possibility that an internally timed Refresh event can be missed when Self-Refresh Exit is registered because entering and exiting Self-Refresh will reset the internal bank counter.
D6-P312-0024 §7.5.2 [body] Upon exit from Self-Refresh, at least one REFab or sixteen REFdb/REFqb commands shall be issued before entry into a subsequent Self-Refresh.
D6-P312-0025 §7.5.2 [body] Refer to the Refresh section when postponing or pulling in Refresh commands.
D6-P312-0026 §7.5.2 [body] This Refresh command does not count toward the tREFI-based periodic refresh requirement and does not modify the postponed or
D6-P313-0001 §7.5.2 [body] pulled-in refresh counts; the Refresh command does count toward the number of maximum refreshes permitted within 2 × tREFI.
D6-P313-0022 §7.5.2 [body] NOTE: 1: Only PDE, PDX, SRX, MRR, MRW, DES, NOP, and MCME (including MRUPDATE mode) commands are allowed during Self-Refresh.
D6-P313-0023 §7.5.2 [body] NOTE: 2: DES commands are shown for ease of illustration; other commands may be valid at these times where the CA input is indicated as “Don’t Care”.
D6-P313-0024 §7.5.2.1 [body] Entry to and exit from Full Power-Down mode is permitted while the device is in Self-Refresh.
D6-P313-0025 §7.5.2.1 [body] There are two ways to enter Full Power-Down during Self-Refresh: issue the SRE command with CA[3] HIGH during the second clock cycle or issue the Full Power-Down Entry (PDE) command after waiting tSRED following the SRE command.
D6-P313-0026 §7.5.2.1 [body] DuringFor Full Power-Down in Self-Refresh on 2p3 and 2p6 devices, the internal clock is stopped and Full Power-Down is entered after both PSDs have received the Full PDE command.
D6-P313-0027 §7.5.2.1 [body] Before Self-Refresh can be exited on the target PSD, the Power-Down Exit (PDX) command shall be issued and CK Sync shall be completed.
D6-P313-0028 §7.5.2.1 [body] The timing parameters and operation of Full Power-Down are defined in the Power-Down section.
D6-P314-0001 §7.5.2.1 [body] The related timing parameters between Self-Refresh entry/exit and power-down entry/exit are shown in the following figures.
D6-P314-0025 §7.5.2.1 [body] NOTE: 1: SRX command on PSD0 after Full Power-Down is shown for ease of illustration; PDE, PDX, MRR, MRW, DES, NOP and MCME commands are valid where SRX is shown.
D6-P314-0026 §7.5.2.1 [body] NOTE: 2: PDX and CK Sync are not shown on PSD0 between PDE and SRX for ease of illustration.
D6-P314-0027 §7.5.2.1 [body] Full Power-Down and CK Sync operation requirements are defined in their respective sections of the standard.
D6-P314-0028 §7.5.2.2 [table_cell] Exit Self-Refresh to valid commands
D6-P315-0001 §7.5.3.1 [body] Per Row Activation Counting (PRAC) monitors for row activity that may adversely affect the data stored in cells on neighboring rows within the DRAM array.
D6-P315-0002 §7.5.3.1 [body] The intended implementation for PRAC is to add Activation Counter bits corresponding to every row in the DRAM.
D6-P315-0003 §7.5.3.1 [body] These bits store a count associated with the number of activations and the duration of the activations received by a row since the last time it was refreshed.
D6-P315-0004 §7.5.3.1 [body] Activations include any commands that result in an activation and precharge of a row.
D6-P315-0005 §7.5.3.2 [body] Upon power-up or any time that device refresh requirements are violated, the Activation Counter bits may be in an unknown state, requiring initialization to put the bits into a known state.
D6-P315-0006 §7.5.3.2 [body] Prior to use of the Activation Counter bits, a full array Activation Counter Initialization (ACI) must be performed.
D6-P315-0007 §7.5.3.2 [body] The DRAM will not track activation counts, nor issue an Alert Back-Off (ABO), until the Activation Counter bits are initialized.
D6-P315-0008 §7.5.3.2 [body] To initialize the activation counter bits, the host sets MR4E35[0] = 1b to indicate to the device that a full array refresh will take place.
D6-P315-0009 §7.5.3.2 [body] Upon completion of a full refresh ACI cycle, the DRAM indicates completion by setting MR4E35[1] = 1b, which the host then follows by setting MR4E35[0] = 0b.
D6-P315-0010 §7.5.3.2 [body] Only REF, MRR, NOP and NT_ODT commands are permitted during initialization.
D6-P315-0011 §7.5.3.2 [body] The DRAM starts counting activations from that point forward and issues the ABO as necessary.
D6-P315-0012 §7.5.3.2 [body] During ACI, the DRAM maintains refresh in the main array.
D6-P315-0013 §7.5.3.2 [body] If the host re-enters ACI by setting MR4E35[0] = 1b, the DRAM will reset MR4E35[1] = 0b until the initialization has been completed.
D6-P315-0014 §7.5.3.2 [body] During ACI, the DRAM will perform refresh commands in the main array based on the ACI Main Array Refresh Control bit, MR34[4].
D6-P315-0015 §7.5.3.2 [body] Performing an ACI with MR34[4]=0 (default) does not require the DRAM to refresh the main array, and any data previously written may be corrupted.
D6-P315-0016 §7.5.3.2 [body] Performing an ACI with MR34[4]=1 requires the DRAM to refresh the main array.
D6-P315-0017 §7.5.3.2 [body] Like normal DRAM cells, the activation counter bits require refresh to maintain the stored values.
D6-P315-0018 §7.5.3.2 [body] Anytime refresh is violated during ACI or after ACI, the counter bits need set to a known state by repeating the ACI.
D6-P315-0019 §7.5.3.2 [body] A full array refresh will set counter bits to a known state, but ABOs may be generated during the refresh.
D6-P315-0020 §7.5.3.2 [body] During hPPR and MBIST/mPPR, the system does not maintain refresh to the DRAM.
D6-P315-0021 §7.5.3.2 [body] Therefore, the host must perform a full array ACI before rewriting data following the hPPR and MBIST/mPPR operations.
D6-P315-0022 §7.5.3.2 [body] During sPPR, the system is required to maintain refresh to DRAM.
D6-P315-0023 §7.5.3.2 [body] However, before the sPPR operation, the DRAM is not required to maintain data integrity (refresh) for the row element designated to be used for the repair.
D6-P315-0024 §7.5.3.2 [body] tPGM_Exit timing provides sufficient time to the DRAM to update the activation count of the new row as well as proactively perform mitigation for neighboring rows of the new row before completion of sPPR operation.
D6-P315-0025 §7.5.3.2 [body] Likewise for sPPR_Undo, the DRAM may update the activation count of the old row as well as proactively perform mitigation for neighboring rows of the old row before completion of sPPR operation.
D6-P315-0026 §7.5.3.3 [body] PRAC requires additional counter cells per row in the DRAM to store Activation (ACT) command counts.
D6-P315-0027 §7.5.3.3 [body] Updating the Activation Counter cells requires a read-modify-write to the Activation Counter cells.
D6-P316-0001 §7.5.3.3 [body] An example of the timings affected by the updating of the activation counter bits is shown below.
D6-P316-0002 §7.5.3.3 [body] The “i” references DRAM internal commands, not external commands issued by the Host.
D6-P316-0003 §7.5.3.3 [body] In this example, the PRE command initiates an internal read (iRD) followed by an internal write (iWR) to increment the Activation Counting bits for all open rows affected by the PRE command.
D6-P316-0004 §7.5.3.3 [body] This is the Activation Counter Update (ACU), with the associated tACU timing.
D6-P316-0020 §7.5.3.4 [body] Since the DRAM is updating row activation counts based on row activity, back-off action is required upon one or more rows reaching a counter threshold level or levels, or when registers holding row addresses for targeted refreshing reaches or passes a critical level.
D6-P316-0021 §7.5.3.4 [body] This back-off action is a request by the DRAM for the host to pause normal activity for a recovery period so that refresh management (RFM) commands or other functions can be performed.
D6-P316-0022 §7.5.3.4 [body] The back-off protocol is described in the next section.
D6-P316-0023 §7.5.3.4 [body] The counter and/or register threshold level(s) may be changed (reduced) by setting the adaptive activation counting levels in MR4D34[3:2] to something other than the default MR4D34[3:2] = 00b.
D6-P316-0024 §7.5.3.4 [body] These adaptive levels are based on the default starting value.
D6-P316-0025 §7.5.3.4 [body] The threshold level(s) reductions are approximately level A = default - 10%, level B = default - 20%, and level C = default - 30%.
D6-P316-0026 §7.5.3.4 [body] Changing levels requires an ACI orwith full array refresh to set/reset all counters to a known state below critical threshold levels and to empty the registers.
D6-P316-0027 §7.5.3.4 [body] In addition to the back-off which is required to prevent activation counts from exceeding the threshold level, the device should proactively perform targeted refreshes during Refresh Management (RFM) and/or Refresh (REF) commands to reduce and/or prevent the occurrence of the alert back-off being triggered.
D6-P316-0028 §7.5.3.4 [body] RFM commands must be issued either periodically or based on a bank activation count, as described below.
D6-P316-0029 §7.5.3.4 [body] The host may implement either Periodic RFM (PRFM) or Bank Activation Threshold (BAT), but is not required to support both modes simultaneously.
D6-P316-0030 §7.5.3.4 [body] When BAT is used to ensure proper refresh management, Periodic RFM commands are not required.
D6-P316-0031 §7.5.3.4 [body] Likewise when BAT is not active, PRFM shall be used to ensure proper refresh management.
D6-P316-0032 §7.5.3.5 [body] Periodic Refresh Management (PRFM) requires the host to send an RFM command to each bank within every tPRFMi window, with the maximum gap of 1.5*tPRFMi between each RFM command.
D6-P316-0033 §7.5.3.5 [body] RFMab, RFMdb and RFMpb commands are applicable, with RFMdb and RFMpb requiring the same time gap stated above on a per equivalent bank basis.
D6-P316-0034 §7.5.3.5 [body] When using PRFM, time is partitioned into consecutive tPRFMi windows.
D6-P316-0035 §7.5.3.5 [body] The host shall issue at least one RFM command to each bank during each window.
D6-P316-0036 §7.5.3.5 [body] To allow scheduling flexibility across window boundaries, the maximum
D6-P317-0001 §7.5.3.5 [body] allowable gap between RFM commands to the same bank is 1.5 × tPRFMi.
D6-P317-0002 §7.5.3.5 [body] To avoid a strictly deterministic refresh pattern, the host is encouraged to vary the timing of RFM commands within and across tPRFMi windows, such that RFM activity remains periodic, not fixed to a rigid schedule.
D6-P317-0003 §7.5.3.5 [body] Since tPRFMi is a window, no more than the equivalent of 1 RFM command may be postponed.
D6-P317-0004 §7.5.3.5 [body] All references to dual bank, REFdb or RFMdb apply to 2p3 and 2p6 configuration devices.
D6-P317-0005 §7.5.3.5 [body] For 1p6 configuration devices, all references to “dual bank” become “quad bank”, references to REFdb become REFqb, and references to “RFMdb” become “RFMqb”, with equivalent command separation and timings.
D6-P317-0006 §7.5.3.6 [body] Bank Activation Threshold (BAT) requires that the host track the level of activity that occurs on a per-bank basis.
D6-P317-0007 §7.5.3.6 [body] When the bank activation threshold (BAT) is reached (shown below), a RFMab, RFMdb or RFMpb command is issued to the bank or banks at the BAT.
D6-P317-0008 §7.5.3.6 [body] Upon issuing an RFM command, any bank or banks receiving the command can have its BAT count decremented by the BAT threshold, with the result not less than zero (0).
D6-P317-0009 §7.5.3.6 [body] Bank activation counting is done for every ACT/PRE combination, and additional counts are added by dividing the activation time by tRC or longer to account for long tRAS.
D6-P317-0010 §7.5.3.6 [body] The duration of the RFM command (tRFM) is sufficient for the row refresh span of ±1 and ±2 physically adjacent rows and the target row to be refreshed.
D6-P317-0011 §7.5.3.6 [body] Each row refresh span is determined as tRRFab, tRRFdb and tRRFpb.
D6-P318-0001 §7.5.3.6 [table_cell] NOTE 1: PRAC-related MR changes are prohibited during the tRFMab(min), tRFMdb(min) and tRFMpb(min) periods.
D6-P318-0021 §7.5.3.6 [body] Similar to Refresh commands, a bank shall be idle before it can receive the RFM command.
D6-P318-0022 §7.5.3.6 [body] The bank(s) receiving the RFM command will be inaccessible for the cycle time tRFM, however, other banks not being refreshed within the device during RFMpb or RFMdb commands are still accessible.
D6-P318-0023 §7.5.3.6 [body] During the RFMpb or RFMdb operation, any of the non-refreshing banks can receive an ACTIVATE or PRECHARGE command, be maintained in an active state, or be accessed by a READ or a WRITE command.
D6-P318-0024 §7.5.3.6 [body] When the RFM command has completed, the affected bank(s) will be in the idle state.
D6-P318-0025 §7.5.3.6 [body] The RFM Command Scheduling Separation Requirements must be met when issuing RFM commands.
D6-P319-0001 §7.5.3.6 [table_cell] NOTE 1: A bank shall be in the idle state before it is refreshed, so following an ACTIVATE command, RFMab is prohibited; RFMpb, RFMdb is permitted only if it affects a bank that is in the idle state.
D6-P319-0002 §7.5.3.6 [table_cell] NOTE 2: "Same bank" and "different bank" indicate "either bank within the Refresh bank pair" and “a bank not within the Refresh bank pair", respectively.
D6-P319-0003 §7.5.3.6 [table_cell] Please refer to "Refresh Bank#" column in the Refresh Operation section table "Bank and Refresh Counter Increment Behavior" for further information.
D6-P319-0004 §7.5.3.7 [body] To maintain data integrity, the DRAM may request the host to pause normal activity for a maintenance period where refresh management (RFM) commands or other functions may be performed.
D6-P319-0005 §7.5.3.7 [body] This is called a “back-off.”
D6-P319-0006 §7.5.3.8 [body] The back-off protocol uses the ALERT signal and mode register settings to provide a handshake between the DRAM and the host.
D6-P319-0007 §7.5.3.8 [body] This is the alert back-off (ABO).
D6-P319-0008 §7.5.3.9 [body] Upon reaching critical threshold levels by one or more rows, or when registers holding addresses for targeted refreshing reaches or passes a critical level, the DRAM asserts ALERT and sets the ABO flag at MR4EC8[0] = 1b, indicating that ALERT was asserted due to additional refresh management (RFM) being required.
D6-P319-0009 §7.5.3.9 [body] All commands received by the device are executed while ALERT is asserted to maintain synchronization.
D6-P320-0001 §7.5.3.9 [body] Commands that may trigger the ABO include PREab/PREdb/PREpb, WRA, RDA, REFab/REFdb, and RFMab/RFMdb/RFMpb.
D6-P320-0002 §7.5.3.9 [body] ALERT will be asserted after issuing the trigger command but before the max(5ns,10nCK) a maximum of 8ns following the completion of the respective command duration (e.g. PREab/PREdb/PREpb = tRP, REFab/REFdb = tRFC, RFMab/RFMdb/RFMpb = tRFM).
D6-P320-0003 §7.5.3.9 [body] Since the PREpb required during hPPR/sPPR and the PREab during mPPR have different functions than closing a row, no counter bits will be incremented associated with the repaired row, therefore no ABO will be triggered in this instance.
D6-P320-0004 §7.5.3.10 [body] To instantiate the ABO, ALERT is asserted with a fixed duration of clock cycles as defined by tALERT_PW.
D6-P320-0037 §7.5.3.10 [body] After the ALERT is asserted, a bounded number of ACTIVATE (ACT) commands are permitted before the host starts the RFM-based recovery period.
D6-P320-0038 §7.5.3.10 [body] Issuing ACT commands along with normal traffic is allowed within the tABO_ACT window, however no additional ACT commands are allowed at the end of the tABO_ACT window until ABO_Recovery is complete.
D6-P320-0039 §7.5.3.10 [body] The host shall complete idling all banks within tREFI after the ALERT is asserted, which is followed by the recovery period (ABO_Recovery).
D6-P320-0040 §7.5.3.10 [body] The recovery period begins when all banks have been idled in preparation for RFMab commands.
D6-P320-0041 §7.5.3.10 [body] The DRAM self-clears the ABO flag by setting MR4FC8[0]=0b by the end of the ABO_Recovery period.
D6-P320-0042 §7.5.3.10 [body] ABO_Recovery ends tRFMab after the last RFMab command or after tALERT_PW, whichever takes longer.
D6-P320-0043 §7.5.3.10 [body] The only RFM command allowed during the ABO_Recovery period is RFMab.
D6-P320-0044 §7.5.3.10 [body] The duration of the RFMab command is described in the previous section (Per Row Activation Counting Response).
D6-P320-0045 §7.5.3.10 [body] The number of RFMab commands required to be issued per ABO maintenance period is defined by mode register ABO_RFM bits, MR4D34[1:0].
D6-P320-0046 §7.5.3.10 [body] Systems that have stricter requirements for isochronous bandwidth may limit the number of RFMab commands by setting MR4D34[1:0] to a lower value.
D6-P321-0001 §7.5.3.10 [body] If additional RFM commands are needed, the ABO protocol is restarted following the ABO delay, as described below.
D6-P321-0002 §7.5.3.10 [body] During the ABO_Recovery period, a host can issue only RFMab, REFab/REFdb, PDE, PDX, SRE, PREab/PREdb/PREpb, NOP, NT_ODT, MRW (PRAC-related MR changes not allowed during ABO_Recovery) and MRR commands.
D6-P321-0003 §7.5.3.10 [body] Refresh commands are issued to meet device refresh requirements, but they do not perform any of the required refresh management.
D6-P321-0004 §7.5.3.10 [body] Power Down Entry and Exit (PDE, PDX) and Self Refresh Entry and Exit (SRE, SRX) are allowed during recovery period.
D6-P321-0005 §7.5.3.10 [body] In Self Refresh, the device de-asserts ALERT if triggered by an ABO.
D6-P321-0006 §7.5.3.10 [body] Upon exiting Self Refresh, the device re-evaluates and issues a new ALERT if ABO is still needed.
D6-P321-0007 §7.5.3.10 [body] In power-down, the device keeps the current ALERT state.
D6-P321-0008 §7.5.3.11 [body] ABO_Delay defines an interval in which the device services a minimum number of commands that may trigger an ABO before it can reassert ALERT.
D6-P321-0009 §7.5.3.11 [body] The ABO_Delay count is specified by MR34[1:0].
D6-P321-0010 §7.5.3.11 [body] Adherence to the ABO_Delay count is set by the ABO_Delay Control bit, MR34[5], where the default MR34[5]=0 requires that ALERT will not be reasserted until the ABO_Delay count is satisfied, and MR34[5]=1 allows the ALERT to be reasserted as needed to maintain data integrity.
D6-P321-0011 §7.5.3.11 [body] ABO_Delay is specified by the minimum number of commands in MR4D[1:0].
D6-P321-0012 §7.5.3.11 [body] Commands that may trigger ALERT include PREab/PREdb/PREpb, WRA, RDA, REFab/REFdb, and RFMab/RFMdb/RFMpb and Manual ECS.
D6-P321-0013 §7.5.3.11 [body] Any of these commands that occur are included in the total ABO_Delay count allowed.
D6-P321-0014 §7.5.3.11 [body] Following ABO_Delay, any of the commands included in the PRAC-Triggered Alert Back-Off section above may trigger the ABO, as well as an ACT command.
D6-P321-0015 §7.5.3.11 [body] ALERT may be shared between sub-channels in a channel, but parameters associated with ABO (tABO_ACT, tALERT_PW, ABO_Recovery, ABO_Delay) are independent per sub-channel.
D6-P322-0001 §7.5.3.11 [table_cell] CK_c CK_t tABO_ACT+ABO_Recovery ABO_delay tABO_ACT+ABO_Recovery Alert Don’t Care Time Break Figure 136 — Bank-to-Bank Alert Back-Off Delay 7.5.3.12.
D6-P322-0002 §7.5.3.11 [table_cell] ALERT Priorities At times, Write CRC, Alert Back-Off, and CA parity errors may occur simultaneously Alert Back-Off may occur simultaneously with other functions that also use ALERT, resulting in an overlap of ALERT assertions or the occurrence of the two events back-to-back (non-overlapped) may result in a short “glitch” on ALERT.
D6-P322-0003 §7.5.3.11 [table_cell] For all events, the DRAM shall treat the ALERT as an “OR” function where ALERT is issued whenever an event occurs, and the DRAM sets the corresponding Write CRC or, Alert Back-Off (MR4FC8[0]), or CA parity error flag indicating which type of event, or possibly both multiple, occurred.
D6-P322-0004 §7.5.3.13 [body] The DRAM has the capability to detect errors that occur within the counter bits, and the following protocol defines how the device reports the associated failing row address to the host.
D6-P322-0005 §7.5.3.13 [body] If a counter bit error an Activation Counter Error (ACE) is detected, the device sets the Counter Error Flag, MR4FC8[1] = 1b, if not previously set, and.
D6-P322-0006 §7.5.3.13 [body] While the Counter Error Flag is set, the counter function for the specific row with the error is disabled.
D6-P322-0007 §7.5.3.13 [body] The DRAM does not assert the ABO to request RFM commands for errors associated with that row.
D6-P322-0008 §7.5.3.13 [body] All other rows without counter errors continue to count activity and assert ALERT, if necessary.
D6-P322-0009 §7.5.3.13 [body] During ECS, if the Counter Error Flag is set and the DRAM logs a failing row address, the DRAM will update MR50-MR53C9-MRCB with the failing row address and set the Counter Address Report Flag, MR4FC8[2]=1b, when the ECS reaches the failing row.
D6-P322-0010 §7.5.3.13 [body] Only the first failing row will be reported.
D6-P322-0011 §7.5.3.13 [body] Additional rows will be reported in a subsequent scrub if they continue to fail after the Counter Address Report Flag is cleared by the host.
D6-P322-0012 §7.5.3.13 [body] A counter RAS error that occurs during the ECS may still trigger the counter error flag, but may not set the counter address report flag and report the failing row address.
D6-P322-0013 §7.5.3.13 [body] The host clears the counter error flag and counter address report flag, once the necessary information about the failing row has been collected and mitigative actions have been taken.
D6-P323-0001 §7.5.3.14 [body] Once enabled, the counter for a target row to be tested is initialized by the host setting MR4E35[3] = 1b, followed by an ACT/PRE to the target row.
D6-P323-0002 §7.5.3.14 [body] Per Row Activation Counting (PRAC) testing is activated by setting MR4E35[2] = 1b.
D6-P323-0003 §7.5.3.14 [body] The host then sets MR4E35[3] = 0b and issues 32 activations or fewer to the target row or rows to trigger the ABO (multiple increments to the counter bits may occur depending on tRAS duration, mimicking normal operations).
D6-P323-0004 §7.5.3.14 [body] ABO is required as described previously before testing subsequent rows.
D6-P323-0005 §7.5.3.14 [body] Depending on counter bit starting values, all DRAMs in the package or on a DIMM devices may not trigger the ABO at the same time (detected by MR4FC8[0]); however, all devices will trigger within 32 activations.
D6-P323-0006 §7.5.3.14 [body] After PRAC testing is complete, the test mode is cleared by the Host setting MR4E35[2] = 0b.
D6-P323-0007 §7.5.3.14 [body] Since the counter cells will be in an unknown state following testing, a full ACI is required before resuming normal operations.
D6-P323-0008 §7.5.3.14 [body] Refresh commands are not allowed while PRAC Testing is enabled.
D6-P323-0009 §7.5.3.14 [body] Data in normal cells are not maintained during in PRAC testing.
D6-P323-0010 §7.5.3.15 [body] ALERT verification is triggered by setting MR4E35[4] = 1b.
D6-P323-0011 §7.5.3.15 [body] The DRAM asserts ALERT and sets the ABO flag at MR4FC8[0] = 1b within tMRD after the MRW and will keep ALERT asserted for tALERT_PW.
D6-P323-0012 §7.5.3.15 [body] The host should follow all normal ABO procedures for clearing the ALERT.
D6-P323-0013 §7.5.3.15 [body] MR4E35[4] and MR4FC8[0] will be self-cleared by the device prior to the end of the ABO_Recovery period.
D6-P323-0014 §7.5.3.15 [body] Data in normal cells are maintained during ALERT verification, as long as refresh is maintained.
D6-P323-0015 §7.5.3.16 [body] NOTE: Self-clearing mode register bits (MR35[4], MRC8[0]) are automatically reset by the DRAM and do not require host intervention to clear.
D6-P325-0001 §7.5.3.18 [table_cell] NOTE 1: When ACI is enabled (MR4E35[0]=1), MR4E35[1] is reset to 0 by the DRAM.
D6-P325-0002 §7.5.3.19 [table_cell] MR4FC8[2]: COUNTER_ERROR_REPORT Counter Address Report 0: Clear 1: Failing row address available in MR50-MR53C9-MRCC
D6-P325-0003 §7.5.3.19 [table_cell] MR4FC8[1]: COUNTER_ERR Counter Error 0: Clear 1: Counter Error detected
D6-P325-0004 §7.5.3.19 [table_cell] MR4FC8[0]: ALERT_BACK_OFF Alert Back-Off 0: Clear 1: Source of ALERT
D6-P325-0005 §7.5.3.19 [table_cell] NOTE 1: These flags can only be set by the DRAM.
D6-P325-0006 §7.5.3.19 [table_cell] MRW of MR4FC8[1]=0 or MR4FC8[2]=0 will clear the flag.
D6-P325-0007 §7.5.3.19 [table_cell] MRW of MR4FC8[1]=1 or MR4FC8[2]=1 by the host will be ignored(1 if set by the DRAM, otherwise 0).
D6-P328-0001 §7.6.1 [body] DDR6 SDRAM supports three power-down modes: Full Power-Down (Full-PD), Full Power-Down with Frequency Change (Full-PD FC), and ODT Power-Down (ODT-PD).
D6-P328-0002 §7.6.1 [body] • Full-PD : In this mode, power consumption is minimized by disabling both the CK and CA input buffers.
D6-P328-0003 §7.6.1 [body] • ODT-PD : In this mode, only the CA input buffers are disabled, while the CK buffer remains active.
D6-P328-0004 §7.6.1 [body] This configuration allows the DDR6 to support the Write NT-ODT command.
D6-P328-0005 §7.6.2 [body] Full-PD with Frequency change is entered when the PDE command is registered with C0:CA4=Land C1:CA3=H.
D6-P328-0006 §7.6.2 [body] Full-PDis entered when the PDE command is registered with C0:CA4=L.
D6-P328-0007 §7.6.2 [body] ODT-PDis entered when the PDE command is registered with C0:CA4=H.
D6-P328-0008 §7.6.2 [body] Likewise, Full-PD with Frequency change also can be entered with C1:CA3=H.
D6-P328-0009 §7.6.2 [body] Full-PDalso can be entered with Self-Refresh Entry (SRE) command with C0:CA3=H.
D6-P328-0010 §7.6.2 [body] The PDE command must not be issued while the following operations are in progress on the same PSD:
D6-P328-0015 §7.6.2 [body] Also, DDR6 SDRAM cannot be placed in Power Down state during following state and until those modes have completely terminated.
D6-P328-0016 §7.6.2 [body] (SRE with Power Down Entry command is contained in Power Down Entry command.
D6-P328-0017 §7.6.2 [body] See Command Truth Table for detail).
D6-P329-0001 §7.6.2 [body] • When any Functional Mode was enabled via MRW: MR5=2’h00 ~ 2’hxy
D6-P329-0010 §7.6.3 [body] Entering Full-PDis permitted when the PSD is in IDLE or Self-Refresh.
D6-P329-0011 §7.6.3 [body] In IDLE PD, all banks are closed and all other commands to the PSD needs to be completed.
D6-P329-0012 §7.6.3 [body] The PSD will be in an Idle state and ready to enter a Power-Down mode.
D6-P329-0013 §7.6.3 [body] Entering Full-PDdeactivates the input and output buffers, excluding CS and RESET_n which remain enabled throughout the duration of this mode.
D6-P329-0014 §7.6.3 [body] CS assertion is not permitted until the Power-Down Exit (PDX).
D6-P329-0015 §7.6.3 [body] To exit Full-PD, the host asserts CS high with the tCSH period to wake up the deactivated input buffers.
D6-P329-0016 §7.6.3 [body] After tXP_- FULL, CK Sync must be initiated.
D6-P330-0001 §7.6.3 [body] In the Full-PD with Frequency change, the DRAM flips the FSP-OP value at PDX sequence and begins referencing the FSP-related Mode Registers (MRs).
D6-P330-0002 §7.6.3 [body] Frequency change sequence is finally determined by the CS pulse-width of PDX sequence.
D6-P330-0003 §7.6.3 [body] The host shall assert CS for duration tCSH during a frequency change, and for duration tCSH_FC_- Cancel when canceling the change.
D6-P330-0004 §7.6.3 [body] Since the frequency change operation may involve VREF and ODT values, the changes will be performed after the CS is de-asserted.
D6-P330-0005 §7.6.3 [body] After the FSP MR transition, deactivated input buffers are enabled.
D6-P330-0006 §7.6.3 [body] After tXP_FC, CK sync must be initiated.
D6-P331-0001 §7.6.3 [body] Since each rank could have different ODT values in a multiple physical-rank package/module, the FSP-OP transition needs to be changed concurrently.
D6-P331-0002 §7.6.3 [body] The host initiates the FSP update sequence concurrently on all ranks by asserting CS on same clock edge.
D6-P332-0011 §7.6.4 [body] Entering ODT-PD is permitted when the PSD is in IDLE.
D6-P332-0012 §7.6.4 [body] In ODT-PD, all banks are closed and all other commands to the PSD needs to be completed.
D6-P332-0013 §7.6.4 [body] The PSD will be in an Idle state and ready to enter a Power-Down mode.
D6-P332-0014 §7.6.4 [body] Entering ODT PD deactivates the input and output buffers, excluding CS, CK_t/c, and RESET_n.
D6-P332-0015 §7.6.4 [body] While in ODT PD Mode, only Write NT ODT commands and PDX ODT commands are allowed tCPDED after ODT-PDE command.
D6-P332-0016 §7.6.4 [body] Clock input buffers CK_t and CK_c remain enabled, allowing the appropriate Write NT ODT command to be passed through and decoded while ODT PD Mode (i.e., the DRAM monitors CS and will not exit ODT PD as a result of a valid Write NT ODT command being registered).
D6-P332-0017 §7.6.4 [body] Table 1 shows the command truth table for ODT PD Mode.
D6-P332-0018 §7.6.4 [body] If CA-parity check is enabled upon entering the ODT-PD, the DRAM disables CA parity checking and operates based on CS only.
D6-P333-0001 §7.6.4 [body] manner as the command issued before entering ODT-PD mode.
D6-P333-0002 §7.6.4 [body] Upon issuance of PDX-ODT, the DRAM resumes checking CA-parity after the tXP_ODT period.
D6-P333-0011 §7.6.4 [body] When ODT PD is entered, the DRAM will be switching from decoding all CA bus command bits to only decoding CS during the tCPDED period.
D6-P333-0012 §7.6.4 [body] During tCPDED, all CA command bits must be valid when CS is asserted for a Write NT ODT command.
D6-P333-0013 §7.6.4 [body] Likewise, following the PDX_ODT command, all CA bits must be valid for Write NT ODT command also, since the DRAM will be transitioning to decoding all bits.
D6-P333-0014 §7.6.4 [body] All CA bits must be valid when CS is asserted between
D6-P334-0001 §7.6.4 [body] the PDE command and the completion of tXP_ODT upon PDX_ODT.The host shall issue Write NT ODT command tCPDED after ODT-PDE and tXP_ODT after PDX_ODT.
D6-P334-0002 §7.6.4 [table_cell] NOTE 1: tCA_PAR_Dealy is considered as Parity Latency (MR09[6:4]) when both CA parity Check and CA Parity Command Blocking(MR01[4:5]) are enabled and considered as 0 when any of them are disabled.
D6-P334-0003 §7.6.4 [table_cell] NOTE 2: PL is considered as Parity Latency (MR09[6:4]) when CA parity Check (MR01[5]) is enabled and 0 when disabled.
D6-P335-0001 §7.6.5 [body] For 2p3 or 2p6 device configurations, Full PDE and PDX can be issued to each PSD independently.
D6-P335-0002 §7.6.5 [body] CK buffer is disabled only when both PSDs enter Full PD.
D6-P335-0022 §7.6.5 [body] For 1p6 or 1p2 configurations, Full Power-down mode entry and exit is controlled with corresponding CS and CA BUS.
D6-P336-0001 §7.6.5 [body] ODT PD mode supports independent operation for each PSD.
D6-P336-0002 §7.6.5 [body] When one PSD enters ODT PD Mode, the PSD not in ODT PD Mode supports normal commands, except for the MCME and MRW commands.
D6-P337-0001 §7.7.1 [body] DDR6 supports Wake-up CS (WUCS) functionality.
D6-P337-0002 §7.7.1 [body] The feature allows the DRAM to enter a clock-gating state on the CMD/ADDR interface during periods of inactivity and to exit that state in a controlled, deterministic manner ahead of the first command of a new access sequence.
D6-P337-0003 §7.7.1 [body] When Wake-up CS is enabled, all four internal clock phases (CK/4_0°, CK/4_90°, CK/4_180°, and CK/4_270°) may be gated during idle periods, and every CS assertion acts as a wake-up event.
D6-P337-0004 §7.7.2 [body] tCS_PRE is the minimum time between the assertion of the Wake-up CS and the first valid command.
D6-P337-0005 §7.7.2 [body] tCS_PRE is an analog timing parameter.
D6-P337-0006 §7.7.2 [body] During tCS_PRE, the CMD/ADDR receivers are enabled and the device prepares to sample incoming commands.
D6-P337-0007 §7.7.2 [body] The minimum value of tCS_PRE(min) is 1.5ns.
D6-P337-0008 §7.7.2 [body] Once tCS_PRE has elapsed from the Wake-up CS assertion, the CMD/ADDR interface remains ready to sample commands indefinitely, with no clock gating applied while in the ready state.
D6-P337-0009 §7.7.3 [body] tCS_POST is a digital, programmable parameter that defines the number of clock cycles after the last command (or last CS pulse assertion) before the CMD/ADDR interface automatically returns to the power-save (clock-gating) state, provided no new command has been issued in the interim.
D6-P337-0010 §7.7.3 [body] tCS_POST is set via MRW as shown in Table 1.
D6-P337-0011 §7.7.3 [body] The host may extend the active window deterministically by issuing periodic NOP commands before tCS_POST expires.
D6-P338-0001 §7.7.3 [table_cell] NOTE: 1: The tCS_POST values of 64/128/256 tCK are an initial proposal.
D6-P338-0002 §7.7.3 [table_cell] Alternative durations (e.g., 32/64/128 tCK) may be considered.
D6-P338-0003 §7.7.4 [body] Wake-up CS settings shall only be modified while the device is in MRUPDATE mode.
D6-P338-0004 §7.7.4 [body] Upon issuing the MCME command, Wake-up CS functionality is automatically disabled after tMCME_Delay.
D6-P338-0005 §7.7.4 [body] Wake-up CS is re-enabled following MRUPDATE mode exit, using either the previous settings (if unchanged) or the newly programmed settings (if reprogrammed during MRUPDATE).
D6-P338-0006 §7.7.5 [body] The Wake-up CS sequence operates as follows:
D6-P338-0007 §7.7.5 [body] 1) After power-up or reset, Wake-up CS functionality is disabled.
D6-P338-0008 §7.7.5 [body] 2) The host shall enable Wake-up CS operation by programming the associated mode register field to a non-default value.
D6-P338-0009 §7.7.5 [body] The default MR setting 00b disables the feature.
D6-P338-0010 §7.7.5 [body] 3) When Wake-up CS is enabled, the DRAM may place the CMD/ADDR receive path into a power-saving state after tCS_POST of command inactivity.
D6-P338-0011 §7.7.5 [body] 4) Prior to issuing a command while the CMD/ADDR receive path is in the power-saving state, the host shall issue a Wake-up CS pulse.
D6-P338-0012 §7.7.5 [body] 5) The first valid command shall not be issued until at least tCS_PRE(min) after the rising edge of the Wake-up CS pulse.
D6-P338-0013 §7.7.5 [body] 6) Following a Wake-up CS pulse, the CMD/ADDR receive path shall remain in the active-ready state and capable of accepting commands.
D6-P338-0014 §7.7.5 [body] 7) Any command CS assertion occurring while the receive path is already active will also reset the tCS_POST activity timer.
D6-P338-0015 §7.7.5 [body] 8) If no command activity occurs for tCS_POST after the previous command, the CMD/ADDR receive path may automatically return to the power-saving state.
D6-P339-0001 §7.8.1 [body] The Mode Register Read (MRR) command is used to read configuration and status data from the DDR6 SDRAM registers.
D6-P339-0002 §7.8.1 [body] The MRR command is initiated with CS and CA [5:0] in the proper state as defined by the Command Truth Table.
D6-P339-0003 §7.8.1 [body] The mode register address operands (MA [7:0]) allow the user to select one of the available Mode Registers (MRs).
D6-P339-0004 §7.8.1 [body] MRR has a command burst length (BL) 24.
D6-P339-0005 §7.8.1 [body] The mode register contents are available at the start of 8th and ends at 23rd UI data bits of DQ [2:0] after RL + tCK2DQO following the MRR command.
D6-P339-0006 §7.8.1 [body] Every Opcode, OP [7:0], is available for 2 UI lengths as shown in Table 1-6.
D6-P339-0007 §7.8.1 [body] The minimum MRR to MRR command period is defined by tMRR.
D6-P339-0008 §7.8.1 [body] The minimum MRR command to Any command period is defined by tMRD as shown in Table 7.
D6-P339-0009 §7.8.1 [body] The truth table for MRR is defined in Table 8.
D6-P339-0010 §7.8.1 [body] To avoid a potentially worst-case pattern, DQ1 bits shall have their contents inverted relative to DQ0 and DQ2 (represented with “!”).
D6-P339-0011 §7.8.1 [body] The data in the burst (BL0-7) shall be either “0” or “1”, with “1” indicating that the content of the later UIs (BL8-23) is inverted.
D6-P339-0012 §7.8.1 [body] Subsequent data bits DQ [5:3] for 2p6, 1p6 and DQ [11:6] for 1p12 configuration contain valid but undefined content.
D6-P339-0013 §7.8.1 [body] DQS is toggled for the duration of the Mode Register Read burst.
D6-P339-0014 §7.8.1.1 [body] The timing diagram below depicts the tMRR and tMRD timing for MRR command to MRR command and MRR to 6-cycle MRW command to PSD specific MR or any other valid.
D6-P339-0022 §7.8.1.1 [body] NOTE: 1: Only DES command is allowed during tMRR and tMRD periods.
D6-P339-0023 §7.8.1.2 [body] For MRR, 2pX and 1pX operate differently for Shared MR and PSD MR accesses.
D6-P339-0025 §7.8.1.2 [body] • Each PSD reads its own PSD MR.
D6-P339-0026 §7.8.1.2 [body] • The corresponding OP values are output through the DQ pins associated with that PSD.
D6-P339-0028 §7.8.1.2 [body] • Shared MR Read: The OP values are output through all DQs, with the data replicated in 3-DQ groups.
D6-P339-0029 §7.8.1.2 [body] • PSD MR Read: Each PSD's OP values are output through the DQ pins associated with that PSD.
D6-P340-0002 §7.8.1.2 [table_cell] NOTE: Option is to repeat the pattern on DQ3-5, V = Valid
D6-P342-0002 §7.8.1.2 [table_cell] NOTE: Option is to repeat the pattern on DQ3-11, V = Valid

D6-P344-0001 §7.8.2 [body] The Mode Register Write (MRW) command is used to write configuration data to the mode registers.
D6-P344-0002 §7.8.2 [body] The MRW command is initiated by setting CS and CA [5:0] to valid levels as defined in Command Truth Table.
D6-P344-0003 §7.8.2 [body] The mode register address operands (MA [7:0]) allow the user to select one of available Mode Registers (MRs).
D6-P344-0004 §7.8.2 [body] The Mode Register Write command is an MRW 6-cycle long command.

D6-P344-0008 §7.8.2 [body] The Mode Register Write command to read-only registers has no impact on the functionality of the device.

D6-P346-0001 §7.8.3 [body] The Mode Register Update (MRUPDATE) Mode performs the equivalent function as the Mode Register Write (MRW) command, but without the command bits, saving two Command/Address (CA) cycles for every Mode Register write operation.
D6-P346-0002 §7.8.3 [body] MRUPDATE is multi-cycle CS per C/A cycle as shown in the first figure below, as compared to the single-cycle CS MRW command.
D6-P346-0003 §7.8.3.1 [body] MRUPDATE Mode is entered by issuing an MCME command, which also enables multi-cycle CS.
D6-P346-0004 §7.8.3.1 [body] Entering MRUPDATE Mode resets the starting reference point for the DRAM to know that all subsequent commands received will be a function of four (4) CA cycles.
D6-P346-0005 §7.8.3.1 [body] The 4 CA cycles are shown in the table below.
D6-P346-0006 §7.8.3.1 [table_cell] NOTE 1: CA4 is the control bit for the MRUPDATE.
D6-P346-0007 §7.8.3.1 [table_cell] CA4 must be HIGH for all four MRUPDATE cycles for the MRUPDATE to be executed.
D6-P346-0008 §7.8.3.1 [table_cell] When CA4 of all four MRUPDATE cycles is LOW, MRUPDATE mode is disabled/exited.
D6-P346-0009 §7.8.3.1 [table_cell] The MRUPDATE will not be performed with any CA4 mixed combination of HIGH and LOW.
D6-P346-0010 §7.8.3.1 [table_cell] NOTE 2: CS behavior is shown in the MRUPDATE Timing diagram.
D6-P346-0011 §7.8.3.2 [body] For the device to latch the MRUPATE in cases where the alignment between CS, CA and CK may be unknown, each CA cycle must meet the required timings.
D6-P346-0012 §7.8.3.2 [body] The CA inputs must reach the proper command state and provide tMC_Setup of setup prior to CS transitioning from LOW to HIGH, CS must remain HIGH for tMC_CSPW, and the CA inputs must remain in the proper command state and provide tMC_Hold of hold after CS transitions from HIGH to LOW.
D6-P347-0001 §7.8.3.2 [body] Following the fourth (4th) CA cycle, tMRUPD_Delay needs to be met to latch the data into the Mode Register prior to starting the next MRUPATE.
D6-P347-0014 §7.8.3.2 [body] NOTE: CA4=HIGH to execute the MRUPDATE.
D6-P347-0015 §7.8.3.2 [body] CA4=LOW to disable/exit MRUPDATE mode.
D6-P347-0020 §7.8.3.3 [body] MRUPDATE Mode is disabled by executing four cycles of MRUPDATE with CA4=LOW.
D6-P347-0021 §7.8.3.3 [body] The MRA and OP bits states may be any combination of HIGH and LOW.
D6-P347-0022 §7.8.3.3 [body] Disabling MRUPDATE will also disable multi-cycle CS.
D6-P348-0001 §7.9.1 [body] On-Die Termination (ODT) is a feature of the DDR6 SDRAM that allows the DRAM to change termination resistance for each DQ and DQS.
D6-P348-0002 §7.9.1 [body] The ODT feature is designed to improve signal integrity of the memory channel by allowing the DRAM controller to independently change termination resistance for any or all DRAM devices.
D6-P348-0003 §7.9.1 [body] In addition to the control capability of the DQ ODT, the DQS ODT can be independently programmed via MR63:OP[5:3].
D6-P348-0004 §7.9.1 [body] All ODT control is command & mode register based.
D6-P348-0005 §7.9.1 [body] ODT is enabled via Write and non-target Write commands, disabled via Read and Mode Register Read commands, or set to the default “PARK” value through settings in MR63-MR66.
D6-P348-0006 §7.9.1 [body] A simple functional representation of the DRAM ODT feature is shown in Figure 1.
D6-P348-0009 §7.9.1 [body] To other Circuitry Like RCV, ...
D6-P348-0012 §7.9.1 [body] The switch is enabled by the internal ODT control logic, which uses command decode, Mode Register Settings and other control information, see below.
D6-P348-0013 §7.9.1 [body] The value of RTT is determined by the settings of Mode Register bits.
D6-P348-0014 §7.9.1.1 [body] The ODT Mode of DDR6 SDRAM has four states: Data/Strobe Termination Disable, RTT_WR, RTT_NT_WR and RTT_PARK/DQS_RTT_PARK.
D6-P348-0015 §7.9.1.1 [body] The ODT Mode is enabled based on Mode Registers for each RTT listed below.
D6-P348-0016 §7.9.1.1 [body] In this case, the value of RTT is determined by the settings of these mode register bits.
D6-P348-0017 §7.9.1.1 [body] Application: Controller can control each RTT condition with Write (WR), Read (RD) or Mode Register Read (MRR) commands and use of ODT Offset Control Mode Registers.
D6-P348-0018 §7.9.1.1 [body] • Data Termination Disable: DRAM driving data upon receiving Read command disables the termination after RL-2-8UI, and re-enables the termination at RL + BL/2 (RL and BL/2 defined in clock cycles).
D6-P348-0019 §7.9.1.1 [body] • Strobe Termination Disable: DRAM driving strobe upon receiving Read command disables the termination after RL-2-tRPRE-(Read_DQS_Pre-shift) and re-enables the termination at RL+BL/2- 0.5+tRPST-(Read_D- QS_Pre-shift) (RL and BL/2 defined in clock cycles).
D6-P348-0020 §7.9.1.1 [body] • RTT_WR: For the rank that is being written to provide termination, it adjusts timing based on ODT Control Mode Register settings.
D6-P348-0021 §7.9.1.1 [body] • RTT_NT_WR: DRAM applies RTT_NT_WR if it sees a CS asserted during the third and fourth pulse of the Write command, and it adjusts timing based on ODT Control Mode Register settings.
D6-P349-0001 §7.9.1.1 [body] • RTT_PARK: Default parked value set via MR63:OP[2:0]; enabled when a Read or Write is not active.
D6-P349-0002 §7.9.1.1 [body] • DQS_RTT_PARK: Default parked value for DQS set via MR63:OP[5:3]; enabled when a Read is not active.
D6-P349-0003 §7.9.1.1 [body] Those RTT values have priority as follows:
D6-P349-0008 §7.9.1.1 [body] This means if there is a Write command, then the DRAM turns on RTT_WR, not RTT_NT_WR, and also if there is a Read command, then the DRAM disables data termination and goes into driving mode.
D6-P349-0009 §7.9.1.1 [body] If CS is driven high during the third and fourth cycle of a Write command, then RTT_NT_WR is enabled for the non-target rank.
D6-P349-0010 §7.9.1.1 [body] This provides additional and potentially different termination options for the other ranks on the channel.
D6-P349-0011 §7.9.1.1 [table_cell] NOTE 1: When a Read command is executed, the DRAM termination state of the target rank will be Hi-Z for a defined period independent of MR setting of RTT_PARK/RTT_WR/RTT_NT_WR.
D6-P349-0012 §7.9.1.1 [table_cell] NOTE 2: If RTT_WR is enabled, RTT_WR will be activated by a Write command for a defined period of time independent of the MR setting of RTT_PARK/RTT_WR/RTT_NT_WR.
D6-P349-0013 §7.9.1.1 [table_cell] NOTE 3: If RTT_PARK is enabled, DRAM RTT_PARK termination will be enabled while WR/RD/MRR are not being executed.
D6-P349-0014 §7.9.1.1 [table_cell] NOTE 4: When a Non-Target ODT command is executed and the RTT_NT_WR is disabled, the DRAM termination state of the non-target rank will be Hi-Z for a defined period, independent of the MR setting of RTT_PARK.
D6-P350-0001 §7.9.1.1 [table_cell] NOTE 1: If RTT_DQS_PARK is enabled, DRAM RTT_DQS_PARK termination will be enabled while RD/MRR are not being executed.
D6-P350-0002 §7.9.1.2 [body] Dynamic ODT is the feature that allows the DDR6 SDRAM to switch between pre-programmed ODT termination states in response to command activity without requiring MRW during normal traffic.
D6-P350-0004 §7.9.1.2 [body] The function is described as follows:
D6-P350-0005 §7.9.1.2 [body] • Four RTT values are available: RTT_NT_WR, RTT_WR, RTT_PARK and DQS_RTT_PARK. – The value for RTT_NT_WR is preselected via MR64:OP[5:3]. – The value for RTT_WR is preselected via MR64:OP[2:0]. – The value for RTT_PARK is preselected via MR63:OP[2:0]. – The value for DQS_RTT_PARK is preselected via MR63:OP[5:3].
D6-P350-0006 §7.9.1.2 [body] • During operation without RD, MRR, WR, Non-target WR commands, the termination is controlled as follows: – RTT_PARK is selected for DQ – DQS_RTT_PARK is selected for DQS.
D6-P350-0007 §7.9.1.2 [body] • When a Write command (WR) is registered, the termination is controlled as follows: – A latency ODTLon_WR after the Write command, termination strength RTT_WR is selected. – A latency ODTLoff_WR after the Write command, termination strength RTT_WR is de-selected.
D6-P350-0008 §7.9.1.2 [body] • The termination, RTT_NT_WR, for the non-target Write command is selected and de-selected by latencies ODTLon_WR_NT and ODTLoff_WR_NT, respectively.
D6-P350-0009 §7.9.1.2 [body] • When a Read command (RD) is registered, the termination is controlled as follows: – A latency ODTLoff_RD after the Read command, data termination is disabled.
D6-P350-0010 §7.9.1.2 [body] Then, ODTLon_RD after the Read command, data termination is enabled. – A latency ODTLoff_RD_DQS after the Read command, strobe termination is disabled.
D6-P350-0011 §7.9.1.2 [body] ODTLon_RD_DQS after the Read command, strobe termination is enabled.
D6-P350-0012 §7.9.1.2 [body] To achieve the minimum write burst duration, ODTLoff_X and ODTLon_X latencies contain independent programmable mode register offsets:
D6-P350-0013 §7.9.1.2 [body] • The values for the Write command ODT control offsets are preselected via MR65.
D6-P351-0002 §7.9.1.2 [body] • The values for the non-target Write command ODT control offsets are preselected via MR66. – MR66:OP[2:0] preselects ODTLon_WR_NT_Offset – MR66:OP[5:3] preselects ODTLoff_WR_NT_Offset
D6-P351-0003 §7.9.1.2 [body] The duration of a Write or Read command is a full burst cycle, BL/2.
D6-P351-0004 §7.9.1.2 [body] The termination select ("ODTLon_...") and de-select ("ODTLoff_...") latency settings shall not result in an ODT pulse width which violates a burst cycle (BL/2) minimum duration.
D6-P351-0005 §7.9.1.2 [body] The equation "ODTLoff_X - ODTLon_X >= BL/2” must be met, where X is the termination latency setting associated with a particular command type (WR, WR_NT).

D6-P354-0008 §7.9.1.2 [body] tODTQon(min) and tODTQon(max) are minimum and maximum RTT change timing skews between different RTT states with respect to the DQ, DQS toggle end point.

D6-P355-0009 §7.9.1.2 [body] tODTon is measured after the corresponding ODT turn-on latency (ODTLon_WR or ODTLon_NT_WR) from the Write command has been satisfied.

D6-P358-0001 §7.9.1.4 [body] ZQ=120ohm, entire operation temperature range, after proper ZQ calibration.
D6-P359-0001 §7.9.1.4 [table_cell] NOTE 1: The tolerance limits are specified after calibration with stable voltage and temperature.

D6-P360-0001 §7.9.2 [body] DDR6 devices feature a temperature sensor whose status (MR04:OP[2:0]) can be read to determine the minimum required refresh rate.
D6-P360-0002 §7.9.2 [body] Either the temperature sensor readout or the device TOPER may be used by the system to determine whether the refresh rate and operating temperature requirements are being met.
D6-P360-0003 §7.9.2 [body] Additionally, the device’s temperature status (MR04:OP[2:0]) can be read by the system to determine the current approximate temperature range sensed by the device.
D6-P360-0004 §7.9.2 [body] DDR6 devices shall monitor device temperature and update MR4 according to tTSI.
D6-P360-0005 §7.9.2 [body] Upon completion of device initialization, the device temperature status bits shall be no older than tTSI.
D6-P360-0006 §7.9.2 [body] MR4 will be updated even when device is in Self Refresh state.
D6-P360-0007 §7.9.2 [body] When using the temperature sensor, the actual device case temperature may be higher than the TOPER specification that applies for the standard or elevated temperature ranges.
D6-P360-0008 §7.9.2 [body] For example, TCASE may be above 85°C when MR04:OP[2:0]=100B.
D6-P360-0009 §7.9.2 [body] DDR6 devices shall allow for 2°C temperature margin between the point at which the device updates the MR04 value and the point at which the controller reconfigures the system accordingly.
D6-P360-0010 §7.9.2 [body] Each MR04[2:0] setting specifies a nominal temperature range, and the six ranges defined by MR04[2:0] are determined by five temperature thresholds which will be nominally 75°C, 80°C, 85°C, 90°C, and 95°C.
D6-P360-0011 §7.9.2 [body] The 3rd threshold (nominally 85°C) is used by the system to switch to 2x refresh.
D6-P360-0012 §7.9.2 [body] The 5th threshold (nominally 95°C) is used by the system to throttle activity to keep the DRAM at a safe operating temperature.
D6-P360-0013 §7.9.2 [body] The 2nd threshold (nominally 80°C) allows the system to take actions which delay reaching the 3rd threshold.
D6-P360-0014 §7.9.2 [body] The 4rd threshold (nominally 90 °C) allows the system to take actions which delay reaching the 5th threshold.
D6-P360-0015 §7.9.2 [body] The 1st threshold (nominally 75°C) has no minimum value specified and the 5th threshold (nominally 95°C) has no maximum temperature value specified.
D6-P360-0016 §7.9.2 [body] To ensure proper operation using the temperature sensor, applications should consider the following factors:
D6-P360-0017 §7.9.2 [body] • TempGradient is the maximum temperature gradient experienced by the memory device at the temperature of interest over a range of 2°C, measured in the range of interest 75-95°C.
D6-P360-0018 §7.9.2 [body] • ReadInterval is the maximum time period between Temperature Sensor mode register bits being read by the system.
D6-P360-0019 §7.9.2 [body] • TempSensorInterval (tTSI) is the maximum delay between internal updates of the Temperature Sensor mode register bits.
D6-P360-0020 §7.9.2 [body] • SysRespDelay is the maximum time between a read of the Temperature Sensor mode register bits and the response by the system.
D6-P361-0001 §7.9.2 [body] For example, if TempGradient is 10°C/s and the SysRespDelay is 100 ms:
D6-P361-0003 §7.9.2 [body] In this case, ReadInterval shall be no greater than 68 ms.
D6-P361-0012 §7.9.2.1 [body] DDR6 provides temperature sensor readout information as reference.
D6-P361-0013 §7.9.2.1 [body] Temperature is not located in the same area as memory array.
D6-P361-0014 §7.9.2.1 [body] Only limited position temperature is measured.
D6-P361-0015 §7.9.2.1 [body] DDR temperature sensor cannot provide accurate enough temperature due to circuits and placement.
D6-P361-0016 §7.9.2.1 [body] Host can use this information as temperature gradient information.
D6-P361-0017 §7.9.2.1 [body] Temperature accuracy is not guaranteed by design values.
D6-P361-0018 §7.9.2.1 [body] MR56 is assigned for temperature sensor report.
D6-P361-0019 §7.9.2.1 [body] Please refer MR56 definition for more detailed information.
D6-P361-0020 §7.9.2.1 [body] Mode register read out shows on-die temperature sensor value.
D6-P361-0021 §7.9.2.2 [body] The host may provide a temperature-offset to the DDR6 SDRAM, allowing the memory’s self-refresh timing or the 2x-refresh decision point to be adjusted for reliable operation.
D6-P361-0022 §7.9.2.2 [body] This offset is programmed via the appropriate MR (e.g., MR04:OP[7:5]).
D6-P361-0023 §7.9.2.2 [body] Once applied, the DRAM’s Temperature-Compensated Self-Refresh (TCSR) circuit uses the adjusted temperature reference to modify the refresh interval as required, and the update is reflected in the refresh-control field (e.g. MR04:OP[2:0]) within approximately 200 µs.

D6-P364-0003 §7.9.2.3 [table_cell] NOTE 2: The 2x Refresh Rate must be provided by the system before the DRAM Tj has gone up by more than 2°C (Temperature Margin) since the first report out of MR04[2:0]=100.

D6-P364-0014 §7.9.2.3 [table_cell] NOTE 8: The two PSDs (PSD0 and PSD1) on a single die share the same thermal sensor, therefore the thermal offset value in MR04[7:5] is applied to the common thermal sensor
D6-P365-0001 §7.9.3 [body] As voltage and temperature change on the SDRAM die, the DQS and CLK clock tree delay will shift and may require re-training.
D6-P365-0002 §7.9.3 [body] The DDR6-SDRAM includes an internal DQS and CLK clock tree oscillator to measure the amount of delay over a given time interval (determined by the controller), allowing the controller to compare the trained delay value to the delay value seen at a later time.
D6-P365-0003 §7.9.3 [body] The DQS and CLK interval oscillator will provide the controller with important information regarding the need to re-train, and the magnitude of potential error.
D6-P365-0004 §7.9.3 [body] The DQS and CLK interval oscillator is started by issuing an MRW MR05 [Start DQS/CLK Interval Osc] command as described in the Mode Register Functional Modes section, which will start an internal ring oscillator that counts the number of times a signal propagates through a replica of the DQS or CLK clock tree.
D6-P365-0005 §7.9.3 [body] The DQS and CLK interval oscillator may be stopped by issuing an MRW MR05 [Stop DQS/CLK Interval Osc] command as described in the MRW Functional Mode section, or the controller may instruct the SDRAM to count for a specific number of clocks and then stop automatically (See MR45xx,46xx, and 47xx for more information).If MR20xx is set to automatically stop the DQS and CLK interval oscillator, then the MRW MR05 [Stop DQS/CLK Interval Osc] command shall not be used.
D6-P365-0006 §7.9.3 [body] Similarly, if MR23 is set to automatically stop the CLK interval oscillator, then the MRW MR05 [Stop CLK Interval Osc] command shall not be used.
D6-P365-0007 §7.9.3 [body] When the DQS and CLK interval oscillator is stopped by either method, the result of the oscillator counter is automatically stored in MR1Exx/MR1Fxx and MR21xx/MR22xx
D6-P365-0008 §7.9.3 [body] MRW commands to MR20xx during an ongoing DQS/CLK interval oscillator operation are not permitted.
D6-P365-0009 §7.9.3 [body] MRW commands to MR23 during an ongoing CLK interval oscillator operation are not permitted.MR20xxand MR23may be reprogrammed after the tMRD delay following the automatic oscillator stop.
D6-P365-0010 §7.9.3 [body] DQS interval oscillator and CLK interval oscillator cannot be operated simultaneously.
D6-P365-0011 §7.9.3 [body] After completing DQS (or CLK) interval oscillator operation, MRW MR05 [Start CLK (or DQS) Interval Osc] command can be issued.
D6-P365-0012 §7.9.3 [body] The DRAM shall respond in one of two ways if an MRW MR05 [Start DQS/CLK Interval Osc] is issued during an ongoing DQS/CLK interval oscillator operation (automatic or manual).
D6-P365-0013 §7.9.3 [body] One option is the DRAM will ignore the concurrent start command, in which case the DQS/CLK interval oscillator operation will proceed as normal with all timing constraints referencing the original start command.
D6-P365-0014 §7.9.3 [body] The other option is for the DRAM to restart the DQS interval oscillator operation, in which case all timing constraints will reference the most recent (subsequent) start command.
D6-P365-0015 §7.9.3 [body] The host must account for the worst-case option in the event that a concurrent start command is issued.
D6-P365-0016 §7.9.3 [body] If issuing a concurrent MRW MR05 [Start DQS/CLK Interval Osc] results in a loss of run time tracking, the results stored in MR1Exx/MR1F and MR21xx/MR22 should be ignored and the full operation restarted.
D6-P365-0017 §7.9.3 [body] Entering Full Power-down during an ongoing DQS/CLK interval oscillator operation is permitted.
D6-P365-0018 §7.9.3 [body] Upon exiting Full Power-down, an operation started in automatic mode shall be allowed to complete naturally based upon the specified number of clocks (cumulative before and after Full Power-down).
D6-P365-0019 §7.9.3 [body] If a manual operation started prior to entering Full Power-down is not stopped prior as well, the operation shall be manually stopped upon Full Power-down exit.
D6-P365-0020 §7.9.3 [body] The results stored in MR1Exx/MR1Fxx and MR21xx/MR22xx for a DQS and CLK interval oscillator operation that spans Full Power-down entry/exit should be ignored and the full operation restarted.
D6-P365-0021 §7.9.3 [body] The controller may adjust the accuracy of the result by running the DQS/CLK interval oscillator for shorter (less accurate) or longer (more accurate) duration.
D6-P365-0022 §7.9.3 [body] The accuracy of the result for a given temperature and voltage is determined by the following equation:

D6-P366-0019 §7.9.3 [body] The result of the DQS/CLK interval oscillator is defined as the number of DQS/CLK clock tree delays that can be counted within the "run time," determined by the controller.
D6-P366-0020 §7.9.3 [body] The result for DQSCLK is stored in MR1Exx and MR1Fxx, and that for CLK is also stored in MR21xx and MR22xx.
D6-P366-0021 §7.9.3 [body] MR1Exx and MR21xx contain the least significant bits (LSB) of the result for DQS and CLK, respectively.
D6-P366-0022 §7.9.3 [body] MR1Fxx and MR22xx contain the most significant bits (MSB) of the result for DQS and CLK, respectively.
D6-P366-0023 §7.9.3 [body] MR1Exx and MR1Fxx are overwritten by the SDRAM when an MRW MR05 [Stop DQS Interval Osc] command is received.
D6-P366-0024 §7.9.3 [body] Similarly, for a CLK interval oscillator stop command, MR21xx and MR22xx are also overwritten when SDRAM receives MRW MR05 [Stop CLK Interval Osc] command.
D6-P366-0025 §7.9.3 [body] The SDRAM counter will count to its maximum value (=2^16) and stop.
D6-P366-0026 §7.9.3 [body] If the maximum value is read from the mode registers, then the memory controller must assume that the counter overflowed the register and discard the result.

D6-P370-0003 §7.9.3 [table_cell] NOTE 2: This parameter will be characterized or guaranteed by design.
D6-P370-0004 §7.9.3 [table_cell] NOTE 3: The WOSCMatch and ROSCMatch are defined as the following: WOSCMatch = [tRX_DQS2DQtD- QS2DQi(V, T) - tDQSOSC(V, T) - WOSCoffset] Where tRX_DQS2DQtDQS2DQi(V,T), tDQSOSC(V,T) and WOSCOffset(V,T) are determined over the same voltage and temp conditions.
D6-P370-0005 §7.9.3 [table_cell] ROSCMatch = [tTX_- CLK2DQtCK2DQo(V, T) - tCLKOSC(V, T) - ROSCOffset] Where tTX_CLK2DQtCK2DQo(V,T), tCLKOSC(V,T) and ROSCOffset(V,T) are determined over the same voltage and temp conditions.
D6-P370-0006 §7.9.3 [table_cell] NOTE 4: The runtime of the oscillator must be at least 200ns for determining tDQSOSC(V,T) and tCLKOSC(V,T).
D6-P370-0007 §7.9.3 [table_cell] NOTE 5: The input stimulus for tRX_DQS2DQtDQS2DQi and tTX_CLK2DQtCK2DQo will be consistent over voltage and temp conditions.
D6-P370-0008 §7.9.3 [table_cell] NOTE 6: The WOSCOffset is the average difference of the endpoints across voltage and temp.
D6-P370-0009 §7.9.3 [table_cell] NOTE 7: tRX_DQS2DQtDQS2DQi(V,T) delay will be the average of DQS to DQ delay over the runtime period.
D6-P370-0010 §7.9.3 [table_cell] tTX_CLK2DQtCK2DQo(V,T) delay will be the average of CLK to DQ delay over the runtime period.
D6-P370-0011 §7.9.3 [table_cell] NOTE 8: The matching error and offset of WOSC came from DQS interval oscillator.
D6-P370-0012 §7.9.3 [table_cell] NOTE 9: The matching error and offset of ROSC came from CLK interval oscillator.
