D6-P378-0001 §7.9.4 [body] DDR6 supports three functions of Post Package Repair (PPR) for failed row addresses: Hard Post Package Repair (hPPR) and MBIST PPR (mPPR) for permanent row repair, and Soft Post Package Repair (sPPR) for temporary row repair.
D6-P378-0002 §7.9.4 [body] mPPR is to be used in conjunction with the MBIST function to repair failing addresses identified during MBIST.
D6-P378-0003 §7.9.4.1 [body] Access to hPPR, sPPR, MBIST, and mPPR modes is protected through a sequential MRW guard key (‘PPR guard key’), which prevents unintended PPR programming.
D6-P378-0004 §7.9.4.1 [body] The sequential MRW guard key is the same for hPPR, sPPR, MBIST and mPPR.
D6-P378-0005 §7.9.4.1 [body] The PPR guard key requires a sequence of four MRW commands to be issued immediately after issuing the MRW to enter PPR or MBIST.
D6-P378-0006 §7.9.4.1 [body] The guard key sequence is entered in the specified order as stated below and shown in the guard key encoding for the MR30 table.
D6-P378-0007 §7.9.4.1 [body] Interruptions to the guard key sequence by other MRW/MRR commands or non-MR commands (e.g., ACT, WR, RD, REFab, REFdb, REFqb) are prohibited.
D6-P378-0008 §7.9.4.1 [body] Although interruption of the guard key entry is not allowed, if the guard key is not entered in the required order or is interrupted by other commands, hPPR/sPPR/MBIST/mPPR entry will not execute and the offending command that interrupted the PPR guard key may or may not execute correctly.
D6-P378-0009 §7.9.4.1 [body] However, the offending command will not cause the device to lock up and subsequent ACT and WR commands will be conducted as normal DRAM commands.
D6-P378-0010 §7.9.4.1 [body] The DRAM does not provide an error indication if an incorrect PPR guard key sequence is entered.
D6-P378-0015 §7.9.4.1 [body] Time Break Any interruptions of the guard key sequencefrom other MRW/R commands or non-MR commandssuch as ACT, WR, RD is not allowed.
D6-P378-0016 §7.9.4.1 [body] NOTE: Only DES commands are allowed during tMRD.
D6-P379-0001 §7.9.4.2 [body] hPPR resources are allocated per bank in the PSD Mode Registers.
D6-P379-0002 §7.9.4.2 [body] The hPPR/sPPR resource designation (MRBF-MRC6) will indicate the hPPR resource availability and should be read/checked prior to attempting a repair.
D6-P379-0003 §7.9.4.2 [body] It is important to note that hPPR repairs are permanent.
D6-P379-0004 §7.9.4.2 [table_cell] 00: More than one hPPR and one single sPPR resource are available.
D6-P379-0005 §7.9.4.2 [table_cell] Transition from 00 after hPPR is executed, or from 10 after sPPR Undo is executed, and more than one hPPR resource remains.
D6-P379-0006 §7.9.4.2 [table_cell] 01: One single hPPR and sPPR shared resource is available.
D6-P379-0007 §7.9.4.2 [table_cell] Transition from 00 after hPPR is executed, or from 10 after sPPR Undo is executed, and one single hPPR resource remains.
D6-P379-0008 §7.9.4.2 [table_cell] 10: hPPR resource is available and sPPR resource is not available.
D6-P379-0009 §7.9.4.2 [table_cell] Transition from 00 or 01 after sPPR is executed.
D6-P379-0010 §7.9.4.2 [table_cell] 11: hPPR and sPPR resource is not available.
D6-P379-0011 §7.9.4.2 [table_cell] Transition from 01 or 10 after hPPR is executed and no hPPR resources remain.
D6-P379-0012 §7.9.4.3 [body] Entry into hPPR is through a mode register enable and an ACT command is used to transmit the bank and row address of the row to be repaired.
D6-P379-0013 §7.9.4.3 [body] For DRAM configured as 2p3 or 2p6, an hPPR shall only be done on one PSD at a time and the other PSD shall remain idle.
D6-P379-0014 §7.9.4.3 [body] After tRCD time, a WRA command is used to select the individual DRAM through the DQ bits and to transfer the repair address to the DRAM.
D6-P379-0015 §7.9.4.3 [body] Following the program time and a PREpb command, exit hPPR through the mode register disable and resume normal operation after tPGMPST_hPPR.
D6-P379-0017 §7.9.4.3 [body] 1) Verify hPPR resource availability by issuing an MRR to read the hPPR/sPPR resource designation registers in the PSDMode Registers (MRBF-MRC6).
D6-P379-0018 §7.9.4.3 [body] If no repair resource is available (11, hPPR and sPPR resource is not available), the host controller should not initiate hPPR mode.
D6-P379-0019 §7.9.4.3 [body] 2) Before entering hPPR mode, precharge all banks and ensure they are idle; disable CRC mode.
D6-P380-0001 §7.9.4.3 [body] 3) Enable hPPR by setting MR32[0] = 1 and wait tMRD.
D6-P380-0002 §7.9.4.3 [body] 4) Issue the guard key as four consecutive MR30[7:0] MRW commands as described above.
D6-P380-0003 §7.9.4.3 [body] 5) Issue an ACT command with the BG, Bank and Row fail address; wait tRCD.
D6-P380-0004 §7.9.4.3 [body] 6) Issue a WRA command with a valid column address; wait WL.
D6-P380-0005 §7.9.4.3 [body] The DRAM will ignore the column address.
D6-P380-0006 §7.9.4.3 [body] 7) After WL, drive DQ[2:0] HIGH for 12tCK on the target DRAM.
D6-P380-0007 §7.9.4.3 [body] For non-target DRAMs, drive DQ[2:0] LOW for 12tCK.
D6-P380-0008 §7.9.4.3 [body] If DQ[2:0] data bits are neither all LOW nor all HIGH for 12tCK, then hPPR mode execution is unknown.
D6-P380-0009 §7.9.4.3 [body] For 2p6, 1p6 and 1p12 devices, data bits other than DQ[2:0] are don't cares.
D6-P380-0010 §7.9.4.3 [body] 8) Wait tPGM_hPPRa or tPGM_hPPRb for internal repair, then issue PREpb.
D6-P380-0011 §7.9.4.3 [body] 9) Wait tPGM_EXIT_hPPR after PREpb command to enable recognition of the repaired row address.
D6-P380-0012 §7.9.4.3 [body] 10) Exit hPPR by setting MR32[0] = 0 and wait tPGMPST_hPPR.
D6-P380-0013 §7.9.4.3 [body] 11) Resume normal operation.
D6-P380-0014 §7.9.4.3 [body] 12) When repairing more than one failed address in a bank with more than one hPPR resource available, repeat steps 3–10.
D6-P380-0015 §7.9.4.3 [body] Otherwise, repeat steps 1-10 for repairs in other banks.
D6-P380-0016 §7.9.4.3 [body] During hPPR mode, REFab/REFdb commands are allowed, but array contents are not guaranteed.
D6-P380-0017 §7.9.4.3 [body] Upon receiving a REFab or REFdb command in hPPR mode, the DRAM may ignore the Refresh operation but will not disrupt the repair operation.
D6-P380-0018 §7.9.4.3 [body] Other commands except REFab/REFdb during tPGM_hPPR can cause incomplete repair so no other
D6-P381-0001 §7.9.4.3 [body] After exiting hPPR mode (MR32:OP[0] = 0 after tPGMPST_hPPR), the host can verify the repaired row by writing data to the target row and subsequently reading it back.
D6-P381-0002 §7.9.4.3 [body] command except REF is allowed during tPGM_hPPR.
D6-P381-0016 §7.9.4.3 [body] NOTE: 1: With one hPPR command, only one row can be repaired at one time.
D6-P381-0017 §7.9.4.3 [body] NOTE: 2: Allow REFab/REFdb/REFqb from WL+12tCK+tWR+tRP after WR, but does not guarantee array contents are refreshed during hPPR.
D6-P381-0018 §7.9.4.3 [body] NOTE: 3: Timing diagram shows possible commands but not all shown can be issued at the same time.
D6-P381-0019 §7.9.4.3 [body] All regular timings must still be satisfied.
D6-P383-0001 §7.9.5 [body] Soft Post Package Repair (sPPR) enables rapid, temporary repair of a single row address per bank on DDR6 DRAM devices.
D6-P383-0002 §7.9.5 [body] In contrast, Hard Post Package Repair (hPPR) provides permanent row repair with a longer execution time.
D6-P383-0003 §7.9.5 [body] sPPR and hPPR differ in their operational limitations and intended use cases.
D6-P383-0004 §7.9.5 [body] The host controller must ensure that refresh requirements for all other rows are maintained during sPPR operations.
D6-P383-0005 §7.9.5 [body] sPPR information is retained as long as VDD remains within the operating range.
D6-P383-0006 §7.9.5 [body] If power is removed or the device is RESET, the sPPR will revert to the unrepaired state.
D6-P383-0007 §7.9.5 [body] If hPPR or mPPR modes are entered while sPPR is enabled, the DRAM will disable sPPR in the target bank and clear the current address prior to executing.
D6-P383-0008 §7.9.5 [body] MBIST and sPPR may not be enabled at the same time.
D6-P383-0009 §7.9.5 [body] sPPR must have been disabled, cleared and unlocked prior to entering MBIST.
D6-P383-0010 §7.9.5 [body] Each bank has one sPPR resource shared with hPPR; when the hPPR resources for a bank are used up, the bank has no more available resources for sPPR.
D6-P383-0011 §7.9.5 [body] To preserve sPPR availability, this shared resource will be used for hPPR by the DRAM only after all other hPPR resources are exhausted.
D6-P383-0012 §7.9.5 [body] The host is responsible for reading the hPPR/sPPR resource registers prior to issuing sPPR to ensure the resource is available in the targeted bank.
D6-P383-0013 §7.9.5 [body] The sPPR Verification mode can be used to validate if the targeted row was repaired correctly.
D6-P383-0014 §7.9.5 [body] The use of the sPPR resource will be reflected in the hPPR/sPPR resource registers as 10B.
D6-P383-0015 §7.9.5 [body] DDR6 shall support the sPPR Undo and sPPR Lock operations.
D6-P383-0016 §7.9.5 [body] sPPR Undo will undo a previous sPPR and cause the sPPR resource to be taken back to its unused condition.
D6-P383-0017 §7.9.5 [body] The original row will appear back in the memory map at its original location following an sPPR Undo and will be reflected in the hPPR/sPPR resource register state.
D6-P383-0018 §7.9.5 [body] sPPR Lock will lock the specific sPPR resource at its current location and not allow another sPPR or an sPPR Undo to be performed to that resource.
D6-P383-0019 §7.9.5 [body] Rows that have been replaced by spare rows do not require refresh by the DRAM.
D6-P383-0020 §7.9.5 [body] When a spare row is moved in or out of the memory map, the host controller is responsible for issuing sufficient ACT commands to any unmapped row to maintain required data retention.
D6-P383-0021 §7.9.5 [body] The host controller is also responsible for performing all necessary data copy operations between the original row and the spare row.
D6-P383-0022 §7.9.5.1 [body] Entry into sPPR is through a mode register enable and an ACT command is used to transmit the bank and row address of the row to be repaired.
D6-P383-0023 §7.9.5.1 [body] For DRAM configured as 2p3 or 2p6, an sPPR shall only be done on one PSD at a time and the other PSD shall remain idle.
D6-P383-0024 §7.9.5.1 [body] After tRCD time, a WR command is used to select the individual DRAM through the DQ bits and to transfer the repair address to the DRAM.
D6-P383-0025 §7.9.5.1 [body] Following the program time and a PREpb command, exit sPPR through the mode register disable and resume normal operation after tPGMPST_sPPR.
D6-P383-0027 §7.9.5.1 [body] 1) Verify sPPR resource availability by issuing an MRR to read the hPPR/sPPR resource designation registers in the PSD Mode Registers (MR33-48 MRBF-MRC6).
D6-P383-0028 §7.9.5.1 [body] If no resources are available (10B, hPPR resource is available and sPPR resource is not available, 11B, hPPR and sPPR resource is not available), do not initiate sPPR mode.
D6-P383-0029 §7.9.5.1 [body] 2) Back up the data of the target row address in the bank prior to executing sPPR.
D6-P383-0030 §7.9.5.1 [body] The backup data should be one row per bank.
D6-P383-0031 §7.9.5.1 [body] 3) Precharge all banks and ensure they are idle; disable CRC mode.
D6-P383-0032 §7.9.5.1 [body] 4) Enable sPPR by setting MR32:OP[2:1] = 01B and wait tMRD.
D6-P383-0033 §7.9.5.1 [body] 5) Issue the guard key as four consecutive MR30:OP[7:0] MRW commands as described in PPR Guard Key Protection.
D6-P383-0034 §7.9.5.1 [body] 6) Issue an ACT command with the BG, Bank, and Row fail address; wait tRCD.
D6-P383-0035 §7.9.5.1 [body] 7) Issue a WR command with a valid column address; wait WL.
D6-P383-0036 §7.9.5.1 [body] The DRAM will ignore the column address.
D6-P384-0001 §7.9.5.1 [body] 8) After WL, drive DQ[2:0] HIGH for 12tCK on the target DRAM.
D6-P384-0002 §7.9.5.1 [body] For non-target DRAMs, drive DQ[2:0] LOW for 12tCK.
D6-P384-0003 §7.9.5.1 [body] If all the DQ[2:0] data bits are neither all LOW nor all HIGH for 12tCK, then sPPR mode execution is unknown.
D6-P384-0004 §7.9.5.1 [body] For 2p6, 1p6 and 1p12 devices, data bits other than DQ[2:0] are don't cares.
D6-P384-0005 §7.9.5.1 [body] 9) Wait tPGM_sPPR for the internal repair register to be written, then issue PREpb to the bank.
D6-P384-0006 §7.9.5.1 [body] 10) Wait tPGM_EXIT_sPPR after PREpb to enable recognition of the repaired row address.
D6-P384-0007 §7.9.5.1 [body] 11) Exit sPPR by setting MR32:OP[2:1] = 00B and wait tPGMPST_sPPR.
D6-P384-0009 §7.9.5.1 [body] 13) sPPR can be performed without affecting the hPPR previously performed, provided a row is available in that repair region.
D6-P384-0010 §7.9.5.1 [body] In the case of conducting a subsequent sPPR in the same bank, a sPPR Undo must be performed before initiating sPPR.
D6-P384-0011 §7.9.5.1 [body] In the case of conducting sPPR in a different bank, repeat steps 1–12.
D6-P384-0012 §7.9.5.1 [body] During sPPR, a REFRESH command is not allowed between sPPR MRW entry and exit.
D6-P385-0001 §7.9.5.2 [body] sPPR Undo restores an sPPR resource to its unused state, equivalent to its condition following reset, and will be reflected in the hPPR/sPPR resource register state.
D6-P385-0002 §7.9.5.2 [body] This operation uses The sPPR Undo procedure follows the same protocol as the sPPR sequence below,except MR32:OP[2:1] is set to 10B.
D6-P385-0003 §7.9.5.2 [body] 1) Precharge all banks and ensure they are idle; disable CRC mode.
D6-P385-0004 §7.9.5.2 [body] 2) Enable sPPR Undo by setting MR32:OP[2:1] = 10B and wait tMRD.
D6-P385-0005 §7.9.5.2 [body] 3) Issue the guard key as four consecutive MR30:OP[7:0] MRW commands as described in PPR Guard Key Protection.
D6-P385-0006 §7.9.5.2 [body] 4) Issue an ACT command with the BG, Bank, and Row fail address; wait tRCD.
D6-P385-0007 §7.9.5.2 [body] 5) Issue a WR command with a valid column address; wait WL.
D6-P385-0008 §7.9.5.2 [body] The DRAM will ignore the column address.
D6-P385-0009 §7.9.5.2 [body] 6) After WL, drive DQ[2:0] HIGH for 12tCK on the target DRAM.
D6-P385-0010 §7.9.5.2 [body] For non-target DRAMs, drive DQ[2:0] LOW for 12tCK.
D6-P385-0011 §7.9.5.2 [body] If all the DQ[2:0] data bits are neither all LOW nor all HIGH for 12tCK, then sPPR mode execution is unknown.
D6-P385-0012 §7.9.5.2 [body] For 2p6, 1p6 and 1p12 devices, data bits other than DQ[2:0] are don't cares.
D6-P385-0013 §7.9.5.2 [body] 7) Wait tPGM_sPPR for the internal repair register to be un-written, then issue PREpb to the bank.
D6-P385-0014 §7.9.5.2 [body] 8) Wait tPGM_EXIT_sPPR after PREpb to enable recognition of the repaired row address.
D6-P385-0015 §7.9.5.2 [body] 9) Exit sPPR Undo by setting MR32:OP[2:1] = 00B and wait tPGMPST_sPPR.
D6-P385-0016 §7.9.5.2 [body] The host controller must issue an ACT command with the same BG bits, Bank bits, and row address used in the most recent sPPR for that resource.
D6-P385-0017 §7.9.5.2 [body] If the row address does not match the most recent sPPR, the DRAM may or may not perform the undo.
D6-P385-0018 §7.9.5.2 [body] All required data copy operations are the responsibility of the host controller.
D6-P385-0019 §7.9.5.2 [body] After an sPPR Undo, the resource can be reassigned to a new or previous location.
D6-P385-0020 §7.9.5.2 [body] Data within the sPPR resource is retained but does not require refresh by the DRAM.
D6-P385-0021 §7.9.5.2 [body] If data validity must be maintained, the host controller shall issue sufficient ACT commands while the row is mapped in.
D6-P385-0022 §7.9.5.3 [body] sPPR Lock secures an sPPR resource in its current location, preventing any subsequent sPPR or sPPR Undo operations on that resource.
D6-P385-0023 §7.9.5.3 [body] Locks are applied individually to each sPPR resource.
D6-P385-0024 §7.9.5.3 [body] To remove a lock, a hardware reset or power cycle must be performed.
D6-P385-0025 §7.9.5.3 [body] A hardware reset or power cycle is also required before executing any hPPR operation if any sPPR resources remain locked.
D6-P385-0026 §7.9.5.3 [body] The sPPR Lock procedure follows the same protocol as the sPPR sequence below, except MR32:OP[2:1] is set to 11B.
D6-P385-0027 §7.9.5.3 [body] 1) Precharge all banks and ensure they are idle; disable CRC mode.
D6-P385-0028 §7.9.5.3 [body] 2) Enable sPPR Lock by setting MR32:OP[2:1] = 11B and wait tMRD.
D6-P386-0001 §7.9.5.3 [body] 3) Issue the guard key as four consecutive MR30:OP[7:0] MRW commands as described in PPR Guard Key Protection.
D6-P386-0002 §7.9.5.3 [body] 4) Issue an ACT command with the BG, Bank, and Row fail address; wait tRCD.
D6-P386-0003 §7.9.5.3 [body] 5) Issue a WR command with a valid column address; wait WL.
D6-P386-0004 §7.9.5.3 [body] The DRAM will ignore the column address.
D6-P386-0005 §7.9.5.3 [body] 6) After WL, drive DQ[2:0] HIGH for 12tCK on the target DRAM.
D6-P386-0006 §7.9.5.3 [body] For non-target DRAMs, drive DQ[2:0] LOW for 12tCK.
D6-P386-0007 §7.9.5.3 [body] If all the DQ[2:0] data bits are neither all LOW nor all HIGH for 12tCK, then sPPR mode execution is unknown.
D6-P386-0008 §7.9.5.3 [body] For 2p6, 1p6 and 1p12 devices, data bits other than DQ[2:0] are don't cares.
D6-P386-0009 §7.9.5.3 [body] 7) Wait tPGM_sPPR for the internal repair register to be locked, then issue PREpb to the bank.
D6-P386-0010 §7.9.5.3 [body] 8) Wait tPGM_EXIT_sPPR after PREpb to enable recognition of the repaired row address.
D6-P386-0011 §7.9.5.3 [body] 9) Exit sPPR Lock by setting MR32:OP[2:1] = 00B and wait tPGMPST_sPPR.
D6-P386-0012 §7.9.5.3 [body] The ACT command shall include BG bits, Bank bits, and the row address associated with the most recent sPPR for that resource.
D6-P386-0013 §7.9.5.3 [body] If the row address does not match the most recent sPPR, the device may or may not perform the lock.
D6-P386-0014 §7.9.5.4 [body] sPPR Verification is a DDR6 feature that validates whether a specified row address matches the most recent sPPR assignment for a given bank.
D6-P386-0015 §7.9.5.4 [body] This operation follows the same protocol as the sPPR sequence, with MR32:OP[3] set to 1.
D6-P386-0016 §7.9.5.4 [body] The verification result is stored in MR32:OP[4], where a value of 1 indicates success and 0 indicates failure.
D6-P386-0017 §7.9.5.4 [body] MR32:OP[4] is shared across all banks within each PSD and is reset to 0 after every MRR to the verification register.
D6-P386-0018 §7.9.5.4 [body] Multiple row addresses from different banks can be verified; however, the host controller must read MR32:OP[4] after each ACT command to prevent overwriting the previous verification status.
D6-P386-0019 §7.9.5.4 [body] The following is the procedure for sPPR Verification:
D6-P386-0020 §7.9.5.4 [body] 1) Precharge all banks and ensure they are idle; disable CRC mode.
D6-P386-0021 §7.9.5.4 [body] 2) Enable sPPR Verification by setting MR32:OP[3] = 1 and wait tMRD.
D6-P386-0022 §7.9.5.4 [body] 3) Issue the guard key as four consecutive MR30:OP[7:0] MRW commands as described in PPR Guard Key Protection.
D6-P386-0023 §7.9.5.4 [body] 4) Issue an ACT command with the BG, Bank and Row fail address and wait tRAS.
D6-P386-0024 §7.9.5.4 [body] 5) Issue a PREpb command to the bank and wait tRP.
D6-P386-0025 §7.9.5.4 [body] 6) Issue an MRR command to MR32:OP[4] to read the verification status and wait tMRR.
D6-P386-0026 §7.9.5.4 [body] 7) Exit sPPR Verification by setting MR32:OP[3] = 0 and wait tMRD.
D6-P386-0027 §7.9.5.4 [body] 8) In the case of verifying a subsequent Row address from another bank, repeat steps 4–6.
D6-P387-0001 §7.9.6 [body] DDR6 devices support Memory Built-In Self-Test (MBIST) and Memory Built-In Self-Test Post Package Repair (mPPR) functions to address hard failures, such as single-bit or multi-bit errors within a device.
D6-P387-0002 §7.9.6 [body] These functions enable detection and repair of weak cells.
D6-P387-0003 §7.9.6 [body] • MBIST: The DRAM executes vendor-specific test patterns to scan the memory array and identify hard failures.
D6-P387-0004 §7.9.6 [body] • mPPR: The DRAM repairs failing addresses identified during MBIST.
D6-P387-0011 §7.9.6 [body] Wait for ALERT to drive HIGH (max tSELF_TEST)
D6-P387-0012 §7.9.6 [body] Wait tSELF_REPAIR and exit mPPR
D6-P387-0017 §7.9.6 [body] Enter mPPR 01B (fails remain) 01B (fails remain)
D6-P387-0019 §7.9.6 [body] 11B (MBIST should be
D6-P387-0020 §7.9.6.1 [body] MBIST shall only be executed when all banks are in the idle state after device initialization but must be completed prior to mPPR.
D6-P387-0021 §7.9.6.1 [body] To enable MBIST, the host shall issue an MRW command with MR31[0] = 1.
D6-P387-0022 §7.9.6.1 [body] During MBIST, the DRAM applies vendor-specific test patterns to the memory array to detect hard failures.
D6-P387-0023 §7.9.6.1 [body] The DRAM asserts ALERT for the duration of MBIST and de-asserts ALERT upon completion.
D6-P387-0024 §7.9.6.1 [body] For 2p3 or 2p6 devices, initiating MBIST through either PSD causes the DRAM to execute MBIST on both PSDs.
D6-P387-0025 §7.9.6.1 [body] After MBIST concludes and ALERT is LOW, the DRAM updates the MBIST transparency status in MRC7[1:0], which the host shall read.
D6-P387-0026 §7.9.6.1 [body] If the transparency status indicates remaining failures and the controller elects to perform mPPR, mPPR shall be executed immediately after reading the transparency status.
D6-P387-0027 §7.9.6.1 [body] Detailed transparency status definitions are provided in MRC7.
D6-P388-0001 §7.9.6.1 [body] The following steps shall be performed to execute MBIST.
D6-P388-0002 §7.9.6.1 [body] For 2p3 or 2p6 devices, steps 2-4 performed through one PSD executes MBIST on both PSDs.
D6-P388-0003 §7.9.6.1 [body] 1) Precharge all banks and ensure they are idle; disable CRC mode.
D6-P388-0004 §7.9.6.1 [body] 2) Enable MBIST by setting MR31[0] = 1 and wait tMRD.
D6-P388-0005 §7.9.6.1 [body] 3) Issue the guard key as four consecutive MR30[7:0] MRW commands as described in PPR Guard Key Protection.
D6-P388-0006 §7.9.6.1 [body] Upon completion of the guard key sequence, the DRAM enters MBIST operation and asserts ALERT HIGH for tSELF_TEST.
D6-P388-0007 §7.9.6.1 [body] a) While ALERT is HIGH, only DES commands are permitted.
D6-P388-0008 §7.9.6.1 [body] b) When MBIST concludes, the DRAM de-asserts ALERT LOW to indicate completion.
D6-P388-0009 §7.9.6.1 [body] MBIST transparency status is updated in MRC7.
D6-P388-0010 §7.9.6.1 [body] 4) Exit MBIST by setting MR31[0] = 0 and wait tMRD.
D6-P388-0011 §7.9.6.1 [body] 5) Issue an MRR to MRC7[1:0] and wait tMRR.
D6-P388-0012 §7.9.6.1 [body] If transparency indicates that failures remain and repair resources are available, proceed to the mPPR procedure.
D6-P388-0013 §7.9.6.1 [body] The following are the MBIST transparency outcomes of MRC7[1:0]:
D6-P388-0014 §7.9.6.1 [body] • 00: No error detected / MBIST has not run since INIT (default)
D6-P388-0015 §7.9.6.1 [body] • 01: Error detected; repair resources are available
D6-P388-0016 §7.9.6.1 [body] • 10: Error detected; no repair resources are available
D6-P388-0028 §7.9.6.2 [body] If the host issues an MRR to MRC7[1:0] and obtains outcome 01 (error detected; repair resources are available) then mPPR shall be performed by issuing an MRW with MR31[1] = 1.
D6-P388-0029 §7.9.6.2 [body] The mPPR procedure will repair a single row identified during MBIST.
D6-P388-0030 §7.9.6.2 [body] Attempting mPPR without MBIST may result in an unexpected repair.
D6-P388-0031 §7.9.6.2 [body] mPPR resources are separated from normal hPPR/sPPR resources.
D6-P389-0001 §7.9.6.2 [body] The following steps shall be performed to execute mPPR.
D6-P389-0002 §7.9.6.2 [body] For 2p3 or 2p6 devices, all steps shall be performed through each PSD.
D6-P389-0003 §7.9.6.2 [body] 1) Precharge all banks and ensure they are idle; disable CRC mode.
D6-P389-0004 §7.9.6.2 [body] 2) Enable mPPR by setting MR31[1] = 1 and wait tMRD.
D6-P389-0005 §7.9.6.2 [body] 3) Issue the guard key as four consecutive MR30[7:0] MRW commands as described in PPR Guard Key Protection.
D6-P389-0006 §7.9.6.2 [body] 4) Issue an ACT command with a valid BG, Bank, and Row address; wait tRCD.
D6-P389-0007 §7.9.6.2 [body] The DRAM will ignore the address.
D6-P389-0008 §7.9.6.2 [body] 5) Issue a WRA command with a valid column address; wait WL.
D6-P389-0009 §7.9.6.2 [body] The DRAM will ignore the column address.
D6-P389-0010 §7.9.6.2 [body] 6) After WL, drive DQ[2:0] HIGH for 12tCK on the target DRAM.
D6-P389-0011 §7.9.6.2 [body] Only DRAMs which most recently have had an MBIST transparency result of 01 may be considered “target DRAMs”.
D6-P389-0012 §7.9.6.2 [body] For non-target DRAMs, drive DQ[2:0] LOW for 12tCK.
D6-P389-0013 §7.9.6.2 [body] If DQ[2:0] data bits are neither all LOW nor all HIGH for 12tCK, then mPPR mode execution is unknown.
D6-P389-0014 §7.9.6.2 [body] For 2p6, 1p6 and 1p12 devices, data bits other than DQ[2:0] are don't cares.
D6-P389-0015 §7.9.6.2 [body] 7) Wait tPGM_mPPR for internal row repair, then issue a PREab command.
D6-P389-0016 §7.9.6.2 [body] 8) Wait tPGM_EXIT_mPPR after PREab command to enable the DRAM to update MBIST transparency.
D6-P389-0017 §7.9.6.2 [body] 9) Exit mPPR by setting MR31[1] = 0 and wait tPGMPST_mPPR.
D6-P390-0001 §7.9.6.2 [body] 10) Issue an MRR to MRC7[1:0] and wait tMRR.
D6-P390-0002 §7.9.6.2 [body] If MBIST transparency result is 01 (error detected; repair resources are available), repeat steps 2-10 to perform additional mPPR.
D6-P390-0003 §7.9.6.2 [body] Otherwise, the DRAM may continue to the next planned operation.
D6-P391-0001 §7.9.7 [body] The DDR6 supports high data rates.
D6-P391-0002 §7.9.7 [body] Operating at elevated frequencies within the memory channel can result in significant inter-symbol interference (ISI) due to insertion loss and reflections.
D6-P391-0003 §7.9.7 [body] To compensate channel characteristics and enhance signal integrity, equalization techniques are required on the DRAM receiver (RX) side.
D6-P391-0004 §7.9.7 [body] DDR6 provides per-pin controlled Decision Feedback Equalization (DFE) for DQ RX.
D6-P391-0005 §7.9.7 [body] A 2-tap DFE is selected to equalize the received DQ signals, as it effectively reduces the impact of insertion loss and reflections without amplifying the noise.
D6-P391-0006 §7.9.7 [body] To eliminate ISI, the per-pin DFE Tap-1 and Tap-2 bits should be enabled and appropriately configured for the data rates.
D6-P391-0007 §7.9.7 [body] When the DFE is enabled, the DQs shall be driven low for a minimum of 8UI prior to the first Write data bit to ensure proper DFE synchronization.
D6-P391-0008 §7.9.7 [body] In case of back to back write, there is no need to add pre-drive.
D6-P391-0021 §7.9.7.1 [body] The DFE tap coefficients shown in Table 1 are used by the memory controller to adjust the strengths of Tap-1 and Tap-2, to adapt ISI cancellation based on channel performance.
D6-P391-0022 §7.9.7.1 [body] Tables 2 and 3 list the DFE Tap-1 and Tap-2 coefficient values, respectively, according to their step size, minimum, and maximum values.
D6-P391-0023 §7.9.7.1 [body] Table 4 is DFE Tap-2 Coefficient Step Table for polarity inversion.
D6-P392-0004 §7.9.7.1 [table_cell] NOTE 1: DFE Quantity setting is specified across DC voltage and temperature.
D6-P392-0005 §7.9.7.1 [table_cell] NOTE 2 These numbers are guaranteed by design.

D6-P395-0002 §7.9.7.2 [table_cell] NOTE 2: The step size and step values are related to the min/max ranges specified in the DFE Tap Coefficient Tables

D6-P407-0001 §7.9.7.3 [body] The following parameters are applied through the Mode Register for DFE update setting.
D6-P407-0005 §7.9.7.3 [table_cell] NOTE 1: This parameter applies only to MRW’s to DFE registers and is defined as the settling time before a new DFE setting is active
D6-P408-0001 §7.9.8 [body] DDR6 supports CRC for write and read operations.
D6-P408-0002 §7.9.8 [body] Write and read CRC can be enabled by separate Write CRC Enable and Read CRC Enable mode register bits in MR01OP[3] and MR01OP[2], respectively.
D6-P408-0003 §7.9.8.1 [body] The controller generates the CRC checksum and forms the write data packet as shown in Section 1.1.4.3.
D6-P408-0004 §7.9.8.1 [body] The DRAM checks for an error in a received write data packet by comparing the received checksum
D6-P408-0005 §7.9.8.1 [body] against the computed checksum and reports errors using the ALERT signal if there is a mismatch.
D6-P408-0006 §7.9.8.1 [body] DRAM can write data to the DRAM core without waiting for CRC check for full writes.
D6-P408-0007 §7.9.8.1 [body] If bad data is written to the DRAM core then controller will retry the transaction and overwrite the bad data.
D6-P408-0008 §7.9.8.1 [body] Controller is responsible for data coherency.
D6-P408-0009 §7.9.8.1 [body] There is no write latency adder when Write CRC is enabled.
D6-P408-0011 §7.9.8.1 [body] When DRAM detects CRC error on a received write data packet, then it drives ALERT signal to ‘1’ for tCRC_ALERT_PW.
D6-P408-0012 §7.9.8.1 [body] The latency to ALERT signal is defined as tCRC_ALERT in Figure 1.
D6-P408-0013 §7.9.8.1 [body] DRAM willshall set the Write CRC Error Status bit in MRxxOP[0] to '1' upon detecting a CRC error within (tCRC_ALERT__ON + tMRD).
D6-P408-0014 §7.9.8.1 [body] The Write CRC Error Status bit remains '1' until the host clears it explicitly using an MRW command or MRUPDATE Mode.
D6-P408-0015 §7.9.8.1 [body] The controller understands the worst-case delay for ALERT (during initialization) and can back up the transactions accordingly or the controller can be made more intelligent and try to correlate the write CRC error to a specific rank or a transaction.
D6-P408-0016 §7.9.8.1 [body] The controller upon seeing an error as a pulse width willmay retry the write transactions.
D6-P408-0017 §7.9.8.1 [body] The controller is also responsible for opening any pages and ensuring that retrying of writes is done in a coherent fashion.
D6-P409-0001 §7.9.8.1 [body] The pulse width may be seen longer than CRC_ALERT_PW at the controller if there are multiple CRC errors as the ALERT is a daisy chain bus or a many-to-one bus.
D6-P409-0010 §7.9.8.1 [body] NOTE: 1: CRC_ALERT_PW is specified from the point where the DRAM starts to drive the signal high to the point where the driver releases and the controller starts to pull the signal down.
D6-P409-0011 §7.9.8.1 [body] NOTE: 2: Timing diagram applies to 2p3 devices in MD_OFF Metadata Mode with CRC-4-SC-8 CRC-8-D64.
D6-P409-0012 §7.9.8.2 [body] The DDR6 SDRAM generates the CRC checksum and forms the read data packet as shown in Section 1.1.4.4.
D6-P409-0013 §7.9.8.2 [body] The controller can check for an error in a received data packet by comparing the received checksum against the computed checksum and if there is a mismatch then controller may retry the transaction.
D6-P409-0014 §7.9.8.2 [body] Read latency adder exists when read CRC is enabled depends on data rate.
D6-P409-0015 §7.9.8.3 [body] The CRC-4 and CRC-8polynomials used by DDR6 are X4 + X3 + X2 + 1 (0x1d)and X8 + X4 + X3 + X2 + 1 (0x11d), respectively, which are different from DDR5.
D6-P409-0016 §7.9.8.3 [body] The CRC-4-SC-8 is a subcode derived from row-splitting the CRC-4 code to get 8 checksum bits.
D6-P409-0017 §7.9.8.3 [body] The CRC-8-D64 code includes 64 data bits and 8 CRC checksum bits.
D6-P409-0018 §7.9.8.3 [body] The CRC-4 code includes 64 data bits, 4 metadata bits, and 4 CRC checksum bits.
D6-P409-0019 §7.9.8.3 [body] The CRC-8-D136 code includes 128 data bits, 8 metadata or zero pad bits, and 8 CRC checksum bits.
D6-P409-0020 §7.9.8.3 [body] The CRC code for the 2p3 device configuration is configured as CRC-4 or CRC-8-D64 using the CRC Code for 2p3 mode register bit in MR01[1].
D6-P409-0021 §7.9.8.3 [body] The CRC code for 1p6/2p6/1p12 device configurations is CRC-8-D136.
D6-P409-0022 §7.9.8.3 [body] The selected CRC code will be instantiated once for the 2p3/1p6/2p6 device configurations or twice for the 1p12 device configuration.
D6-P410-0002 §7.9.8.3 [body] Figure 2 shows the CRC-4 H-matrix, and the logic equations are shown afterwards in Figure 3.
D6-P410-0008 §7.9.8.3 [body] Figure 4 shows the CRC-4-SC-8CRC-8 H-matrix, and the logic equations are shown afterwards in Figure 5.
D6-P412-0010 §7.9.8.3 [body] Table 1 shows error detection coverage of DDR6 CRC codes.
D6-P413-0001 §7.9.8.4 [body] CRC is supported for metadata modes MD_OFF, MD_8b_A, MD_8b_B, and MD_16b., and the CRC mode configured separately as CRC-4 or CRC-4-SC-8 using the CRC Mode mode register bit in MRxx OP[y].
D6-P413-0002 §7.9.8.4 [body] The device configuration as 2p3/1p6/2p6/1p12 will determine how many times the CRC code is instantiated in the data packet.
D6-P413-0003 §7.9.8.4 [body] Table 2 shows which CRC codes are supported for various device configurations and metadata modes.
D6-P414-0002 §7.9.8.4 [body] CRC-4 checksum bits alwaysoccupy the last 4 bits of DQ2, and in addition, the last 4 bits of DQ5 for 1p6/2p6/1p12, and in addition, the last 4 bits of DQ8 and DQ11 for 1p12.
D6-P414-0003 §7.9.8.4 [body] CRC-4-SC-8CRC-8-D64checksum bits alwaysoccupy the last 8 bits of DQ2, and in addition, the last 8 bits of DQ5 for 1p6/2p6/1p12, and in addition, the last 8 bits of DQ8 and DQ11 for 1p12.
D6-P414-0004 §7.9.8.4 [body] CRC-8-D136 checksum bits occupy the last 8 bits of DQ5 for 1p6/2p6/1p12, and in addition, the last 8 bits of DQ11 for 1p12.
D6-P414-0006 §7.9.8.4 [body] In certain combinations of metadata modes and CRC codes, the CRC parity bits are a subset of the metadata bits.
D6-P414-0007 §7.9.8.4 [body] Stored CRC is configured by using MR01OP[1:0] to select a CRC code, whose checksum bits are located within a subset of the metadata bits associated with the metadata mode.
D6-P414-0008 §7.9.8.4 [body] During writes, the CRC parity bits will be checked and also stored as metadata bits.
D6-P414-0009 §7.9.8.4 [body] The read CRC function may then be disabled by setting MR01OP[2] to 0, as reading the metadata bits will produce the CRC parity bits.
D6-P414-0010 §7.9.8.4 [body] This concept, known as stored CRC, avoids additional read latency normally associated with read CRC.
D6-P414-0011 §7.9.8.4 [body] If the CRC parity bits are a subset of the metadata bits but the read CRC function is enabled by setting MR01OP[2] to 1, then the CRC parity bits shall be regenerated during a read and output on the DQ bus.
D6-P414-0013 §7.9.8.4 [body] When non-data bit locations in the data packet are unused, for example, by metadata or CRC checksum bits, the unused bits in the data packet shall be set to 0s.
D6-P415-0001 §7.9.8.4 [body] Figures 8-13 show detailed bit mapping for the CRC codewords for the 2p3 device configuration for various CRC and metadata modes.
D6-P417-0001 §7.9.8.4 [body] Figures 14-18 show detailed bit mapping for the CRC codewords for the 1p6/2p6 device configurations for various CRC and metadata modes.
D6-P419-0001 §7.9.8.4 [body] Figures 19-23 show detailed bit mapping for the CRC codewords for the 1p12 device configuration for various CRC and metadata modes.
D6-P421-0002 §7.9.8.5 [table_cell] 0B – Clear 1B – Error
D6-P421-0003 §7.9.8.5 [table_cell] NOTE 1: The CRC Code for 2p3 mode register only applies to the 2p3 device configuration.
D6-P421-0004 §7.9.8.5 [table_cell] It is ignored for the 1p6/2p6/1p12 device configurations.
D6-P421-0005 §7.9.8.5 [table_cell] NOTE 2: The host shall disable Write CRC, if it was enabled, prior to entering Write Leveling Training mode.
D6-P423-0003 §7.9.9 [body] The device supports on-die ECC covering the array bits.
D6-P423-0004 §7.9.9 [body] The on-die ECC may be whatever type of ECC the device manufacturer determines is to best support its design objectives.
D6-P423-0005 §7.9.9 [body] However, the multi-bit error (MBE) diagnostic coverage (DC) shall be available to host.
D6-P423-0006 §7.9.9.1 [body] SBE = Single bit error: one single bit error per ECC code word; sometimes referred to as payload or mission data.
D6-P423-0007 §7.9.9.1 [body] MBE = Multi-bit error: two or more single bit errors per ECC code word; sometimes referred to as payload or mission data.
D6-P423-0008 §7.9.9.2 [body] The on-die ECC corrects one bit error (SBE) detected in the ECC code word.
D6-P423-0009 §7.9.9.2 [body] More than one bit error detected in the ECC code word is classifieds as an uncorrectable MBE.
D6-P423-0010 §7.9.9.2 [body] The device’s on-die ECC has the ability to meet or exceed the following diagnostics:
D6-P423-0015 §7.9.9.2 [body] • On-die ECC classifies more than one bit error detected in the ECC code word as an uncorrectable MBE and the detected MBE should be registered
D6-P423-0016 §7.9.9.3 [body] The on-die ECC MBE detection should be registered in MR97 OP[3].
D6-P424-0002 §7.9.10.1 [body] A bounded fault shall not impact more than 32 bits in a 128-bit data fetch.
D6-P424-0003 §7.9.10.1 [body] The data bits impacted by a bounded fault will be confined to at most 4 sub-packets of the data packet.
D6-P424-0004 §7.9.10.1 [body] The impacted data sub-packets can be present anywhere in the data packet.
D6-P424-0005 §7.9.10.2 [body] A bounded fault shall not impact more than 24 bits in a 64-bit data fetch.
D6-P424-0006 §7.9.10.2 [body] The data bits impacted by a bounded fault will be confined to at most 3 sub-packets of the data packet.
D6-P424-0007 §7.9.10.2 [body] The impacted data sub-packets can be present anywhere in the data packet.
D6-P427-0002 §7.9.13.1 [body] Two fault registers, MR97 and MR98, register up to 15 functions when a fault(s) is detected by a covered function.
D6-P427-0003 §7.9.13.1 [body] Fault registers MR97 and MR98 are default LOW and remain LOW unless a covered function has detected a fault and sets the associated register bit HIGH.
D6-P427-0004 §7.9.13.1 [body] The affected fault registers’ bit(s) retain their fault status until either (1) the affected register is read from; (2) a RESET command is applied; or (3) power is removed.
D6-P427-0005 §7.9.13.1 [body] The supported functions, when enabled via MR99 and MR100, control the alert output when a fault occurs.
D6-P427-0006 §7.9.13.1 [body] FMR3 and FMR4 may be written to when the device is in idle state and no data is driven.
D6-P427-0007 §7.9.13.1 [body] The alert output is driven active, asynchronously, upon fault occurrence and remains active until either (1) the affected MR97 and/or MR98 is read from, with one exception: MR97 OP[0] clearingw is controlled by PRAC operation and is cleared as defined by PRAC alert back-off protocol (ABO); (2) a RESET command is applied; or (3) power is removed.
D6-P427-0008 §7.9.13.1 [body] The logical diagram below depicts the functionality.
D6-P427-0016 §7.9.13.1 [body] Fault register MR97 OP[7] is derived by ORing MR98 OP[7:0], logical diagram below depicts this functionality.
D6-P427-0017 §7.9.13.1 [body] When the host interrogates MR97 and if MR97 OP[7] is LOW, then the host does not need to read MR98.
D6-P428-0001 §7.9.13.2 [body] Fault registers MR97 and MR98 define which functions register fault occurrence.
D6-P428-0002 §7.9.13.2 [body] These functions are not required to be supported by the device; however, if a function is supported, it should use the defined register bit.
D6-P428-0003 §7.9.13.2 [body] MR97 and MR98 are default LOW and remain LOW unless a covered function has a detected fault and sets the associated register bit HIGH.
D6-P428-0004 §7.9.13.2 [body] The affected fault registers' bit(s) retain their fault status until either (1) register is read from (Note: MR97 OP[7] is not required to be reset by MRR command; MR97 OP[7] may be cleared by clearing MR98); (2) a RESET command applied, or (3) power is removed.
D6-P428-0005 §7.9.13.2 [body] NOTE: FMR1 OP[7] is the sum of FMR2 OP[7:0]
D6-P429-0001 §7.9.13.3 [body] The two registers, MR99 and MR100, control which functions with registered faults affect the alert output status.
D6-P429-0002 §7.9.13.3 [body] MR99 and MR100 are default LOW and remain LOW until written to; these two MR registers may be written to when the device is in idle state and no data is driven.
D6-P429-0003 §7.9.13.3 [body] These two registers retain set values until either (1) rewritten to different state (2) a RESET command is applied, or (3) power is removed.
D6-P429-0004 §7.9.13.3 [body] NOTE: MR99 OP[0] controls whether PRAC ABO protocol drives ALERT active.

D6-P432-0015 §7.9.13.4 [body] The time for clearing fault from alert output is as follows:
D6-P432-0018 §7.9.13.5 [body] SBE: Single bit error: one single bit error per ECC code word; sometimes referred to as payload or mission data.
D6-P432-0019 §7.9.13.5 [body] DBE: Double bit error: two single bit errors per ECC code word; sometimes referred to as payload or mission data.
D6-P432-0020 §7.9.13.5 [body] MBE: Multi-bit error: two or more single bit errors per ECC code word; sometimes referred to as payload or mission data.
D6-P433-0001 §7.9.14.1 [body] CA parity checking detects bit errors in the command address bus.
D6-P433-0002 §7.9.14.1 [body] The CA Parity Checking mode register, MR01OP[4], enables or disables CA parity checking.
D6-P433-0003 §7.9.14.1 [body] The DRAM must be in idle state when CA Parity checking mode register is changed.
D6-P433-0004 §7.9.14.1 [body] The default is disenabled.
D6-P433-0005 §7.9.14.1 [body] When CA parity checking is disabled, the DRAM does not need to check CA parity bit(s) in each command.
D6-P433-0006 §7.9.14.1 [body] When CA parity checking is enabled, the CA parity shall be checked and ALERT shall be asserted if a CA parity error is detected.
D6-P433-0007 §7.9.14.1 [body] The CA Parity Command Blocking mode register, MR01OP[5], selects blocking or nonblocking mode when a CA parity error is detected.
D6-P433-0008 §7.9.14.1 [body] The default is nonblocking.
D6-P433-0009 §7.9.14.1 [body] When CA parity checking is enabled in nonblocking mode, an erroneous command will be executed, but the DRAM may execute an unknown command.
D6-P433-0010 §7.9.14.1 [body] There is no additional latency increase compared to when CA parity checking is disabled, but DRAM will be in unknown state when a CA parity error is detected, so it is highly recommended that the host reset the DRAM.
D6-P433-0011 §7.9.14.1 [body] When CA parity checking is enabled in blocking mode, an erroneous command that fails the CA parity check shall not be executed.
D6-P433-0012 §7.9.14.1 [body] An additional delay (PL : Parity LatencytPAR_CA_Delay) is applied to all commands.
D6-P433-0013 §7.9.14.1 [body] The command is held for the time of the Parity Latency (PL) before it is executed inside the device.
D6-P433-0014 §7.9.14.1 [body] This means that issuing timing of the internal command is determined with PL.
D6-P433-0015 §7.9.14.1 [body] Since some commands on one or more DRAM devices will be blocked after the first CA parity error, while these commands may be executed on one or more other DRAM devices that did not have a CA parity error, the host should replay commands starting from the first command that might have had a CA parity error.
D6-P433-0016 §7.9.14.1 [body] The earliest command that could possibly have had a detected CA parity error can be determined by working backwards from the time the host detects the ALERT, the propagation delay of ALERT from the DRAM to the host, and tPAR_ALERT_ON.
D6-P433-0017 §7.9.14.1 [body] The exact command that had a CA parity error can be determined by reading the CA Parity Log C0 to C5 mode registers.
D6-P433-0018 §7.9.14.2 [body] Most commands in the Command Truth Table have a PAR1 bit in the 2nd cycle in CA4.
D6-P433-0019 §7.9.14.2 [body] 4-cycle and 6-cycle commands in the Command Truth Table have a PAR2 bit in the 4th or 6th cycle, respectively, in CA4.
D6-P433-0020 §7.9.14.2 [body] The convention of parity is even parity, meaning that valid parity is defined as an even number of ones across the inputs used for parity computation combined with the parity signal.
D6-P433-0021 §7.9.14.2 [body] In other words, the parity bit is chosen so that the total number of 1’s in the transmitted signal, including the parity bit is even.
D6-P433-0022 §7.9.14.2 [body] The parity check passes if the PAR1 is valid in 2-cycle commands and passes if both PAR1 and PAR2 are valid in 4- and 6-cycle commands.
D6-P433-0023 §7.9.14.2 [body] To generate valid parity bits, the host shall calculate PAR1 and PAR2 as follows.
D6-P433-0024 §7.9.14.2 [body] PAR1 and PAR2 satisfy even parity.
D6-P433-0025 §7.9.14.2 [body] The host shall calculate PAR1 as the XOR of all of the bits except itself in CA0, CA1, CA2, CA3, CA4, and CA5 (if supported) in the 1st and 2nd cycles of the command, excluding itself.
D6-P433-0026 §7.9.14.2 [body] In 4-cycle commands, the host shall calculate PAR2 as the XOR of all of the bits except itself in CA0, CA1, CA2, CA3, CA4, and CA5 (if supported) in the 3rd and 4th cycles of the command.
D6-P433-0027 §7.9.14.2 [body] In 6-cycle commands, the host shall calculate PAR2 as the XOR of all of the bits except itself in CA0, CA1, CA2, CA3, CA4, and CA5 (if supported) in the 3rd, 4th, 5th, and 6th cycles of the command.
D6-P433-0028 §7.9.14.2 [body] Only DES is allowed between valid commands to prevent DRAM from any malfunctioning.
D6-P433-0029 §7.9.14.3 [body] When CA parity checking is enabled, and the DRAM detects a CA parity error in any command as qualified by CS, then it must perform the following steps:
D6-P434-0001 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] Applies to Blocking Mode
D6-P434-0002 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] Applies to Non-blocking Mode
D6-P434-0003 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] In blocking mode only, the DRAM shall delay the erroneous command by PL and then ignore it.
D6-P434-0004 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] In both blocking and nonblocking modes, log the error by storing the erroneous command and address bits in the CA Parity Error Log C0-C5 mode registers for the PSD with the error.
D6-P434-0005 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] The DRAM maintains the CA Parity Error Log C0-C5 mode registers for the first erroneous command until the CA Parity Error Status mode register bit is cleared to 0.
D6-P434-0006 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] Set the CA Parity Error Status bit in the mode register to ‘1’.
D6-P434-0007 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] The CA Parity Error Status bit must be setbefore tCMD_BLOCKING finishes within (tPAR_ALERT_ON + tMRD).
D6-P434-0008 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] The CA Parity Error Status bit remains 1 until the host clears it explicitly using an MRW command or MRUPDATE Mode.
D6-P434-0009 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] All in-progress commands shall continue without interruption.
D6-P434-0010 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] In blocking modeonly, the DRAM shall ignore subsequent commands immediately after the erroneous command on the same PSD’s CA bus and shall continue to ignore commands on the same PSD’s CA bus until after tCMD_BLOCKING.
D6-P434-0011 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] If the Alert State Enable on CA Parity Error mode register bit is 1,Assert the ALERT signal to the host within tPAR_ALERT_ON time for tPAR_ALERT_PW.
D6-P434-0012 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] After tPAR_ALERT_PW has been satisfied, the DRAM shall de-assert ALERT.
D6-P434-0013 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] In nonblocking mode only, disable DQS outputs starting between tPAR_ALERT_ON and (RL - tRPRE) after the erroneous command and keep the DQS outputs disabled until after tCMD_BLOCKING; and disable DQ outputs starting between tPAR_ALERT_ON and RL after the erroneous command and keep DQ outputs disabled until after tCMD_BLOCKING.
D6-P434-0014 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] This means that READ and MRR commands that were issued prior to the CA parity error may have all, some, or none of the typical DQS or DQ activity after tPAR_ALERT_ON.
D6-P434-0015 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] It is highly recommended that the host ignore read data on DQ after ALERT is asserted for a CA parity error in nonblocking mode.
D6-P434-0016 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] It is highly recommended that the host assert RESET_n when a CA parity error is detected in nonblocking mode.
D6-P435-0001 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] Applies to Blocking Mode
D6-P435-0002 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] Applies to Non-blocking Mode
D6-P435-0003 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] In blocking mode only, if the PSD is not already in self-refresh mode, the DRAM shall internally issue PREab and SREREFabafter waiting PL + tRFCab, followed by SRE at least Max(tSR, tSRED) before tCMD_BLOCKING finishes.
D6-P435-0004 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] Similarly, the host may stop normal command sequences to all DRAMs that share the same ALERT pin, wait for all previous commands to be fully executed, and then issue PREab, REFab,and SRE commands to put the devices in a safe state after ALERT is detected and before (tCMD_BLOCKING × 80%)elapses.
D6-P435-0005 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] In blocking mode only, if a CA parity error is detected and the CA Parity Recovery Mode mode register bit is 1b, then the DRAM shall copy the VREFCA shadow mode register value to the VREFCA normal mode register before tCMD_BLOCKING elapses.
D6-P435-0006 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] In blocking mode only, after tCMD_BLOCKING, the DRAM shall automatically stop blocking commands from the host.
D6-P435-0007 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] In blocking mode only, the host shall stop sending commands before (tCMD_BLOCKING × 80%) elapses.
D6-P435-0008 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] After (tCMD_BLOCKING × 120%), the DRAM is ready to accept commands for normal operation.
D6-P435-0009 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] Parity latency will be in effect and parity checking will resume immediately.
D6-P435-0010 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] It is possible that the DRAM might have ignored a refresh command during the tCMD_BLOCKING window or the refresh command is the first erroneous frame, so it is recommended that the controller issues extra refresh cycles as needed.
D6-P435-0011 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] The CA Parity Error Status mode register bit may be read any time by the host after(tCMD_BLOCKING× 120%), to determine which DRAM had the error.
D6-P435-0012 §7.9.14.3 [table_cell] The DRAM maintains the CA Parity Error Log C0-C5 mode registers for the first erroneous command until the CA Parity Error Status mode register bit is reset to ‘0’.
D6-P436-0009 §7.9.14.4 [body] DDR6 DRAM commands ACT, RD, REFab, REFdb, REFqb, RFMab, RFMdb, RFMqb, RFMpb, MRR, MRW are 4- and 6-cycle commands without associated ODT control requirements.
D6-P436-0010 §7.9.14.4 [body] The DRAM will not execute these 4- and 6-cycle commands if the CS is HIGH on the last two cycles of the command (command cancel).
D6-P436-0011 §7.9.14.4 [body] For example, if the RCD or IMB detects a parity error on the 1st and/or 2nd cycles of a 2-, 4-, or 6-cycle command using the PAR1 bit, the CS will remain LOW for the duration of the 2, 4, or 6 cycles, respectively.
D6-P436-0012 §7.9.14.4 [body] For example, if the RCD or IMB detects a parity error on the 3rd and/or 4th cycles of a 4-cycle command using the PAR2 bit, the CS will remain HIGH for the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, and 4th cycles of the command.
D6-P436-0013 §7.9.14.4 [body] If the command is a Write, then it will be converted to non-target termination command in the DRAM.
D6-P436-0014 §7.9.14.4 [body] If the command is either RD, REFab, REFdb, REFqb, RFMab, RFMdb, RFMqb, RFMpb, or MRR, then the erroneous command will be canceled in the DRAM.
D6-P436-0015 §7.9.14.4 [body] For example, if the RCD or IMB detects a parity error on the 3rd, 4th, 5th, and/or 6th cycles of a 6-cycle command using the PAR2 bit, the CS will remain HIGH for the 1st and 2nd cycles, LOW for the 3rd, and 4th cycles, and HIGH for 5th, and 6th cycles of the command.
D6-P436-0016 §7.9.14.4 [body] If the command is either ACT or MRW, then the erroneous command will be canceled in the DRAM.
D6-P436-0017 §7.9.14.4 [body] Command cancel is not intended by the host rather it is a result of a CA parity error detected by the RCD or IMB.
D6-P436-0018 §7.9.14.4 [body] So the relationship between the canceled command and the next valid command shall not be illegal.
D6-P437-0001 §7.9.14.4 [body] If CA parity checking is enabled, the check also applies to cancelled commands.
D6-P437-0002 §7.9.14.4 [body] For example, if the RCD or IMB detects a parity error using the PAR2 bit, the RCD or IMB may recalculate the PAR2 bit, so the DRAM should still expect the PAR2 check to pass.
D6-P437-0003 §7.9.14.4 [body] After a command is cancelled, the CS will remain LOW for a period of time.
D6-P437-0004 §7.9.14.4 [body] The last cycle of the next valid command must be at least tCMD_cancel after the last cycle of the cancelled command.
D6-P438-0001 §7.9.14.5 [table_cell] 0B – Clear 1B – Error
D6-P438-0002 §7.9.14.5 [table_cell] 0B – Do not use the VREFCA shadow register (Default) 1B – Copy VREFCA shadow mode register value to the VREFCA normal mode register when a CA Parity Error is detected in blocking mode.
D6-P439-0001 §7.9.14.5 [table_cell] NOTE 1: Parity Latency is applied to all commands.
D6-P439-0002 §7.9.14.5 [table_cell] NOTE 1: Parity Latency can be changed only when CA Parity Checking is disabled.
D6-P439-0004 §7.9.14.5 [table_cell] NOTE 3: CA Parity Error Log C0-C5 only stores the first erroneous command, until CA Parity Error Status is cleared.
D6-P439-0007 §7.9.14.6 [table_cell] NOTE 1: tPAR_CA_DELAY is applied to all commands.
D6-P439-0008 §7.9.14.6 [table_cell] NOTE 2: If CA parity check is enabled and CA parity command blocking mode is enabled, then tPAR_- CA_DELAY = PL; else tPAR_CA_DELAY = 0.
D6-P439-0010 §7.9.14.6 [table_cell] Command cancel timing for ACT, RD, REFab, REFdb, REFqb, RFMab, RFMdb, RFMqb, RFMpb, MRR, MRW when CS is high on the last two cycles
D6-P440-0001 §7.10 [body] The host can dynamically choose which Activate, Read, Write, and/or Precharge commands to ignore or execute for selected DRAM die(s) by configuring mode register bits and by setting an Ignore bit in C0:CA4 in the command to low (active low) or high (active high).
D6-P440-0002 §7.10 [body] Ignored commands (including Write-NTODT) shall be treated like NOP commands.
D6-P440-0003 §7.10 [body] Write-NTODT commands will also be treated like NOP commands.CA parity checks, when enabled, will also apply to ignored commands.
D6-P440-0004 §7.10 [body] When CA parity checking is enabled, responses to CA parity errors will be the same whether a command is ignored or not.
D6-P440-0005 §7.10 [body] The Command Ignore function is only supported by 2p3 DRAM devices.
D6-P440-0006 §7.10 [body] All command timing constraints need to be satisfied as though the command is being executed, even if the command is ignored.
D6-P440-0007 §7.10 [body] If the current or next command is ignored, assume different bank groups when applying command timing constraints.
D6-P440-0008 §7.10 [body] The Command Ignore Enable/Disable bit, MR0EOP[0], may be set to 0b (active low) or 1b (active high) to dynamically ignore Activate, Read, Write, or Precharge commands for a DRAM die when the Ignore bit in the Activate, Read, Write, or Precharge command is low (active low) or high (active high) as defined in the Command Ignore Polarity bit, MR0EOP[1].
D6-P440-0009 §7.10 [body] When the Command Ignore function is disabled (i.e., MR0EOP[0] = 0b), then the Ignore bit in C0:CA4 becomes don’t care.
D6-P440-0010 §7.10 [body] When the Command Ignore function is enabled (i.e., MR0E OP[0] = 1b) and the Command Ignore Polarity is ‘low’ (i.e., MR0EOP[1] = 0b) then the DRAM die shall dynamically ignore Activate, Read, Write, or Precharge commands when the Ignore bit in C0:CA4 is low.
D6-P440-0011 §7.10 [body] When the Command Ignore function is enabled (i.e., MR0EOP[0] = 1b) and the Command Ignore Polarity is ‘high’ (i.e., MR0EOP[1] = 1b) then the DRAM die shall dynamically ignore Activate, Read, Write, or Precharge commands when the Ignore bit in C0:CA4 is high.
D6-P440-0012 §7.10 [body] A Mode Register Write command with Per-DRAM Addressability (PDA) may be used to set the Command Ignore Enable/Disable bit or the Command Ignore Polarity bit.
D6-P441-0001 §7.10.1 [table_cell] 0B – The Command Ignore function is disabled.
D6-P441-0002 §7.10.1 [table_cell] The Ignore bit in C0:CA4 doesn’t take any effect.
D6-P441-0003 §7.10.1 [table_cell] (Default) 1B – The Command Ignore function is enabled.
D6-P441-0004 §7.10.1 [table_cell] The DRAM die will dynamically ignore Activate, Read, Write, or Precharge commands depending on the Ignore bit in C0:CA4 and the ‘Command Ignore Polarity’ as set in the MR0E OP[1].
D6-P441-0005 §7.10.1 [table_cell] This bit is only supported by 2p3 DRAM devices.
D6-P441-0006 §7.10.1 [table_cell] This bit is a don’t care when the Command Ignore function is disabled.
D6-P441-0007 §7.10.1 [table_cell] 0B – The Ignore bit in C0:CA4 is active low.
D6-P441-0008 §7.10.1 [table_cell] 1B – The Ignore bit in C0:CA4 is active high.
D6-P441-0009 §7.10.1 [table_cell] (Default) This bit is only supported by 2p3 DRAM devices.

D6-P466-0002 §11.1 [body] This section provides an overview of the bit error rate (BER) and the desired statistical level of confidence.
D6-P466-0011 §11.1 [body] If no errors are assumed in a given test period, the second term drops out and the equation becomes:
D6-P466-0014 §11.1 [body] JEDEC recommends testing to 99.5% confidence levels; however, one may choose a number that is viable for their own manufacturing levels.
D6-P466-0015 §11.1 [body] To determine how many bits of data should be sent (again, assuming zero errors, or N = 0), using BER = 1E-9and confidence level SLC = 99.5%, the result is n = (1/BER)(-ln(1 - 0.995) = 5.298 x 109.

D6-P468-0001 §11.2.1 [body] This section describes the Unit Interval (UI) and UI Jitter definitions associated with the Jitter parameters specified in Tx DQS2DQ Stressed Edge Jitter and Input Clock Jitter specifications.
D6-P468-0002 §11.2.2 [body] The definition of Unit Interval (UI) is a minimum time interval between condition changes of a signal.
D6-P468-0003 §11.2.2 [body] DDR-based signals are referenced to the differential crossing point of CK_t and CK_c.
D6-P468-0004 §11.2.2 [body] 2UI=1tCK for DDR-based signals (for example, DQ, DQS).
D6-P468-0005 §11.2.2 [body] The UI definitions shown in Figure 1and Figure 2 are for DDR-based signals.
D6-P468-0006 §11.2.2 [body] The times at which the differential crossing points of the clock occur are defined at t1, t2, …, tn-1, tn.
D6-P468-0007 §11.2.2 [body] The UI at index "n" is defined as an arbitrary time in steady state, where n=0 is chosen as the starting crossing point.
D6-P468-0009 §11.2.2 [body] UI T0 T1 T2 Tn-1 Tn ...
D6-P469-0001 §11.2.3 [body] If a number of UI edges are computed or measured at times t1, t2, …, tn-1, tn,..., tK, where K is the maximum number of samples, then the UI jitter at any instance "n" is defined in Figure3, where T = the ideal UI size.
D6-P469-0003 §11.2.3 [body] In a large sample with random Gaussian-like jitter (therefore very close to symmetric distribution), the average of all UI sizes usually turns out to be very close to the ideal UI size.
D6-P469-0004 §11.2.3 [body] The equation described in Figure 3 assumes starting from an instant steady state, where n=0 is chosen as the starting point.1 UI = one bit, which means 2 UI = one full cycle or time period of the forwarded strobe.
D6-P469-0005 §11.2.3 [body] Example: For 6.4 GT/s signaling, the forwarded strobe frequency is 3.2 GHz, or 1 UI = 156.25 ps.
D6-P469-0006 §11.2.3 [body] Deterministic jitter is analyzed in terms of the peak-to-peak value and in terms of specific frequency components present in the jitter, isolating the causes for each frequency.
D6-P469-0007 §11.2.3 [body] Random jitter is unbounded and analyzed in terms of statistical distribution to convert to a bit error rate (BER) for the link.
D6-P469-0008 §11.2.4 [body] UI-UI (read as "UI to UI") jitter is defined to be the jitter between two consecutive UI as shown in Figure 4.
D6-P470-0001 §11.2.5 [body] Accumulated jitter is defined as the jitter accumulated over any consecutive "N" UI as shown in Figure 5 .
D6-P470-0006 §11.2.5 [body] where UI is defined in the equation shown in Figure 6.
D6-P471-0013 §11.3.1 [table_cell] NOTE 1: The VIX_CK voltage is referenced to VCKmid(mean) = (CK_t voltage + CK_c voltage) /2, where the mean is over 8 UI.
D6-P471-0015 §11.3.1 [table_cell] NOTE 3: Only applies when both CK_t and CK_c are transitioning

D6-P474-0002 §12.1 [body] The command, address and control (including chip select) input receiver compliance mask for voltage and timing is shown in the figure below.
D6-P474-0003 §12.1 [body] All CA signals apply the same compliance mask and the CS signal applies its own compliance mask independently.
D6-P474-0004 §12.1 [body] All signals applied to the compliance mask operate in single data rate mode.
D6-P474-0005 §12.1 [body] The CA input receiver mask (Rx Mask) for voltage and timing is applied across all CA[13:0] pins and the CS_n pin.
D6-P474-0006 §12.1 [body] For the remainder of this section, the reference to "CA" should be applied to the device CA[13:0] pins as well as the CS_n pin.
D6-P474-0007 §12.1 [body] The Rx Mask defines the area the input signal must not encroach for the device input receiver to successfully capture a valid input signal; it is not the valid data-eye.
D6-P474-0014 §12.1 [body] Vcent_CA(pin mid) is defined as the midpoint between the largest Vcent_CA voltage level and the smallest Vcent_CA voltage level across all CA and CS_n pins for a given device component.
D6-P474-0015 §12.1 [body] Each CA Vcent level is defined by the center (widest opening) of the cumulative data input eye as depicted in the previous figure (Across-Pin VREFCA Voltage Variation).
D6-P474-0016 §12.1 [body] This clarifies any device component level variation must be
D6-P475-0001 §12.1 [body] accounted for within the CA Rx Mask.

D6-P482-0002 §12.4 [body] The clock is being driven to the device either by the RCD for L/RDIMM modules, or by the host for U/SODIMM modules.
D6-P482-0006 §12.4.1 [body] The Random Jitter (Rj) specified is a random jitter meeting a Gaussian distribution.
D6-P482-0007 §12.4.1 [body] The Deterministic Jitter (Dj) specified is bounded.
D6-P482-0008 §12.4.1 [body] Input clock violating the min/max jitter values may result in malfunction of the device.
D6-P482-0010 §12.4.1 [body] NOTE: f0 = Data Rate/2, example: if data rate is 3200MT/s, then f0 = 1600
D6-P482-0011 §12.4.1 [body] NOTE: Rise and fall time slopes (V/ns) are measured between +100 mV and -100 mV of the differential output of reference clock
D6-P482-0012 §12.4.1 [body] NOTE: On-die noise is similar to that occurring with all the transmitter and receiver lanes toggling need to be stimulated.
D6-P482-0013 §12.4.1 [body] When there is no socket in transmitter measurement setup, in many cases, the contribution of the cross-talk is not significant or can be estimated within tolerable error even with all the transmitter lanes sending patterns.
D6-P482-0014 §12.4.1 [body] When a socket is present, such as DUT being a device component, the contribution of the cross-talk could be significant.
D6-P482-0015 §12.4.1 [body] To minimize the impact of crosstalk on the measurement results, a small group of selected lanes in the vicinity of the lane under test may be turned off (sending DC), while the remaining Tx lanes send patterns to the corresponding Rx receivers so as to excite realistic on-die noise profile from device switching.
D6-P482-0016 §12.4.1 [body] Note that there may be cases when one of Dj and Rj specifications is met and another violated in which case the signaling analysis should be run to determine link feasibility.
D6-P482-0017 §12.4.1 [body] NOTE: Duty Cycle Error defined as absolute difference between average value of all UI with that of average of odd UI, which in magnitude would equal absolute difference between average of all UI and average of all even UI.

D6-P495-0001 §12.7.2 [body] The following table lists the amount of duty cycle distortion (DCD) and/or random jitter (Rj) that must be applied to the forwarded strobe when measuring the Rx strobe jitter sensitivity parameters specified in the previous tables.
D6-P495-0002 §12.7.2 [body] NOTE: While imposing these specifications, the strobe lane is stressed, but the data input is kept large amplitude and no jitter or ISI injection.
D6-P495-0003 §12.7.2 [body] The specified voltages are at the Rx input pin.
D6-P495-0004 §12.7.2 [body] The DQS and DQ input voltage swing and/or slew rate can be adjusted, without exceeding the specifications, for this test.
D6-P495-0005 §12.7.2 [body] NOTE: The jitter response of the forwarded strobe channel depends on the input voltage, primarily due to bandwidth limitations of the clock receiver.
D6-P495-0006 §12.7.2 [body] For this revision, no separate specification of jitter as a function of input amplitude is specified; instead, the response characterization is done at the specified clock amplitude only.
D6-P495-0007 §12.7.2 [body] The specified voltages are at the Rx input pin.
D6-P495-0008 §12.7.2 [body] NOTE: Various DCD values should be tested, complying within the maximum limits.
D6-P495-0009 §12.7.2 [body] NOTE: Various Rj values should be tested, complying within the maximum limits.
D6-P495-0010 §12.7.2 [body] NOTE: Various combinations of DCD and Rj values should be tested, complying within the maximum limits.
D6-P495-0011 §12.7.2 [body] The maximum timing margin degradation as a result of the injected jitter is specified in a separate table.
D6-P495-0012 §12.7.2 [body] NOTE: Although the device has bursty traffic, current available BERTs that can be used for this test do not support burst traffic patterns.
D6-P495-0013 §12.7.2 [body] A continuous strobe and continuous DQ are used for this parameter.
D6-P495-0014 §12.7.2 [body] The clock-like pattern repeating three 1s and three 0s is used for this test.
D6-P495-0015 §12.7.2 [body] NOTE: Duty cycle distortion (in UI DCD) as applied to the input forwarded DQS from BERT (UI).
D6-P495-0016 §12.7.2 [body] NOTE: RMS value of Rj (specified as edge jitter) applied to the input forwarded DQS from BERT (values of the edge jitter RMS values specified as % of UI).
D6-P495-0017 §12.7.2 [body] NOTE: Duty cycle distortion (specified as UI DCD) and rms values of Rj (specified as edge jitter) applied to the input forwarded DQS from BERT (values of the edge jitter RMS values specified as % of UI).
D6-P495-0018 §12.7.2 [body] NOTE: Voltage swing and slew rates can be set for strobe and DQ signals as long as they meet the specification.
D6-P495-0019 §12.7.2 [body] The DQS and DQ input voltage swing and/or slew rate can be adjusted, without exceeding the specifications, for this test.

D6-P510-0001 §13.4.1 [body] Tx DQS-to-DQ Stress Edge provides the methodology for testing the DRAM transmitter’s timing characteristics under various DQS to DQ skew conditions.
D6-P510-0002 §13.4.1 [body] This test measures how each DQ edge aligns to its associated strobe when stressed and reports Random Jitter (Rj), Deterministic Jitter (Dj), and Total Jitter (Tj).
D6-P510-0003 §13.4.1 [body] The applied DQS-to-DQ skew conditions are determined by the Host’s responsibility and capability to adjust advanced DQS edges through the Read DQS Offset Timing (mode TBD).
D6-P510-0004 §13.4.1 [body] The total Tx DQS_to_DQ Stressed Edge jitter, Tj, is defined as Dj+Q*Rj, where Q isTBD 14.07 calculated at BER of 1E-12.
D6-P510-0005 §13.4.1 [body] The Deterministic Jitter (Dj) as specified is bounded.
D6-P510-0006 §13.4.1 [body] The Random Jitter (Rj) as specified meets a Gaussian distribution.

D6-P516-0003 §13.5.1 [body] • Minimum BER E-9 and Confidence Level of 99.5% per pin.
D6-P516-0004 §13.5.1 [body] • Refer to the minimum Bit Error Rate (BER) requirements for DDR5.
D6-P516-0005 §13.5.1 [body] • The validation methodology for these parameters will be covered in the future.
D6-P516-0006 §13.5.1 [body] • Mismatch is defined as DQS to DQ mismatch in UI increments.
D6-P516-0007 §13.5.1 [body] • The DQ pins' duty cycle must be adjusted as close to 50% as possible using the Duty Cycle Adjuster feature prior to running the Tx DQS jtter test.
D6-P516-0008 §13.5.1 [body] • The mode registers for the Duty Cycle Adjuster are MR43 and MR44.
D6-P516-0009 §13.5.1 [body] The mode registers for the per-pin DCA of DQS are MR103-MR110.
D6-P516-0010 §13.5.1 [body] The mode registers for the per-pin DCA of DQLx are MR(133+8x) and MR(134+8x), where 0 ≤x ≤7.
D6-P516-0011 §13.5.1 [body] The mode registers for the per-pin DCA of DQUy are MR(197+8y) and MR(198+8y), where 0 ≤y ≤7.
D6-P516-0012 §13.5.1 [body] • Spread spectrum clocking (SSC) must be disabled while running these tests.
D6-P516-0013 §13.5.1 [body] • These parameters are tested using the continuous PRBS8 LFSR training pattern, which is sent out on all DQ lanes off the device without the need for sending out continuous MRR commands.
D6-P516-0014 §13.5.1 [body] The MR25 OP[3] is set to 1 to enable this feature.
D6-P516-0015 §13.5.1 [body] • These parameters are tested on the CTC2 card only.
D6-P516-0016 §13.5.1 [body] • Matched DQS to DQ would require the DQs to be adjusted by 0.5UI to place it in the center of the DQ eye.
D6-P516-0017 §13.5.1 [body] 1UI mismatch would require the DQS to be adjusted 1.5UI.
D6-P516-0018 §13.5.1 [body] Generally, for XUI mismatch the DQ must be adjusted XUI + 0.5UI to be placed in the center of the eye.
D6-P516-0019 §13.5.1 [body] NOTE: The number of UI’s accumulated will depend on the speed of the link.
D6-P516-0020 §13.5.1 [body] For higher speeds, higher UI accumulation may be specified.
D6-P516-0021 §13.5.1 [body] For lower speeds, N=4,5 UI may not be applicable.

D6-P521-0002 §16.1 [body] The figure below represents the effective reference load of 50 ohms used in defining the relevant AC timing parameters of the device as well as output slew rate measurements.
D6-P521-0003 §16.1 [body] RON nominal of DQ, DQS_t, and DQS_c drivers uses 34 ohms to specify the relevant AC timing parameter values of the device.
D6-P521-0004 §16.1 [body] • The maximum DC high level of output signal = 1.0 × VDDQ.
D6-P521-0006 §16.1 [body] • The nominal reference level of an output signal can be approximated by the following: – The center of maximum DC high and minimum DC low
D6-P521-0009 §16.1 [body] The actual reference level of output signal might vary with driver RON and reference load tolerances.
D6-P521-0010 §16.1 [body] Thus, the actual reference level or midpoint of an output signal is at the widest part of the output signal’s eye.
D6-P521-0011 §16.1 [body] Prior to measuring AC parameters, the reference level of the verification tool should be set to an appropriate level.
D6-P521-0012 §16.1 [body] It is not intended as a precise representation of any particular system environment or a depiction of the actual load presented by a production tester.
D6-P521-0013 §16.1 [body] System designers should use IBIS or other simulation tools to correlate the timing reference load to a system environment.
D6-P521-0014 §16.1 [body] Manufacturers correlate to their production test conditions, generally one or more coaxial transmission lines terminated at the tester electronics.
